GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 50.01 TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the Health and Sanitary
Ordinance of Bonneville County.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
§ 50.02 AUTHORITY.
   The County Board of Health, under and pursuant to the powers vested in it by
Idaho Code § 39-4401, and in order to carry out the work effectively and to
perform properly the duties intended and required by said statute, does hereby
make and place into effect rules and regulations deemed necessary and proper to
prevent the outbreak and spread of dangerous, contagious and infectious
disease, in the county, said rules and regulations to be known collectively as
the Sanitary Ordinance of Bonneville County.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
§ 50.03 PURPOSE.
   (A)   This chapter is based on the premises that controlling and changing
the physical environment is necessary to protect and improve the public health.
Certain types of illness may be prevalent where living conditions place
dependence on common water supplies and on others for food and drink, where
vermin abound, where disposal of body wastes endanger others and where filth in
general abounds.
   (B)   Such conditions perpetuate diseases and may set into action a series
of events that result in epidemics.
   (C)   Methods have been developed to deal competently with conditions
promoting the transmission of disease.
   (D)   It is hereby declared to be the duty of the Board of Health to deal
with the forces and processes of the environment and to influence or control
them in the interest of health, individual and community alike, to prevent the
outbreak and the spread of dangerous, contagious and infectious disease.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
§ 50.04 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning. Words in
the present tense include the future and the future includes the present; the
singular number includes the plural and the plural the singular; the words
SHALL and MUST are always mandatory.
   BOARD OF HEALTH. The Board of Health of Bonneville County.
   COLLECTOR. The county or its agent or person holding a license or contract
who is authorized or designated to collect, transport and dispose of refuse.
   DOMESTIC SEWAGE. Sewage derived principally from dwellings, business
buildings and institutions and may or may not contain groundwater, surface
water or stormwater.
   GARBAGE. All animal and vegetable wastes resulting from the handling,
storage, preparation, cooking or consumption of food.
   HEALTH OFFICER. The County Health Officer appointed by the Board of Health
or his or her authorized representative.
   INDUSTRIAL SEWAGE. Sewage in which industrial wastes predominate.
   INDUSTRIAL WASTE. The liquid wastes from industrial processes as distinct
from domestic or sanitary sewage.
   INDUSTRIAL WASTE TREATMENT. Any artificial process to which industrial waste
is subjected in order to remove or alter its objectionable constituents and
thus render it less offensive or dangerous, and approved by the State Board of
Health.
   INSPECTOR. The Health Officer or other duly authorized officer of the county
having the duty of enforcing this regulation.
   MARINE TOILET. Any toilet on or within any watercraft.
   OWNER and OCCUPANT. Every person in possession, charge or in control of any
dwelling, flat, rooming house, apartment house, hospital, school, hotel, motel,
boarding house, eating establishment, place of business or manufacturing plant
where refuse is created or accumulated, and such terms may be used
interchangeably.
   PERSONS. Any natural person, association, partnership, firm, corporation or
institution.
   PRIVATE WATER SUPPLY. Any individual supply serving only one residence and
includes the source and pumping equipment with appurtenances.
   PUBLIC WATER SUPPLY. Any supply which serves two or more residences or
serves any other type of establishment which provides drinking water and
includes the source, pumping equipment and appurtenances and distribution
system.
   REFUSE. Solid wastes, including, but not limited to garbage and rubbish. It
shall not include human body wastes.
   RUBBISH. Solid wastes other than garbage, dead animals, abandoned vehicles,
industrial wastes, sewage treatment residue and extremely hazardous substances.
   SANITARY ORDINANCE. The entire series of rules now and hereafter adopted by
the Board of Health, which govern the environment of the area under
jurisdiction of the Board, and specifically this chapter.
   SANITARY SEWAGE. Domestic sewage with stormwater and surface water included;
sewage discharged from sanitary conveniences of dwellings, (including apartment
houses and hotels), office buildings, factories and institutions; or the water
supply of a community after it has been used and discharged into a sewer.
   SEWAGE. The liquid or water carried wastes from residences, business
buildings and institutions, together with the sanitary sewage from industrial
establishments, and with such groundwater, surface water and stormwater as may
be present, but for the purpose of these regulations shall not include
industrial wastes.
   SEWAGE TREATMENT. Any artificial process to which sewage is subjected in
order to remove or alter its objectionable constituent and thus render it less
offensive or dangerous, and approved by the State Department of Health.
   SEWER. A pipe or conduit, generally closed, but normally not flowing full,
for carrying sewage or other waste liquids.
   SEWERAGE SYSTEM. A system of sewers and appurtenances for the collection,
transportation and pumping of sewage and industrial wastes.
   WASTE. Unwanted solid, liquid or gaseous materials.
   WATERCRAFT. Any contrivance used or designed for navigation on water,
specifically including houseboats.
   WATERS OF THIS COUNTY. All of the waters on which watercraft shall be used
or operated.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
SEWAGE
§ 50.15 PROHIBITED CONDITIONS.
   (A)   Sewage, sewage effluent or human excretes shall not be allowed to
remain open to the atmosphere, or on the surface of the ground so that it is or
is likely to be:
      (1)   A cause of flies or fly breeding or mosquito breeding or a breeding
for other vermin; and
      (2)   A source of noxious or offensive odors or conditions inimical to
public interest, dangerous or prejudicial to health or a public nuisance.
   (B)   Sewage, sewage effluent or human excreta shall not be allowed to
endanger any source or supply of drinking water, or cause damage to any public
or private property.
   (C)   Raw or untreated sewage shall not be allowed in any body of water,
watercourse or any underground water drain, any stormwater drain, channel or
other surface water drain, except if subjected to satisfactory sewage
treatment.
   (D)   No raw or untreated sewage shall be permitted to irrigate or flow onto
any ground so that edible portions of vegetable, fruit, root crops or other
edible portions or other growing food plants may come in contact with the
sewage or with such soil; or onto any ground used or intended to be used for
the pasture or enclosure of animals producing milk likely to be used for human
consumption so that the sewage or soil containing such sewage will come in
contact with such animals, except if subject to satisfactory sewage treatment.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.16 STANDARD OF MAINTENANCE.
   (A)   Corrections and alterations to existing sewerage systems or sewage
treatment processes, or new sewerage systems may be required, when in the
opinion of the Health Officer, there is a violation of §
50.15.
   (B)   Privies shall be maintained in such condition so as not to be in
violation of §
50.15.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to construct,
alter or extend individual sewage disposal systems within the county unless
such person, firm or corporation holds a valid permit issued by the Health
Officer in the name of such person, firm or corporation for the specific
construction, alteration or extension proposed. Applications for a permit shall
be made to the Health Officer. It shall be the duty of the Health Officer to
issue a permit after satisfactory evidence of intent is presented, which shall
consist of:
      (1)   The applicant’s name and address;
      (2)   Lot and block number and/or street address of property on which
construction, alteration or extension is proposed; and
      (3)   Complete plan of proposed disposal facility, including plot plan,
construction, alteration or extension is proposed.
   (D)   The Health Officer may refuse to grant a permit for construction of
any individual sewage disposal system where approved public or community
sewerage systems are available. All individual sewage disposal systems shall be
designed and constructed in accordance with standards as stated in Bulletin #6,
“Standards for Subsurface Sewage Disposal Systems,” State Department of Health.
The Health Officer shall make an inspection during the construction prior to
the stage of final earth covering to determine compliance with the plans and
standards. It shall be unlawful to cover or use the individual sewage disposal
system without written approval of the Health Officer. This division (D) shall
be applicable to all residential and commercial individual sewage disposal
installations, except that it shall not apply to rural farm installations on
tracts of five acres or more provided rural farm installations shall meet the
requirements of §
50.15.
   (E)   It shall be the responsibility of the owner of a proposed subdivision
to submit the provisions and/or plans for the domestic water and sewerage
facilities to the Health Officer who shall review and approve the provisions
and/or plans prior to the filing of the plat with the County Recorder.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.17 DISPOSAL OF INDUSTRIAL SEWAGE AND WASTE.
   (A)   Said application for a permit to construct sanitary disposal, permits
for industrial sewage and waste facilities, shall be accompanied by a sketch
indicating the approximate layout of the building and approximate dimensions of
the land ownership to be used with said building or buildings.
   (B)   All proposed industrial sewage and waste facilities shall comply with
the standards issued by the State Department of Health and with applicable
regulations hereafter approved. It is also necessary that plans and
specifications for industrial sewage and waste facilities be submitted to the
State Department of Health for approval prior to construction. Maintenance and
operation of such facilities are also subject to the approval of the State
Department of Health.
   (C)   No permit shall be issued until sanitary disposal facilities for
industrial sewage and waste have been constructed and inspected by the Health
Officer. Said sanitary disposal facilities shall not be covered with dirt or
otherwise completed or used until inspected and approved.
   (D)   (1)   The Health Officer shall inspect any industrial sewage or waste
facility heretofore constructed where he or she has reason to believe sewage
treatment or waste treatment may not conform to the requirements of this code.
      (2)   If any industrial sewage or waste disposal system heretofore
constructed or in operation shall be found to be causing contamination or
endangering any source or supply of drinking water or causing damage to any
public or private property, the Health Officer shall notify in writing the
owner or occupant of said property that said industrial sewage or waste
disposal facility is a nuisance which must be abated within 30 days from the
date of such notice by altering, modifying or rebuilding said facility to
conform to the standards of the State Department of Health, as aforesaid.
      (3)   If said owner or occupant shall neglect or refuse to comply with
said notice, the Health Officer shall invoke the penalties of Idaho Code § 39-
4414 and shall further ask the State Board of Health to act under the
provisions of Idaho Code § 39-4401 and related provisions.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.18 REMOVAL AND TRANSPORTATION OF DISPOSAL OF SEWAGE, SEWAGE SLUDGE OR
HUMAN EXCREMENT.
   (A)   Any tank wagon, tank truck, can or other container or equipment used
or intended to be used for the purpose of removal and transportation or
disposal of sewage, sewage sludges or human excrement shall conform to the
following requirements:
      (1)   The tank or container shall be watertight and so constructed as to
prevent spillage or leakage while being loaded or transported; and
      (2)   Tanks and containers shall be so constructed that every portion of
the interior and exterior can be easily cleaned and kept in a clean condition
at all times and while not in actual use.
   (B)   The material shall be disposed of in one or more of the following
ways:
      (1)   Discharge to a manhole on a public sewer;
      (2)   Discharge to a sewage treatment plant;
       (3)   Burial under two feet of earth in a location and in a manner not
conflicting with §
50.15; and
      (4)   Drying in a manner not creating a nuisance and not conflicting with
§
50.15.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.19 SEWAGE DISPOSAL FROM BOATS.
   (A)   Restrictions. No marine toilet on any water craft used in this county
shall be so constructed and operated as to discharge any sewage into said water
directly or indirectly and, further, it shall be unlawful to discharge or
deposit any garbage, refuse or other solids or other liquid waste from any
water craft, onto any waterfront property or into any waters in the county.
   (B)   Dock facilities. Every dock servicing watercraft shall have
conveniently located thereto adequate toilet facilities for men and women, and
every dock shall have covered garbage and waste facilities for the disposal of
garbage and wastes other than sewage.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
REFUSE CONTROL
§ 50.30 REFUSE CONTAINERS.
   (A)   Refuse containers shall be of a type manufactured specifically for the
purpose of refuse storage and shall be made of durable watertight, rust-
resistant material having a close-fitting lid and handles to facilitate
collection and shall be rodent- and fly-proof.
   (B)   Refuse containers shall be of not less than 20 gallons, not more than
32 gallons in capacity, with the exception that standard metal refuse bins
manufactured for this purpose may be used where the volume of refuse produced
per day is greater than the refuse from an average single-family dwelling.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful to permit the accumulation or residue of liquids,
solids or a combination of such material on the bottom or sides of containers.
It is the intention of this provision that the interior of containers shall be
kept clean by thorough washing, rinsing and drainage as often as necessary.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.31 STORAGE OF REFUSE.
   (A)   Each person having refuse shall provide himself or herself with
approved refuse containers and shall place and keep all refuse therein; except
as otherwise provided in this regulation.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful to place refuse in any street, alley or body of
water, irrigation canal, drainage canal or any public place, or upon private
property, unless such refuse is placed in an approved container; except as
otherwise provided in this regulation.
   (C)   No person shall bury, dump, collect, remove or in any other manner
dispose of refuse within the prescribed limits of this regulation, except as
hereby provided.
   (D)   No person shall throw, deposit or permit to accumulate any refuse in
any building or on any premises, improved or vacant or any lot or area, either
public or private, so that it is or is likely:
      (1)   To be a source of flies or fly breeding or mosquito breeding;
      (2)   To afford food or harborage for rodents or other vermin; or
      (3)   To be a source of noxious or offensive odors or conditions inimical
to public interest, dangerous or prejudicial to health, or an unhealthy
nuisance.
   (E)   Under no circumstance shall any material causing excessive smoke,
liberating a toxic substance on combustion, or producing a noxious odor likely
to affect the occupant of any premises, be burned except under conditions and
in equipment approved by the Health Officer.
   (F)   No person shall deny the inspector the right of ingress or egress to
any premises for the purpose of inspecting all places and containers where
refuse is created or kept.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.32 COLLECTION OF REFUSE.
   (A)   Where refuse collection service is provided by a governmental agency
or by contract to a government agency, all persons, hotels, restaurants,
institutions or community establishments within the geographical boundaries or
areas of any municipality and incorporated villages or subdivisions shall
subscribe to the refuse collection service as provided.
    (B)   Collectors shall collect refuse for disposal from each premises at
least once a week. Hotels, restaurants, institutions and commercial
establishments may be required to have more frequent collection, if determined
by the Health Officer, to be essential to protect the public health. Persons
not retaining or receiving the collection service shall be bound by the
provisions of this division (B) as it pertains to the frequency of disposal.
   (C)   All refuse containers shall be kept in a place accessible to the
collector, except that in isolated cases where reasonable access cannot be had
by the collection vehicle, the container may be kept in such places as may be
agreed upon by the owner and collector; or at such place as may be designated
by the inspector.
   (D)   Collectors shall follow the traveled portion of streets, alleys and
roadways or the regular walks for pedestrians while on private property and
shall take care in loading such refuse so that none of the material to be
collected is left upon the private property or the streets, alleys or roadways.
The collector shall return the receptacle with all contents removed and replace
the lid thereon.
   (E)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to engage in
the business of collecting, transporting, hauling or conveying any refuse over
the streets or alleys of the county or its subdivisions unless such person is
licensed for this business. This regulation does not prohibit a person from
hauling or transporting refuse from the premises owned or occupied by said
person to one of the official disposal sites.
   (F)   All collectors shall furnish vehicles designed for the collection and
hauling of refuse. The collectors shall keep such vehicles clean, sanitary,
neatly painted and in good running order and shall wash, clean and disinfect
all such vehicles at least once a week. Each vehicle shall be equipped with a
tight metal body or, if a wood body, shall be metal-lined so as to be
watertight, and each vehicle shall at all times be provided with a suitable
cover of an approved type that may be securely fastened on all sides when
required to retain dust and prevent scattering of refuse. Any person hauling
refuse shall keep such vehicle covered with a canvas tarp, or like material, to
prevent the scattering or loss of such refuse along or upon public roads or
properties adjacent to such public roads.
   (G)   The inspector shall at least yearly inspect all vehicles designed and
used for collection or transportation of refuse and shall certify his or her
approval or disapproval as to compliance with the provisions of this
regulation. No private franchise or contract collector shall haul, collect,
transport or dispose of refuse until the refuse collection vehicle to be used
has been approved by the inspector and a license obtained from the County
Health Authority.
   (H)   The license for each vehicle for private, franchise or contract
collectors shall be issued for the calendar year, or such portion thereof as
shall remain after the issuance of the license.
   (I)   The license of a collector may be revoked or renewal denied by the
County Health Authority for violation of these regulations.
   (J)   Dead animals, hides, viscera and parts of carcasses shall be removed
to a rendering plant or other designated or authorized disposal site or buried
under two feet of compacted soil and at least 100 feet from a domestic water
source on the premises of the owner of the animal within 24 hours from the
known time of death of the animal; except that small dead animals or parts of
animal carcasses may be tightly wrapped or packaged in at least five layers of
newspaper or equivalent for disposal in an approved refuse container for
storage until collection where weekly collection is provided.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.33 DISPOSAL OF REFUSE.
   (A)   All persons hauling or transporting refuse for disposal shall dispose
of the refuse at the disposal site or sites designated or authorized by the
County Commissioners.
    (B)   (1)   All persons hauling or transporting refuse to an authorized
disposal site shall deposit the refuse in accordance with directions of the
person in charge of the site.
      (2)   The inspector is hereby authorized, subject to the approval of the
Health Officer, to make rules and regulations designating the manner of and the
hours and limits of time of depositing refuse, providing that before such rules
and regulations become effective, appropriate signs shall be placed at or upon
the premises of the disposal site giving notice of such rules and regulations
and enforceable under the penalty clause of this chapter.
(Ord. passed - -1968) Penalty, see §
50.99
WATER SUPPLIES
§ 50.45 PUBLIC SUPPLIES.
   All public water supplies shall be designed, constructed, operated and
maintained in accordance with the State Department of Health Water standards
and regulations.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
§ 50.46 PRIVATE SUPPLIES.
   (A)   Prior to the development of any new source for a private water supply,
a permit shall be obtained from the County Health Authority.
   (B)   A permit application shall be submitted to the County Health Authority
on forms provided for this purpose and shall contain a description of the
proposed source site in relation to possible sources of contamination, such as
ditches, floodwaters, sewage disposal facilities, streams, livestock pastures,
barnyards and the like. Also, if the source is to be a well the application
should list the probable size and minimum depth of the well along with a plan
design of the well head construction.
   (C)   All supplies shall be constructed to meet the minimum design standards
for private supplies as listed in the State Department of Health Bulletin No.
5, as amended.
   (D)   Upon proper notification of completion of the project, the county will
survey the water supply and will issue a certificate of approval upon
satisfactory compliance with this chapter.
   (E)   Abandoned wells shall be properly sealed or capped to meet State
Department of Health requirements.
(Ord. passed - -1968)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person who shall violate any provision of these regulations shall,
upon conviction, be fined not to exceed $50, or be imprisoned for not to exceed
60 days, or be both fined and imprisoned, and every day that such conduct is in
violation of this section is carried on shall constitute a separate offense,
and in addition to such fine and imprisonment shall be liable for any expense
incurred by the County Board of Health in enforcing this act or in removing or
terminating any nuisance, source of filth, cause of sickness or health hazard.
   (B)   Conviction under these penalty provisions or any other health law
shall not relieve any person from any civil action and damages that may exist
for an injury resulting from any violation of the public health laws or rules
and regulations promulgated by the County Board of Health.
(Ord. passed - -1968)

SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SYSTEM
§ 51.01 TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known as the Solid Waste Management Ordinance.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BOARD. The Board of Commissioners of Bonneville County, Idaho.
   COUNTY. The County of Bonneville, a political subdivision of the State of
Idaho.
   DANGEROUS, HAZARDOUS UNAUTHORIZED WASTE MATERIAL. Any substance in any
combination of solid, liquid or gaseous forms which, because of its quantity,
concentration or characteristics, may cause or pose a substantial threat of
injury to human health, or to the environment, or as otherwise defined by
federal, state or local law, and including, but not limited to, hazardous
materials, automobile batteries, white goods, large metal items, tires of any
type and dead animals.
   DEPARTMENT. The State Department of Health and Welfare.
   EPA. The United States Environmental Protection Agency.
   PERSON. Any natural person, firm, corporation or other entity, including
municipalities, a state or federal agency, or a fraternal, educational or
religious institution located within the county.
   SEWAGE SLUDGE. A semi-liquid substance consisting of settled sewage solids
combined with water and dissolved material in varying amounts.
   SITE. Any official solid waste disposal site including any transfer station.
   SOLID WASTE. As defined by present Idaho Code § 31-4902(8), or as
subsequently amended.
   SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SITE. Any landfill or other facility and any land upon
which containers are placed for public disposal of solid waste, as designated
in §
51.05, whether the land is publicly owned or is provided by a contractor
pursuant to an agreement with the county.
   SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SYSTEM. All lands, sites, facilities, equipment and
manpower, owned or approved by the county, necessary or used for the
collection, transportation, storage, treatment, processing, re-use, recycling
or other means necessary for the handling and disposal of solid waste in the
county. The SYSTEM shall include all solid waste disposal system components
approved by the county in existence at the time of and subsequent to the
adoption of this subchapter.
   SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SYSTEM COMPONENT. Any public or privately owned solid
waste disposal site, collection location, disposal facility, transportation
element or other functioning part of the solid waste disposal system for the
county where one or more phases of solid waste disposal is carried out by one
or more persons. A COMPONENT shall not include the solid waste receptacle
provided by a commercial solid waste hauler or generator for use by a single
business, customer or client, unless the receptacle is to be removed from the
site where it regularly rests for transportation to or emptying at another
location.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.03 FINDINGS.
   (A)   Idaho Code Title 31, Chapter 44 requires counties in the state to
provide municipal solid waste disposal as deemed appropriate by the respective
Board of County Commissioners.
   (B)   Idaho Code § 31-4406 requires the Board of County Commissioners for
each county to adopt by ordinance rules and regulations for the operation and
maintenance of solid waste disposal.
   (C)   The Board adopts this subchapter to further the purposes stated in
Idaho Code § 31-4401. Specifically, this subchapter is designed to establish a
solid waste disposal system in the county, to reduce the threat to health,
safety and welfare posed by garbage, refuse and scrap, to maintain the natural
and aesthetic setting of the land, water and air resources of the county and to
develop a coordinated and self-sufficient system to operate a unified solid
waste disposal system.
   (D)   As authorized by Idaho Code § 31-4404, the county will fund the solid
waste disposal system with ad valorem taxes, user fees, current revenues and/or
other sources of monies. The user fees are reasonably related to the benefit
conveyed to the person charged the fee, are reasonable approximations of the
cost of service and will partially support existing and future solid waste
disposal sites.
   (E)   The increased fees established under the Solid Waste User Fee
Ordinance for solid waste from outside the county is justified because county
residents are charged an ad valorem tax and user fee to partially support the
solid waste disposal system in the county while out of county residents are not
charged such an ad valorem tax. Furthermore, any additional fee for solid waste
generated outside of the county is justified because the county qualifies as a
market participant and would not be subject to the restraints of the Interstate
Commerce Clause of the United States Constitution. Any additional fee for solid
waste generated outside of the county as established by the Solid Waste User
Fee Ordinance only applies to the solid waste disposal system, not to other
landfills located in the county.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.04 APPLICABILITY.
   All solid waste generated within the county and all solid waste generated
elsewhere, but proposed or destined for disposal within the county, shall
comply with the standards and procedures set forth herein. All solid waste
shall be managed, whether during storage, collection, transfer, transport,
processing, separation, composting, treatment, re-use, recycling or disposal,
to prevent health hazards, public nuisances or pollution of the environment.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.05 ESTABLISHMENT OF OFFICIAL SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SITES.
   The following are established as the official solid waste disposal sites to
be used by the residents and visitors of the county:
   (A)   County Transfer Station; (Hemert Drive);
   (B)   County Landfill (Peterson Hill); and
   (C)   Other landfills or transfer stations designated by the Board.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.06 ESTABLISHMENT OF A SOLID WASTE FUND.
   (A)   To finance the operation of the solid waste disposal system, there is
created a Solid Waste Fund to be maintained as a separate fund within the
county budget.
   (B)   This fund shall support activities necessary for the continued
operation of the existing and future solid waste disposal system.
   (C)   The Board may establish special accounts within the Solid Waste Fund
to meet anticipated operational needs of the solid waste disposal system as it
deems necessary and proper.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.07 MANAGEMENT OF SOLID WASTE.
   The county reserves the right to authorize recycling of material in any
official solid waste disposal site or any other solid waste disposal site
approved by the county in accordance with standards and conditions set by the
Board. Dangerous, hazardous or unauthorized waste material or improperly
handled waste shall remain in the ownership of the person depositing the
material until they are disposed of in compliance with this subchapter and
other applicable federal, state and county laws, statutes, ordinances, rules
and regulations.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.08 UNAUTHORIZED SALVAGING AT SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SITES PROHIBITED.
   (A)   (1)   Except as otherwise permitted by this subchapter, no person
shall salvage or attempt to salvage materials delivered to or deposited at an
official solid waste disposal site.
      (2)   No party shall interfere or intercede in any way with the lawful
activities of residents of the county, or solid waste disposal system users who
are delivering or depositing solid waste for disposal at an official solid
waste disposal site or with commercial haulers, or county employees who are
removing or handling materials which have been deposited at an official solid
waste disposal site.
   (B)   The county may grant a permit to qualified private contractors to sort
and remove specific material deposited at a gated site. To qualify for such a
permit, a private contractor shall agree with several conditions, including,
but not limited to:
      (1)   Executing an agreement to indemnify the county from any and all
loses and damages;
      (2)   Staying 100 feet away from any operating or non-operating
equipment;
      (3)   Not interfering with any person(s) depositing solid waste;
       (4)   Salvaging only the solid waste specified in the permit;
      (5)   Not handling any hazardous, dangerous or unauthorized waste
material; and
      (6)   Immediately reporting the existence of any hazardous, dangerous or
unauthorized waste material to a county employee at an official solid waste
disposal site.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.09 PERMITS FOR SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SYSTEM COMPONENTS.
   The county may require an operator of any solid waste disposal system
component to obtain a permit from the county. Permits may be granted for
operation of solid waste disposal system components upon compliance with the
standards set forth in this subchapter and with the applicable rules and
regulations for official solid waste disposal sited adopted by the county and
applicable state and federal agencies. Permit applications shall bear full
responsibility for showing compliance with the standards applicable to their
proposed operation of a solid waste disposal system component. Fees may be
charged for permit issuance as established by the Board.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.10 RESPONSIBILITIES OF COMMERCIAL HAULER.
   Any person collecting or hauling solid waste within the county may be
required to report the location of disposal, the number of containers, their
size and frequency of pickup within or outside of the county to official solid
waste disposal sites. These reports shall be filed in a form and on a schedule
acceptable to the county. The county may establish bond requirements or similar
financial guarantees to assure performance of responsibilities established by
this subchapter.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
§ 51.11 LOCATION AND HOURS OF DISPOSAL OF SOLID WASTE.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful, except at an official solid waste disposal site,
for any person to throw away, dump or discard, or allowed to be dumped or
discarded, or aid or assist in throwing away, dumping or discarding, or allow
to be deposited or discarded, any type of solid waste or litter on any public
lands, rights-of-way of any kind or private lands.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to throw away, dump or discard any
type of solid waste in or upon an official solid waste disposal site, at a time
other than the regular business hours and days during which such site is open
to the public.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.12 HAULING SOLID WASTE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to cause or to permit any vehicle used
to haul or transport solid waste to be so loaded, or to be of such condition as
to permit any of such solid waste to drop or fall upon or to drop upon any
public lands, rights-of-way of any kind or private lands all persons whether
commercial or not shall cover all solid waste. A violation of this section may
result in the imposition of criminal and civil penalties set forth in §
51.99.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.13 DISPOSAL OF HAZARDOUS, DANGEROUS OR UNAUTHORIZED WASTE MATERIAL.
   It shall be unlawful and a violation of this subchapter for any person to
deposit or dispose of or in any way aid or assist in the deposition or disposal
of, or provide for delivery of, any dangerous, hazardous or unauthorized waste
material within the solid waste disposal system or anywhere within the county,
unless specifically authorized at an official solid waste disposal site.
Nothing in this subchapter is intended to conflict with the provisions of the
State Hazardous Waste Management Act, Idaho Code Title 39, Chapter 44 or any
other state or federal law or regulation.
   (A)   Prohibited disposals. No person shall deposit or discharge any of the
following substances into or upon an official or private solid waste disposal
site or anywhere within the county:
      (1)   Hazardous or acutely hazardous waste pesticides designated by the
Department or EPA;
      (2)   Hazardous solid waste as designated by the Department or the EPA;
      (3)   Flammable or explosive liquids, solids or gasses;
      (4)   Acids;
      (5)   Radioactive materials defined as hazardous materials under federal
law and applicable regulations, including any substance required by the United
States Department of Transportation to have type A packaging or type B
packaging, as defined in 49 C.F.R. § 173.426, or as subsequently amended;
      (6)   Oversized tires for which the county does not have a contract or
capability to remove from a solid waste disposal site;
      (7)   Wind turbines or any associated equipment;
      (8)   Solar panels or any of their associated equipment;
      (9)   Commercial/industrial waste from commercial/industrial generators
that will prevent the safe continuous operation of the county waste storage
facilities as determined by the County Commissioners from time to time; and
      (10)   Any waste that will cause or could cause, by its deposit therein,
the county to violate any DEQ regulations as outlined by state statue.
   (B)   Controlled disposals. No person shall deposit or discharge any of the
following substances anywhere within the county, or into or upon an official
solid waste disposal site, except at designated locations within an official
solid waste disposal site:
      (1)   Appliances: washing machines, dryers, stoves, refrigerators,
freezers or other large household appliances;
      (2)   Vehicles;
      (3)   Sewage sludge;
      (4)   Tires of any type;
      (5)   Automobile batteries;
      (6)   Chemical cans (before being accepted, must be emptied, triple
rinsed and punctured);
      (7)   Paint;
      (8)   Yard waste (trees, shrubs, limbs, leaves, grass or similar
material);
      (9)   Construction or demolition waste; and
      (10)   Solid waste, other than the above listed substances.
   (C)   Special burials. The following material may be deposited into an
official solid waste disposal site only by special permission of the county.
      (1)   Asbestos; and
      (2)   Animals or any portion of animals.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.14 USER FEES.
   (A)   To partially fund the operations of the solid waste disposal system,
the Board shall establish a user fee to be assessed annually. Such user fees
shall be set by ordinance, known as the Solid Waste User Fee Ordinance.
   (B)   User fees shall be paid by all persons and by municipal and commercial
haulers of solid waste dumped or deposited, which is generated or picked up
within the county or outside the boundaries of the county as assessed.
   (C)   There shall be a separate and additional fee set by the Solid Waste
User Fee Ordinance for all persons and municipal and commercial haulers for
controlled disposals and special burials at any official solid waste disposal
site.
(Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)
TIRE DISPOSAL
§ 51.25 POLICY.
   (A)   It shall be the policy of the county and the county’s solid waste
system to accept tires for disposal.
   (B)   Said tires will not be disposed of in landfills but instead will be
disposed of by the county through various means as the county deems in its best
interests.
   (C)   Tires will be processed for recycling to meet the requirements of
Idaho Code Title 39, Chapter 65, as amended.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994)
§ 51.26 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COUNTY RESIDENT OR BUSINESS. Any person or business owning or leasing
property or operating a business within the confines of the county.
   SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL SYSTEM. A division of the county authorized by the
Board of County Commissioners to provide for the appropriate unlawful disposal
of tires.
   TIRE. Any rubberized material generally used for the purpose of conveying a
vehicle, implement, equipment and the like from one location to another
location, excepting therefrom bicycle and wheelbarrow tires.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994)
§ 51.27 FEES.
   (A)   Fee charged on purchase/non-disposal. It is the belief of the County
Commission that most effective means of providing for the cost of disposal is
to collect said cost at the time of sale of said tires. Such will provide for
those tires which may be removed out of the county and disposed of, and those
tires which may be illegally disposed of and for which liability or penalty
cannot be established. For this purpose, the aforementioned fees shall be
charged at the time any tire, whether new or used, is sold in the county. All
persons or entities selling tires shall keep accurate accounting of all tires
sold and shall provide the accounting to the county on a quarterly basis with
payment, as provided herein. Further, all tire sellers within the county shall
be subject to an audit by the county of all of their books relating to the sale
of tires for the purpose of verifying that the amounts collected are correct
and have been paid over to the county.
   (B)   Fee estimate. Further, any seller of tires not making payment to the
county in a timely fashion as provided for herein shall be assessed an
estimated charge by the solid waste disposal system which shall be assessed and
included on a tax roll of the entity or person and shall become a lien against
all property, whether personal or real property owned by the person or entity.
Further, said tire seller shall be subject to a fine of $100 per day for every
day of late payment.
   (C)   Terms and payments. There shall be no charge to county residents and
county businesses for the disposing of tires except as specifically provided
for herein. Tire sellers shall have the right to make application to the county
to create a billing system for their businesses. Anyone with an account with
the county shall make said payment on a quarterly basis with each quarter
ending on March 31, June 30, September 30 and December 31. All fees collected
shall be paid within ten days after the close of each quarter. Tire sellers who
do not create an account with the county shall be required to make payments on
a daily basis.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994)
§ 51.28 FEE SCHEDULE.
   (A)   The Board of County Commissioners shall set on or before October 1 of
each year a fee schedule. For the calendar year 1995, said fee schedule shall
be in such amount as is provided for in Exhibit “A” attached to the ordinance
codified in this subchapter. Prior to October 1 each year, the Commissioners
shall be authorized by resolution to adopt a fee schedule which shall become
effective under this subchapter and shall become the appropriate fee schedule
under this subchapter for the subsequent calendar year. Said fee shall be
charged upon the sale of any tire as described in this subchapter. Schedule A
shall be the fee schedule for the period of January 1, 1995, to December 31,
1995.
   (B)   Businesses and residents of the county may dispose of tires at the
county waste transfer station at the charges as set forth herein. The proposed
fees are for tires with the rims removed, an additional fee of $5 will be
assessed per tire for automobile and light truck tires still on the rim. Large
truck, agricultural implement and earth-moving equipment tires will not be
accepted if the tires are still on the rim.
 
Agricultural implement tires     $20 per tire
Automobile and light truck tires $1.50 per tire
Earth-moving equipment tires     $50 per tire
Large truck tires                $3 per tire
 
   (C)   Tires to be disposed of will be accepted at the county waste transfer
station located at 2455 Hemmert Avenue, Idaho Falls, during normal working
hours of 7:00 a.m. until 7:00 p.m., Monday through Saturday.
   (D)   Tire dealers will collect said fees at the time of sale of said tires
at the time any tire, whether new or used, is sold in the county. All persons
or entities selling tires shall keep accurate accounting of all tires sold and
shall provide the accounting to the county on a quarterly basis with payment,
as provided herein. Quarterly payments shall be made within ten days of the end
of each quarter. The end of the quarters are considered to be March 31, June
30, September 30 and December 31. All tire sellers within the county shall be
subject to an audit of their books relating to the sale of tires by the county
for the purpose of verifying that the amounts are correct and have been paid
over to the county.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994)
§ 51.29 PROCEDURE.
   (A)   Idaho Code Title 39, Chapter 65 defines proper disposal procedures for
waste tires. Said legislation provides for a fine of $500 per tire if disposed
of through a landfill. The intent of the legislation is to encourage the
recycling of tires and to create an available rubber source for road
construction as directed in federal legislation. Businesses and residents of
the county may dispose of tires at the county waste transfer station without
charge. Nonresidents and businesses of the county may dispose of tires at the
county waste transfer station by making payment in the amounts set by the
County Commissioners. All persons or entities will be charged an additional
cost of $5 per tire for each tire mounted on a rim.
   (B)   Tires from bicycles and wheelbarrows and lawnmowers are exempt from
recycling requirements and may be disposed of at the county waste transfer
station at no charge for said bicycle and wheelbarrow tires.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994)
§ 51.30 ENFORCEMENT.
   Any private residents and businesses whether or not a resident of the county
disposing of tire(s) in a manner which does not conform to the procedures
outlined above may be denied disposal until proper procedures are followed.
Failure to follow disposal as set forth herein shall be deemed inappropriate
and unlawful disposal.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.31 CIVIL AND CRIMINAL REMEDIES.
   The prosecution of any civil remedy of this subchapter set forth shall not
bar the prosecution of any criminal proceeding herein provided.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   (1)   A violation of any provision of §§
51.01 through
51.14 shall be a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine not to exceed $300, or
imprisonment for a period not to exceed six months in the county jail, or both
for each offense. Each day or each occurrence if more than one a day, that such
a violation occurs or continues shall constitute a separate criminal offense.
      (2)   Any violation of any provision of §§
51.01 through
51.14 may also result in the filing of a civil complaint for civil damages
imposed upon and person committing such act. The civil damages shall amount to
three times the actual damage or cost to restore the site of disposal to its
original condition, whichever is greater. Such civil action shall be filed in
the county, and any remainder of damages collected after restoration shall be
deposited in the Solid Waste Find.
      (3)   Whenever it appears to the Board that any person has engaged or is
about to engage in any act or practice constituting a violation of state law or
of §§
51.01 through
51.14, the county may bring an action to enjoin any such acts or practices and
to enforce compliance with state law or with §§
51.01 through
51.14. Any civil action for civil damages described above shall be in addition
to the criminal penalties set forth herein.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
51.25 through
51.31 shall be guilty of the greater of a $500 fine for each tire improperly
disposed of as provided by Idaho Code Title 39, Chapter 65 or shall be guilty
of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be punished as provided
under the definition of misdemeanor and misdemeanor convictions in Idaho Code.
   (C)   Any person unlawfully depositing or disposing of, or in any adding or
assisting in the disposing or depositing of or providing for delivery of any
tire within the county solid waste disposal system, or anywhere within the
county in an improper manner, as provided in §§
51.25 through
51.31, shall be civilly liable for the full cost and expense of the cleanup,
control, elimination, abatement, radication, decontamination and other
remediation which is undertaken by the county or any other public agency to
properly correct damage or potential damage associated with said violation and/
or civil responsibility for the violation; criminal misdemeanor liability for
maximum of six months imprisonment and/or $300 fine; and/or civil liability for
cleanup costs.
(Ord. 167-94, passed 10-26-1994; Ord. 255-23, passed 8-16-2023)

VEHICLE LICENSING
§ 70.01 TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known and cited as the Bonneville County Vehicle
License Fee Ordinance.
(Ord. 135-83, passed 11-2-1983)
§ 70.02 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter is to provide funds sufficient to pay the
expenses incurred by the county in its exercise of duties relating to vehicle
licensure.
(Ord. 135-83, passed 11-2-1983)
§ 70.03 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, all words and phrases not specifically
defined herein will be given their ordinary, commonly understood and accepted
meaning.
(Ord. 135-83, passed 11-2-1983)
§ 70.04 ADMINISTRATIVE FEES.
   An administrative fee for vehicle licensure shall be established by
resolution of the Board of County Commissioners; provided, that the fee
collected shall: be reasonably related to, but shall not exceed, the actual
cost of the service being rendered; be collected in addition to each motor
vehicle registration tax or fee amount collected under the provisions of the
Idaho Code Title 49; and be paid to the County Treasurer and be placed in the
county current expense fund.
(Ord. 135-83, passed 11-2-1983; Ord. passed 6-20-1988; Res. passed 6-20-1988)
§ 70.05 CONFLICTS.
   Requirements specifically addressed by more than one code will be in
accordance with the most restrictive code adopted herein.
(Ord. 135-83, passed 11-2-1983)
DUAL ROADWAY USE
§ 70.20 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CLOSURE.
      (1)   The period of time designated by the Board of County Commissioners
in which roads may be closed to public access or limited access.
      (2)   The prohibition of any person from plowing and/or using said
roadway for vehicular purposes or any other purpose designated by the Board of
County Commissioners.
   DESIGNATED ROADWAY. The roadways specifically designated by the Board of
County Commissioners for closure.
   DOG SLED. Any device or sled drawn by any animal, including dogs.
   PUBLIC ROADWAY. Any portions of any highway or road used for vehicular
travel, whether possessing pavement or not which is controlled by an authority
other than the State Transportation Department.
   SNOWMOBILE. Any self-propelled vehicle under 1,000 pounds unladed gross
weight, designed primarily for travel on snow or ice over natural terrain,
which may be steered by tracks, skis or runners, and which is not otherwise
registered or licensed under the laws of the state.
   VEHICULAR. Motor vehicle and every vehicle, other than a snowmobile, which
is self-propelled and every vehicle which is propelled by electrical power or
operated from overhead trolley wires but not operated upon rails, except
vehicles moving slowly by human power and motorized wheelchairs, including
every device in which or by which any person or property is or may be
transported or drawn upon a highway, except devices used exclusively upon
stationary rails or tracks.
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996)
§ 70.21 ROADWAY DESIGNATION.
   (A)   The Board of County Commissioners shall, prior to October 1 of each
year, by resolution, designate those roadways which shall be designated as
snowmobile trails and/or road closure areas within the county. The Board of
County Commissioners shall prior to October 1 of each year, designate the
nature and the extent of the closure during which vehicles may not operate upon
designated roadways and upon which no one shall be permitted to plow said
roadways within the county for the next one year.
   (B)   The snowmobile trails and road closure designation for the period of
October 1, 1996, through September 30, 1997, is attached to the ordinance
codified in this subchapter as Exhibit “A.”
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996)
§ 70.22 ROADWAY CLOSURES.
   (A)   Inasmuch as certain roadways within the county receive sufficient
snowfall that it is impractical for the county to maintain said roadways during
heavy snowfall periods; and inasmuch as the county has the duty to maintain
said roadways and to secure the roadway system within the county; and inasmuch
as the cost and feasibility are not within reasonable demands of the county,
necessitating the closure of certain roadways; and because of the need to
safeguard the public from operating vehicles, dog sleds and cross-country
skiing in areas which would result in injury to snowmobile trails and in areas
where public assistance and emergency care would be difficult to provide; and
because said roadways may become non-navigable because of snowfall in very
short periods of time; and because said roadways may be valuable for the use by
snowmobiles; the County Commission shall, by resolution and by posting notice
of said resolution at the Board of Commissioners office, have the right to
close both designated roadways and such roadways as designated in §
70.21 and such additional roadways as the County Commissioners shall, in their
sole discretion, deem necessary upon resolution of a majority of the Board and
upon notice being posted by notice at the County Courthouse and at the roadway
at least 24 hours prior to said roadway closure.
   (B)   (1)   Said resolution shall designate the description of the roadway
and the date and time when the road shall be closed to vehicles.
      (2)   The 24-hour notice shall be applicable regardless of the day of the
week on which said notice is posted.
      (3)   Said notice shall reference the resolution of the Board of County
Commissioners and shall designate that anyone violating the road closure may be
issued a citation for said violation.
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996)
§ 70.23 VIOLATION.
   (A)   It shall be a violation of this subchapter for any individual to
operate a motor vehicle upon the designated roadways within the county at such
times as said roadways have been closed by the County Commission.
   (B)   (1)   The owner of any vehicle operated upon any closed road within
the county during the closure period shall be guilty of an infraction violation
as defined by Idaho Code.
      (2)   It shall further be a violation for anyone to use said closed
roadways for dog sled or cross-country skiing use.
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.24 CIVIL AND CRIMINAL REMEDIES.
   (A)   The prosecution of any civil remedy in this subchapter set forth shall
not bar the prosecution of any criminal proceeding herein provided.
   (B)   Further, the prosecution of any criminal proceeding shall not bar the
prosecution of any civil remedy herein provided.
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996)
§ 70.25 INJUNCTIVE RELIEF.
   The County Commission, by and through the Prosecuting Attorney’s office,
shall be authorized upon the determination of anyone in violation of this
subchapter to immediately apply for injunctive relief with any court of
competent jurisdiction which said injunctive relief should be granted for the
purpose of providing for the enforcement of this subchapter.
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996)
AIR COMPRESSION BRAKES
§ 70.40 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COMPRESSION BRAKE. Any braking system of a motor vehicle that modifies
engine valve operation to use engine compression to slow the motor vehicle and
shall include brakes commonly referred to as JAKE BRAKES or ENGINE BRAKES.
   DYNAMIC BRAKING DEVICE. A device used primarily on trucks for the conversion
of the engine from an internal combustion to air compressor for the purpose of
braking. This term is also commonly referred to as a JACOB’S BRAKE, JAKE BRAKE,
ENGINE BRAKE or COMPRESSION BRAKE).
   ENGINE BRAKE. A hydraulically operated device that operates a power
producing diesel engine into a power absorbing retarding mechanism that
produces a noise.
(Ord. 263-24, passed 8-7-2024)
§ 70.41 BRAKE USE RESTRICTIONS.
   (A)   Use near any city (municipal corporation). No operator of any motor
vehicle, excepting emergency vehicles operated by first responders (such as
fire, ambulance and law enforcement), shall use or activate a compression
brake, engine brake or dynamic braking device within one mile of any of the
following incorporated city limits:
      (1)   City of Idaho Falls;
      (2)   City of Ammon;
      (3)   City of Ucon;
      (4)   City of Iona; and
      (5)   Any other incorporated area.
 
   (B)   Use in other designated county areas. No operator of any motor
vehicle, excepting emergency vehicles operated by first responders (such as
fire, ambulance and law enforcement), shall use or activate a compression
brake, engine brake or dynamic braking device within designated areas of the
county. Said designated areas will have two road signs designating the
beginning and ending of the dynamic braking device prohibition area, reading
respectively at least as follows.
 
ENGINE BRAKES PROHIBITED
NEXT          MILES
Bonneville County Ordinance 263-24
 
END OF ENGINE BRAKE PROHIBITION AREA
Bonneville County Ordinance 263-24
 
         (a)   Said designated areas shall be made by the County Commissioners.
To establish a designated area, any person may apply to the County
Commissioners setting forth a proposed designated area. A public meeting shall
be set. At the meeting, any interested party may present evidence or testimony
concerning the proposed designated area.
         (b)   The County Commissioners will then decide whether or not to
designate the area as a prohibited one. The County Public Works Director will
order corresponding MUTCD approved signs after direction given in a public
meeting and recorded in the official minutes of the County Commissioners.
(Ord. 263-24, passed 8-7-2024) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any operator of a vehicle or dog sled or a person cross-country
skiing, violating any provision of §§
70.20 through
70.25 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be
punished as provided under the definition of a misdemeanor and misdemeanor
convictions in state law. The owner of any vehicle operated upon any closed
road in violation of any provision of §§
70.20 through
70.25 shall be guilty of an infraction and, upon conviction thereof, shall be
punished as provided under the definition of an infraction in state law.
   (C)   (1)   (a)   Any person violating any provision of §
70.41(A) for the first time shall be guilty of an infraction and shall be
punished by imposition of a fine in the amount of $50.
         (b)   Any person violating any provision of §
70.41(A) who has previously been found guilty or pled guilty to a violation
shall be guilty of an infraction and shall be punished by imposition of a fine
in the amount of $90.
         (c)   Any person violating any provision of §
70.41(A) who has previously been found guilty or pled guilty of at least two
prior violations of §
70.41(A) shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be punished by imprisonment
confinement in a county jail for a period not to exceed six months, or by a
fine not to exceed $1,000, or by both (as per Idaho Code § 18-113).
         (d)   All fine amounts set forth above for infraction and misdemeanor
offenses shall be separate and apart from any court costs assessed pursuant to
Idaho Code § 31-3201A and any other provision of state law.
      (2)   (a)   Any person or entity violating the provisions of §
70.41(B) for the first time, shall be guilty of an infraction and shall be
punished by imposition of a fine in the amount of $50.
         (b)   Any person or entity which violates §
70.41(B) who has previously been found guilty or pled guilty of a violation
within five years shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be punished by
imprisonment confinement in a county jail for a period not to exceed six
months, or by a fine not to exceed $1,000, or by both (as per Idaho Code § 18-
113).
(Ord. 176-96, passed 2-23-1996; Ord. 263-24, passed 8-7-2024)
CHAPTER 71: PARKING REGULATIONS
Section
   
71.01   Parking and storing of vehicles, trailers and the like
   
71.02   Ririe Reservoir parking regulations
 
   
71.99   Penalty
§ 71.01 PARKING AND STORING OF VEHICLES, TRAILERS AND THE LIKE.
   (A)   No vehicle which is not properly and currently licensed and in
operating condition, or any stripped dismantled or junked machinery, salvage
material, debris, vehicle body or parts may be parked, stored or maintained
upon any roads, streets, sidewalks or highways in the unincorporated area of
the county, and that none of the items described above may be parked, stored or
maintained on any premises at a location between the minimum building setback
line and the road right-of-way. In a platted residential subdivision,
agricultural density subdivision and residential zones, all such items shall be
stored or maintained behind the front face of the residential building and
screened or covered as needed to protect the public view by a maintained tarp,
or by growth, fences, garages or other substantial structures allowed by the
Building and Zoning Codes of the county which will effectively provide such
screening; and that where a gate is used as a screening device it shall be kept
closed when not being used for ingress or egress. The total amount of area
devoted to the outdoor storage of the items listed above will be limited to 300
square feet in R and MH Zones, 600 square feet in RA, RP and R-F Zones. In A-
1 and G-1 Zones, no more than 1% of the lot or parcel may be used, with a
maximum limit of 10,000 square feet. In all cases contiguous single owned
parcels and lots shall be aggregated and considered to be a single parcel.
   (B)   All recreational vehicles, trailers, campers, boats, motorcycles,
commercial vehicles and farm machinery not regularly used (moved at least once
per month) shall be stored or parked behind the building setback line and not
in front of the residential dwelling unit and behind the side yard setback line
for corner lots for the side yard facing the street, road or highway.
   (C)   No vehicle, object or equipment shall be parked, placed or maintained
except when necessary to allow movement of other vehicular traffic or pursuant
to the direction of a peace officer or traffic control device, in any of the
following designated places, to-wit:
      (1)   Upon any sidewalk or obstructing any sidewalk or upon any regularly
used or designated walkway, bridle path or bicycle path;
      (2)   On the traffic side of any vehicle stopped or parked at the edge or
curb of a highway;
      (3)   Within any portion of a public or private driveway or entrance to a
public street;
      (4)   Within an intersection or a crosswalk, within 15 feet of a fire
hydrant;
      (5)   Within 30 feet of the approach to any flashing beacon, stop sign or
traffic control signal;
      (6)   Within 50 feet of the nearest rail of a railroad crossing;
      (7)   Within 20 feet of the driveway entrance to any fire station or
crosswalk at an intersection;
      (8)   Beside or opposite any street excavation or obstruction in a manner
which obstructs traffic, upon any street or portion thereof which blocks or
interferes with the regular flow of vehicular traffic;
      (9)   Other than headed in the direction of traffic flow;
      (10)   Upon any bridge or elevated structure upon or part of a public
highway;
      (11)   At any place where traffic regulatory signs prohibit such parking;
      (12)   In any fire apparatus access road as defined in the International
Fire Code; and
      (13)   Use any street for the purpose of repairing any vehicle, except
for temporary emergency repairs.
   (D)   Parking shall only be allowed on county streets which are designed
with enough additional pavement width to allow for on street parking. No
vehicle shall be parked upon any street within the county for a period of more
than 48 consecutive hours in the same location. Any vehicle so parked or stored
may be removed by or under the direction of any peace officer and may be
impounded in accordance with the provisions of this chapter. The owner of the
impounded vehicle shall pay a reasonable towing and storage fee to the person
or company effecting the removal before such vehicle or trailer may be
released.
   (E)   Any person who parks or allows a vehicle to be parked in any parking
space designated for use by persons with disabilities and signed in conformity
with Idaho Code § 49-410 is guilty of an infraction, unless the vehicle is
momentarily in the space for the purpose of allowing a disabled person to enter
or leave the vehicle, or unless special license plates or a temporary card
issued for the disabled pursuant to Idaho Code § 49-410 is displayed on the
vehicle. For the purpose of this division (E), the term PERSON WITH A
DISABILITY shall have the same meaning ascribed in Idaho Code § 49-117(7)(b).
The provisions of this section shall be enforceable with respect to handicapped
parking spaces upon public property and private property open to public use.
   (F)   Only cars and pickups that are regularly used shall be parked on the
street, road or highway adjacent to any residential dwelling lot or location,
and all such vehicles so parked shall be parked with the interior wheels no
farther than 18 inches from the curb line where there is a curb and, if there
is no curb, so as not to impede any traffic lane. No vehicle with body
dimensions exceeding 263 inches in length, 82 inches in height and 80 inches in
width shall be parked on any street, road or highway. All commercial vehicles
and farm machinery shall not be parked on any street, road or highway other
than for the purpose of loading or unloading such vehicle in an expeditious
manner not to exceed two hours. All recreational vehicles, trailers, campers,
boats, motorcycles shall not be parked on any street, road or highway other
than for the purpose of loading or unloading such vehicle in an expeditious
manner not to exceed a cumulative total of 48 hours in any consecutive seven-
day time period.
(Ord. 105-76, passed 6-21-1976; Ord. 224-12, passed 10-17-2012) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.02 RIRIE RESERVOIR PARKING REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      CLOSURE. The period of time designated by the Board of County
Commissioners in which the parking area may be closed to the general public.
      PARKING AREA. Any portion of any highway or road, parking area, restroom,
boat landing, boat dock, park area or any associated area of Blacktail and
Juniper boat landing areas at the Ririe Reservoir.
      PARKING FEE or FEE. Fees designated by the Board of County Commissioners
for the use of a parking area.
   (B)   Use fee. The Board of County Commissioners shall hereby set the
parking fee as follows:
      (1)   Winter season passes (November 1 to April 30): $5 per admitted
vehicle, per day, Monday through Friday; $10 per admitted vehicle per day on
weekends and holidays; $30 for a winter season pass which said season pass is
discounted to $20 for seniors aged 62 and older;
      (2)   Summer season passes (May 1 to October 31): $5 per admitted
vehicle, per day, Monday through Friday; $10 per admitted vehicle per day on
weekends and holidays; $80 for a summer season pass which said season pass is
discounted to $60 for seniors aged 62 and older; and
      (3)   Combined summer and winter pass (January 1 to December 31): $95 for
an annual combined summer and winter pass, which said annual pass is $70 for
seniors aged 62 and older. The county may, by resolution, from time-to-time
hereafter, designate an increase in the parking fee by resolution adopted by
the County Commissioners.
   (C)   Closure blacktail. As there exists a need to close a parking area as
to protect the parking areas from vandalism, misuse and provide periodic
maintenance and repairs, and to protect the safety and welfare of the public,
the Blacktail parking areas shall be closed daily from 10:00 p.m. until the
following morning at 6:00 a.m. The County Commissioners may, from time to time,
alter the dates of closure and/or the seasons of closure, by resolution.
   (D)   Juniper. Juniper Boat Ramp lower parking lot shall be closed from
Memorial Day to Labor Day to all vehicles which are not towing a trailer.
   (E)   Violations. It shall be a violation of this section for any individual
to refuse to pay the parking fee or to be in a parking area or to operate a
vehicle in a parking area during the periods of closure.
(Ord. 267-25, passed 4-30-2025) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Violation of any of the provisions of §
71.01 shall be deemed guilty of an infraction and, upon conviction thereof,
shall be fined $25 for each violation. If an owner or operator parks more than
one vehicle in violation of §
71.01, each vehicle shall be deemed a separate violation. Each day a violation
continues to exist shall constitute a separate offense. The issuance of a
notice of violation shall not be the exclusive remedy for enforcing the parking
regulations of §
71.01 and all other lawful remedies are reserved, including prosecution by
filing a criminal complaint for an infraction violation.
   (B)   Any person in violation of §
71.02 or refusing to pay the required fee shall be guilty of violating §
71.02 and shall pay the fee provided for herein. Any unauthorized person in the
parking area or operating a vehicle in a parking area during any period of
closure shall be guilty of violating §
71.02 and shall pay the fee provided for herein.
(Ord. 105-76, passed 6-21-1976; Ord. 224-12, passed 10-17-2012)
CHAPTER 72: RECREATIONAL VEHICLES
Section
