SNOWMOBILES
§ 72.01 POLICY.
   (A)   It shall be the policy of the county to permit and allow in
conjunction with any regulation adopted by and permitted by the State
Department of Fish and Game and as permitted by the Federal Bureau of
Reclamation, ice fishing upon Ririe Reservoir from the location of the Ririe
Reservoir Dam and one mile up lake/stream.
   (B)   Because the area around, at and surrounding the Ririe Reservoir, lying
within the county, is frequently used by those operating snowmobiles, all-
terrain vehicles (ATV) and utility-terrain vehicles (UTV), it shall be the
policy of the county to prohibit the use of snowmobiles, all-terrain vehicles
(ATV) and utility-terrain vehicles (UTV) upon any portion of the Ririe
Reservoir or on public lands within one-quarter mile of the median high-water
mark of the Ririe Reservoir, except for the explicit administrative purposes of
the county, the State Department of Fish and Game, Federal Bureau of
Reclamation and for ice fishers who are using said snowmobiles, all-terrain
vehicles (ATV) and utility-terrain vehicles (UTV) for the direct access to and
from fishing sites on said Ririe Reservoir.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLES (ATV) and UTILITY-TERRAIN VEHICLES (UTV). Include any
vehicle commonly known as a quad, quad bike, three-wheeler, four-wheeler or
quadricycle as defined by the American National Standards Institute (ANSI) and
is a vehicle that travels on low-pressure tires, with a seat that is straddled
or used by the operator, along with handlebars for steering control.
   COUNTY. Bonneville County, Idaho, a political subdivision of the State of
Idaho.
   PERSONS. Any individual, entity, corporation, partnership, business and the
like.
   PUBLIC LAND. The Ririe Reservoir and any land owned by the public or any
entity of the public and all other land that is not owned by private persons.
   RIRIE RESERVOIR. The reservoir currently existing within the county and
commonly referred to as the Ririe Reservoir with water levels at the median
high-water mark regardless of the actual levels of the water in said Reservoir.
   SNOWMOBILE. Any self-propelled vehicle designed primarily for travel on snow
or ice or natural terrain, which may be steered by tracks, skis or runners.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017)
§ 72.03 PROHIBITION.
   It shall be unlawful and illegal for any person, whether or not a resident
of the county, to operate a snowmobile, all-terrain vehicles (ATV) or utility-
terrain vehicles (UTV) on any part of the Ririe Reservoir and/or on any public
land within one-quarter mile of the median high-water mark of the Ririe
Reservoir except for the explicit purpose of direct access to ice fishing and
direct access back off said reservoir to parting areas. This subchapter shall
not apply to employees and personnel of the county, the State Department of
Fish and Game or the Federal Bureau of Reclamation when acting in their
official duties.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.04 ENFORCEMENT.
   Any persons, whether or not a resident of the county, operating a
snowmobile, all-terrain vehicles (ATV) or utility-terrain vehicles (UTV), in
opposition to this subchapter or any provision herein, shall be deemed
inappropriate and unlawful.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.05 CIVIL AND CRIMINAL REMEDIES.
   The prosecution of any civil remedy of this subchapter shall not bar the
prosecution of any criminal proceedings herein provided, nor shall any criminal
prosecution of any violation of this subchapter bar the prosecution of any
civil remedy of this subchapter.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017) Penalty, see §
72.99
WATERCRAFT
§ 72.20 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   EXCESSIVE WAKE. The operation of a vessel that:
      (1)   In an artificially bow-high manner, increases or enhances a wake,
including wake enhancement by use of ballast, mechanical hydrofoils, uneven
loading;
      (2)   Causes water to lap onto or over a dock, pier or other lawfully
permitted encroachment; or
      (3)   Causes water to without due caution and circumspection and in a
manner as to endanger or be likely to endanger any person or property.
   NO EXCESSIVE WAKE ZONE. An area were no excessive wake shall occur.
   NO-WAKE ZONE. A designated area where no wake shall occur.
   VESSEL. Includes every description of watercraft, including a seaplane on
the water, used or capable of being used as a means of transportation on water,
but does not include float houses, diver’s aids operated and designed primarily
to propel a diver below, or objects modified to be used as a means of
transportation on the water, such as inflatable air mattress, single inner
tubes and beach and water toys. For purposes of defining the term VESSEL, a
motorboat, motor-driven craft and watercraft are synonymous.
(Ord. 268-25, passed 5-7-2025)
§ 72.21 NO-WAKE ZONES.
   The following no-wake zones are hereby established pertaining to the
following public bodies of water in the county.
   (A)   Ririe (Blacktail) Reservoir. There shall be no excessive wake:
      (1)   Within 100 feet from any person or vessel in the water;
      (2)   Within 100 feet from any shoreline or other structure (including
boat docks and floating docks);
      (3)   Within 300 feet from the point where the water meets the
unsubmerged portion of the following boat ramp(s):
         (a)   Juniper boat ramp; and
         (b)   Blacktail boat ramp.
      (4)   Within 200 feet from the point where the water meets the shore of
the following recreation area(s):
         (a)   Blacktail Park;
         (b)   Benchland Park; and
         (c)   Willow Point Park.
   (B)   Palisades Reservoir. There shall be no excessive wake:
      (1)   Within 100 feet from any person or vessel in the water;
      (2)   Within 100 feet from any shoreline or other structure (including
boat docks and floating docks); and
      (3)   Within 300 feet from the point where the water meets the
unsubmerged portion of the following boat ramp(s):
         (a)   Calamity boat ramp;
         (b)   Blowout boat ramp; and
         (c)   Big Elk boat ramp.
      (4)   Within 200 feet from the point where the water meets the shore of
the following recreation area(s):
         (a)   Pine Bay boat mooring park;
         (b)   Snake River boat mooring park; and
         (c)   McCoy Creek campground.
(Ord. 268-25, passed 5-7-2025)
§ 72.22 ENFORCEMENT.
   This subchapter may be enforced by any officer or deputy of the County
Sheriff’s Office, Forest Service law enforcement or Bureau of Land Management
law enforcement.
(Ord. 268-25, passed 5-7-2025)
§ 72.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   (1)   Any person violating any provision of §§
72.01 through
72.05 shall be guilty of the a misdemeanor and shall be subject to fine in an
amount of $300 and/or subject to imprisonment in the county jail for a maximum
of six months or as otherwise provided for by state law.
      (2)   Any unlawful or inappropriate operation of a snowmobile, all-
terrain vehicles (ATV) or utility-terrain vehicles (UTV) as provided for in §§
72.01 through
72.05, or any abating or assisting in the operation of a snowmobile in
violation of §§
72.01 through
72.05, shall constitute a violation which shall cause said person who does
violate §§
72.01 through
72.05 to be civilly liable for the full cost and expense of any and all damage
caused by said illegal operation, and/or civil responsibility for the violation
in an amount not to exceed $500 and/or such additional amount to reimburse for
any cost whether directly or indirectly incurred by any person or public
entity; criminal misdemeanor liability punishable by imprisonment in the county
jail for a maximum of six months and/or a $300 fine, and/or any other civil or
criminal liability that may exist in state law or in common law.
   (C)   (1)   Each violation of §§
72.20 through
72.22 shall constitute a separate offense.
      (2)   Unless otherwise specified herein or by the Idaho Code, a violation
of any provision of §§
72.20 through
72.22 shall constitute an infraction or misdemeanor as follows.
         (a)   A first offense shall constitute an infraction punishable by a
fine of $50.
         (b)   A second offense shall constitute an infraction punishable by a
fine of $100.
         (c)   A third offense shall constitute a misdemeanor punishable by a
fine not to exceed $300 and jail time not to exceed six months.
(Ord. 229-17, passed 2-10-2017; Ord. 268-25, passed 5-7-2025)
CHAPTER 73: TRAFFIC SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   Roads requiring helmets while operating off-highway vehicles
SCHEDULE I. ROADS REQUIRING HELMETS WHILE OPERATING OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLES.
   (A)   It is the intent of these undersigned commissioners to promote the
safety of individuals driving OHV vehicles on the following roads in the county
which lie in the National Forest area: South Fork Snake River Road (#206);
Table Rock Canyon Road (#217); and McCoy Creek Road (#087). These roads
historically have a greater volume of traffic and sustain drivers of OHV
vehicles at a higher rate of speed. Such conditions require greater driving
safety and training to protect other drivers on these roads. The Commissioners
therefore feel compelled to protect the health and safety of all drivers by
instituting the ordinance codified in this schedule.
   (B)   For the purpose of this schedule, OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE (OHV) means any
all-terrain vehicle, motorbike, specialty off-highway vehicle or utility type
vehicle which are further defined by Idaho Code § 67-7101.
   (C)   All drivers operating an OHV vehicle shall be required to wear a
helmet and possess their own valid drivers’ license on the following roads in
the National Forest area in the county:
      (1)   South Forth Snake River Road (#206);
      (2)   Table Rock Canyon Road (#217); and
      (3)   McCoy Creek Road (#087).
(Ord. 219-10, passed 3-5-2010)

§ 90.01 USE OF RECREATION AREAS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      CLOSURE. The period of time designated by the Board of County
Commissioners in which the recreation area may be closed to the general public.
      RECREATION AREA. Any portions of any highway or road, park area,
restroom, boat landing or boat dock or any associated area at the Blacktail and
Juniper boat landing areas on the Ririe Reservoir.
      USE FEES. Fees designated by the Board of County Commissioners for the
use of the Recreation area.
   (B)   Use fee. The Board of County Commissioners shall, prior to May 1 of
each year, by resolution, designate the use fee to be charged at the
recreational areas for the upcoming next one year. The use fee for the period
of May 1, 1996, through April 30, 1997, shall be the amount of $2 per admitted
vehicle or $18 per vehicle per year.
   (C)   Closure. Inasmuch as there exists a need to close the recreational
areas to protect the recreational areas from vandalism, misuse and to provide
periodic maintenance and repairs and to protect the safety and welfare of the
public, the recreational areas shall be closed daily from 11:00 p.m. until the
following morning at 5:00 a.m. For the purpose of this section, closure shall
not include or apply to those persons using the recreational area exclusively
for fishing purposes and access or egress thereto.
   (D)   Violations. It shall be a violation of this section for any individual
to refuse to pay the use fee or to be in a recreational area or to operate a
vehicle in a recreational area during any period of closure. Any person in
violation of or refusing to pay the required use fee shall be guilty of an
infraction violation as defined by state law. Any unauthorized person in the
recreational area or operating a vehicle in the recreational area during any
period of closure shall be guilty of a misdemeanor as defined by state law.
(Ord. 177-96, passed 4-30-1996) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.02 POSSESSION OF ALCOHOL IN PUBLIC PARKS OR COUNTY FAIRGROUNDS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR. Any liquid or solid, patented or not, containing
alcohol, spirits or wine and susceptible of being consumed by human being, for
beverage purposes and containing more than 4% of alcohol by weight; and
includes:
         (a)   ALCOHOL. The product of distillation of any fermented liquor,
rectified either once or oftener, whatever maybe the origin thereof, or
synthetic ethyl alcohol.
         (b)   SPIRITS. Any beverage which contains alcohol obtained by
distillation mixed with drinkable water and other substances in solution,
including, among other things, brandy, rum, whiskey and gin.
         (c)   WINE. Any alcoholic beverage obtained by the fermentation of
natural sugar content of fruits (grapes, apples and the like) or other
agriculture products containing sugar (honey, milk and the like).
      BEER. Any beverage obtained by the alcoholic fermentation of an infusion
or decoction of barley, malt and/or other ingredients in drinkable water and
which beverage contains not more than 4% of alcohol by weight.
   (B)   Exercise of police power. This section shall be deemed an exercise of
the police power of the county for the protection of the public health and
safety of the citizens, and all its provisions shall be liberally construed for
the attainment of that purpose.
   (C)   Unlawful to possess alcoholic liquor or beer in any public park or the
County Fairgrounds. It shall be unlawful for any person to possess or have in
his or her possession any alcoholic liquor or beer at the County Fairgrounds.
(Ord. 241-22, passed 2-9-2022) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   All person or operator of a vehicle, violating any provision of §
90.01 shall be guilty of either an infraction or a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction thereof, shall be punished as provided under either the definition
of an infraction or misdemeanor convictions in state law.
   (B)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
90.02 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be
punishable by fine of not more than $300 or by imprisonment for not more than
30 days, or by both such fine and imprisonment.
(Ord. 177-96, passed 4-30-1996; Ord. 241-22, passed 2-9-2022)

WEEDS
§ 91.01 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   WEEDS AND OFFENSIVE PLANTS. All plants of more than ten inches in height,
measured from the surface of the ground, growing or existing in open spaces on
any land or parcel of land within the county, whether within a subdivision or
outside a subdivision, except such plants as are grown for use, ornament or the
production of food for human or beast, or for fuel. NOXIOUS WEEDS are declared
to be offensive plants regardless of their height. NOXIOUS WEEDS shall include,
but not be limited to, all noxious weeds described as part of Idaho Code Title
22, Chapter 24, and by this reference, all provisions of the aforementioned
Code are hereby affirmed and made a part of this subchapter. A non-inclusive
list of noxious weeds are attached to the ordinance codified in this subchapter
as Exhibit “A” and by this reference are made a part of this subchapter.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.02 REMOVAL OF WEEDS.
   It shall be unlawful for the owner or the person in control of any land or
parcel of land within the county to allow, permit or suffer weeds or offensive
plants to grow or exist on such land within the county and the existence of
such weeds or offensive plants is declared to constitute a public nuisance. The
owner or person in control of such premises shall destroy and remove such weeds
or plants.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.03 PARKING STRIPS, SIDEWALKS, CURBING, VACANT LOTS AND FIELDS.
   It shall be unlawful for the owner or the person in control of any land
within the county, whether within a subdivision or outside a subdivision, to
allow, permit or suffer weeds or offensive plants to grow or exist on or in
parking strips, sidewalks, curbing, canals or upon vacant lots and fields and
subdivisions.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.04 CITATION TO APPEAR.
   (A)   Whenever it is determined by any police officer that any person has
violated any provision of this subchapter, said police officer may prepare in
quadruplicate, and serve upon the owner or person in control of the land where
the violation exists, a written citation containing a notice to appear in
magistrate court, the name and address of the person charged, the offense
charged, the time and place when and where the offense charged occurred, the
time when the person charged shall appear in magistrate court and such other
pertinent information as may be necessary or advisable.
   (B)   The issuance of such citation shall not be the exclusive method for
enforcement of this subchapter.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.05 FAILURE TO APPEAR.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to fail or neglect to appear, in
response to and as required by such citation, regardless of the disposition of
the charge upon which such citation was originally issued. Appearance in
magistrate court shall be deemed complied with by an appearance by the person
charged or by his or her attorney.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.06 CIVIL REMEDY TO ABATE.
   The County Weed Control Supervisor may notify the owner or person in control
of land where weeds and offensive plants exist to cut, remove and destroy the
same. The notice shall be in writing and shall describe the property where the
nuisance exists and shall specify the time limited for the weeds and plants to
be removed and destroyed. Said notice shall be served personally upon such
owner or person in control if he or she can be found; if such owner or person
in control cannot be found, then the Weed Control Supervisor shall post such
notice in a conspicuous place on the premises and mail a copy thereof to such
owner or person in control by registered mail to his or her last known address.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.07 FAILURE TO COMPLY WITH NOTICE.
   If the owner or person in control of such premises fails to comply with the
notice to abate the weed nuisance, the County Weed Control Supervisor may order
the work done at the expense of the county, and the same shall be charged
against and be a lien against the property until paid. Said lien shall be filed
in the same manner as is provided herein for the filing of special assessments
against the property.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.08 SPECIAL ASSESSMENTS.
   (A)   Notwithstanding the imposition of any criminal fine penalty or
imprisonment, the county may, subject to the provisions of this section, remove
any weeds from any private property within the county and levy a special
assessment against such property for the reasonable costs of such removal.
   (B)   Prior to the removal of such weeds, the county shall give notice in
writing to the owner of such property, which notice shall state the street
address of the property where the weeds exist, and shall describe the nature
and general location of the weeds to be removed. Such notice shall state that
if such weeds are not removed within 15 days from the date the notice is
delivered, the county may cause the same to be removed and a special assessment
made against such property for the costs of such removal. Such notice shall be
personally served upon any occupant, if any, of the property wherein the weeds
are located, and shall be mailed by certified mail, return receipt requested,
postage prepaid, to the owner of the property at the address listed upon the
real property rolls of the county. Such notice shall be deemed to be delivered
upon its physical delivery and deposit into the United States mail, as set
forth above. If the owner or occupant fails to remove the weeds within the time
specified in the notice, the county may order the removal of the weeds and
cause a notice of special assessment for the amount thereof to be mailed to the
owner of the property in the manner set forth above.
   (C)   If the amount of the assessment is not paid within 30 days after
mailing of the notice of special assessment, the same shall be declared
delinquent and may be certified to the County Assessor by the County Clerk, not
later than August 1 of each year. Upon such certification, the assessment shall
be placed upon the tax roll and shall thereafter become a lien against the
property described in the notice and shall be collected in the same manner and
subject to the same penalties as other real property taxes. All monies received
on account of such special assessment shall be held by the County Treasurer in
a special fund to be applied to the payment of the costs of such removal, and
the money shall be used for no other purpose except to reimburse the county for
all amounts expended in removing such weeds.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.09 CIVIL AND CRIMINAL REMEDIES.
   The prosecution of any civil remedy in this subchapter set forth shall not
bar the prosecution of any criminal proceedings herein provided.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994)
§ 91.10 PERMIT FROM COUNTY WEED DEPARTMENT REQUIRED TO DISTURB RIGHT-OF-WAY.
   (A)   Authorization. All persons or entities (permittee), prior to working
in the right-of-way in the county, shall obtain from the County Weed Department
a permit to disturb the ground.
   (B)   Remediation plan. As part of the permitting process, the permittee
shall choose one of the following programs to ensure the proper revegetation or
that proper invasive weed control takes place to maintain proper vegetation
after construction within the ROW.
      (1)   If there is less than 15% of the ROW disturbed, then the permittee
will be assessed a fee of $0.02 per linear foot of disturbed landscape, payable
to County Weed Department, which the County Weed Department will control
unwanted weeds within the disturbed site. This will generally be the case when
utility conduit is placed by boring, ripping, pulling cable or dragline type of
installation.
      (2)   If there is between 15% and 30% of the ROW disturbed, the permittee
will reseed the site by the use of incorporating approved grass seed into or
over the disturbed area. More information on seeding methods, seed types and
where to obtain seed can be found on the County Weed Control website. This will
generally be the case for any open trenching that is performed.
      (3)   If greater than 30% of the ROW is disturbed, the permittee shall
make sure that four inches of top soil exists, or shall import that amount of
top spoil necessary to constitute four inches of top soil, and will then reseed
the site by means of seed drilling (conventional or no-till) or hydroseeding,
or erosion control blankets of the disturbed area utilizing approved grass
seed. More information on seeding methods, seed types and where to obtain seed
can be found on the County Weed Control website or in Appendix I. This will
generally be the case when complete excavation occurs within the ROW.
      (4)   Due to the nature of this project being a curb and gutter or other
special circumstance, this project is exempt from this permitting process.
   (C)   Seeding options. In those circumstances where one of the options in
division (B) above require seeding, the permittee shall incorporate approved
grass seed into or over the disturbed area as required by the County Weed
Department but not by means of less than by dragging a harrow or similar tool
over the soil, topdressing seed with a spreader and then dragging a chain over
the site. This should be followed by a roller to ensure adequate seed to soil
contact. Additional requirements may be seed varieties which are pre-approved
and accepted by the County Weed Department must be used, including non-
irrigated pasture blend of seed should be acceptable, including, but not
limited to, a combinations up to 20 pounds, 80% to 90% germination rate,
noxious weed free combinations of three or more species approved by the county
such as Vavilov II wheatgrass, Siberian wheatgrass, Sodar wheatgrass, Crested
wheatgrass, Idaho fescue or Hard fescue.
   (D)   Violations. Failure of permittee to complete remediation shall result
in no additional permits being granted to permittee to work in rights-of-way
until the remediation has been completed or all costs to the county for
remediation have been paid.
(Ord. 234-19, passed 1-22-2019) Penalty, see §
91.99
GRAFFITI
§ 91.25 FINDINGS AND PURPOSE.
   (A)   (1)   The county finds that graffiti is a public nuisance and
destructive to the rights and values of property owners as well as the entire
community. Unless the county acts to remove graffiti from public and private
property, the graffiti tends to remain, thereby causing other properties to
become the target of graffiti and entire neighborhoods become less livable, all
to the detriment of the county.
      (2)   The county also finds that graffiti leads to other, more violent
crimes and gang-related activity. The county shall be entitled to abate this
nuisance of graffiti pursuant to Idaho Code §§ 52-202 and 52-205.
   (B)   The purpose of this subchapter is:
      (1)   To prevent the spread of graffiti vandalism and to establish a
program for the removal for the removal of graffiti from public and private
property; and
      (2)   To provide additional enforcement tools to protect the public and
private property from acts of graffiti vandalism and defacement.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011)
§ 91.26 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GRAFFITI. Any inscription, word, figure, painting or other defacement that
is written, etched, scratched, sprayed, drawn, painted or engraved upon any
surface of public or private property by any implement, when application of the
mark was not expressly authorized in advance by the owner, occupant or
authorized representative of the owner of the property.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011)
§ 91.27 DEFACEMENT.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to apply graffiti to any natural or
human-made surface that is visible from any public street, alley or sidewalk
within the county.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.28 REMOVAL REQUIRED; NOTICE.
   (A)   Graffiti removal required.
      (1)   The existence of graffiti on public or private property is hereby
declared to be a public nuisance, and persons owning or controlling the
property having graffiti thereon shall be subject to the removal and abatement
provisions specified in this subchapter.
      (2)   The owner or person in control of the premises on which graffiti
has been applied may promptly remove the graffiti after notice is given as
hereinafter set forth. If, after receipt of such notice, The graffiti nuisance
is not abated, the county may proceed to abate the graffiti nuisance in the
manner set forth.
   (B)   Graffiti notice to remove.
      (1)   Whenever graffiti is located on any public or private property or
any surface visible from any public rights-of-way within the county, a notice
shall be issued to the owner or person in control of the premises to abate the
nuisance and remove the graffiti or cover it with paint or other substance
masking the graffiti from public view.
      (2)   Said notice shall be served upon the owner(s) of the affected
premises, as shown on the last property tax assessment rolls of the county, and
upon any known occupant of the premises. Service of the notice may be
accomplished through personal service on the owner, occupant or person in
charge or control of the property, by United States mail, postage prepaid, or
by posting in a conspicuous place upon the premises.
      (3)   Such notice shall be in writing and shall clearly state that the
property has been determined to be in violation of this subchapter and that he
or she owner/occupant’s failure to remove within 48 hours of receipt of the
notice will cause the county declare the property to be a public nuisance; that
the owner or person served may, within 48 hours of receipt of the notice,
deliver in writing to the County Sheriff his or her objections to the removal
of the graffiti and request a hearing before the County Commissioners.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011)
§ 91.29 COUNTY ABATEMENT OPTION.
   (A)   Generally. Nuisances which remain unabated after notice may be
removed, abated or destroyed by the county or its agents, after the following
steps have been taken.
      (1)   If, after delivery of the notice in accordance with this
subchapter, abatement of the nuisance has not occurred and the owner or
occupant has not requested a hearing, the county may remove, abate or destroy
the nuisance.
      (2)   If the property owner requests a hearing to show cause before the
County Commissioners, the hearing shall, if feasible be placed on the agenda on
the next regularly scheduled meeting. The decision of the County Commissioners
shall be final. If the Commissioners require abatement of the nuisance, a ten-
day period shall be given to the property owner after the Commissioner’s
decision so that the property owner shall have additional opportunity to abate
the nuisance or to pursue any legal remedies or defenses at the district court
level.
   (B)   Exceptions to removal. The removal requirements above shall not apply
if the property owner or responsible party can demonstrate that:
      (1)   The property owner or reasonable party can show that if the county
removes the graffiti, it will cause irreparable harm to his or her property;
and/or
      (2)   The property owner or responsible party can show that the alleged
graffiti is not actual graffiti.
   (C)   Right of the county to remove graffiti.
       (1)   Use of public funds. The county may use public funds to abate the
nuisance.
      (2)   Right of entry on private property. If the property owner or
occupant fails to remove the graffiti within the time specified by this
subchapter, or if the county has requested consent to remove or paint over the
graffiti and the property owner or the responsible party has refused consent
for entry for such removal purposes, the county may enter the property and
commence abatement and cost of recovery proceedings for the graffiti removal,
pursuant to Idaho Code §§ 52-202 and 52-205.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011)
§ 91.30 INJURY BY GRAFFITI.
   (A)   No person shall purposely and knowingly vandalize, deface or otherwise
damage the property of another by painting, writing, drawing or otherwise
inscribing thereon in any fashion that which is commonly known as graffiti.
   (B)   For the purpose of this subchapter, GRAFFITI includes any form of
painting, written or inscription regardless of content or the nature of
materials used which is applied to any public or private surface without the
consent of the owner of the property.
(Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011) Penalty, see §
91.99
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see Idaho Code § 18-7036
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
91.01 through
91.09 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be
punished as provided under the definition of misdemeanor and misdemeanor
convictions in state law. Each ten days after a violation of §§
91.01 through
91.09 continues after citation is issued or notice is served as herein provided
shall constitute a separate offense.
   (C)   Any person or entity in violation of §
91.10, shall be guilty of an infraction and, upon conviction, shall be punished
as provided under the definition of an infraction conviction in state law.
   (D)   A violation of any provision of §§
91.25 through
91.30 shall be a misdemeanor. In addition to any punishment specified herein,
the court shall order any violator to make restitution to the victim for any
damages or loss caused directly or indirectly by the violator’s offense in the
amount or manner determined by the court. In the case of a minor, the parents
or legal guardian may be ordered to make restitution.
(Ord. 163-94, passed 5-4-1994; Res. 223-11, passed 5-31-2011; Ord. 234-19,
passed 1-22-2019)
