DOG CONTROL
§ 92.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ANIMAL CONTROL SHELTER. Includes the Idaho Falls Animal Control Shelter and/
or premises deemed necessary for the confinement and care of dogs seized either
under this subchapter or otherwise deemed necessary for confinement and care of
the dog.
   AT LARGE. Any dog off or away from the premises of the owner, keeper or
person harboring a dog, and the dog is not under control of the owner, keeper
or person harboring the dog, or his or her agent or a member of his or her
immediate family, either by leash, cord or chain, or while confined in a
vehicle.
   AT-RISK DOG. As defined in Idaho Code § 25-2810, any dog that without
justified provocation bites a person without causing a serious injury as
defined by state law. For the purpose of this definition, JUSTIFIED PROVOCATION
is defined in Idaho Code § 25-2810(3).
   DANGEROUS DOG. As defined in Idaho Code § 25-2810, any dog that without
justified provocation has inflicted serious injury on a person; or has been
found to be “at risk” and thereafter bites or physically attacks a person
without justified provocation. For the purpose of this definition, JUSTIFIED
PROVOCATION is defined in Idaho Code § 25-2810(3).
   DOG. Either male or female canines.
   DOG KENNEL. Commercial and non-commercial, as herein provided for, and will
be considered a premises where more than two dogs of licensing age are kept or
maintained.
   DOG OF LICENSING AGE. Any dog that attained the age of four months old.
   IMPOUNDED. Having been received into custody of the Animal Control Shelter
or into the custody of an Animal Control Officer/Deputy, County Sheriff’s
Office or any authorized agent or representative thereof.
   MICROCHIP. A small radio transmitter and minute electronic device,
approximately the size of a grain of rice, that contains the dogs
identification number. This identification number will link to a database so as
to contact the owner of the dog.
   OWNER and/or KEEPER. Applies to the proprietorship of a dog and shall mean
any person owning, keeping or harboring a dog.
   UNLICENSED DOG. A dog for which a license has not been issued for the
current year, or to which a tag provided in for in this subchapter is not
attached to a collar on the dog.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.02 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2801, the Board of County Commissioners
requires all the county resident dog owners who reside in unincorporated areas
of the county to identify their dog and pay an annual license fee for each dog
over four months of age.
      (1)   The application or receipt information for an individual dog
license may identify the dog and dog owner in the following way:
         (a)   License tag number and year of issue;
         (b)   Date the license was issued and the fee amount;
         (c)   A dog owner’s name, address and telephone number(s); and
         (d)   Identifying information relating to the dog, such as a dog’s
name, size, gender, color, markings, breed, existing microchip or tattoo and
the like.
   (B)   The annual county dog license fee is valid for the calendar year that
appears on the license, from January l to December 31 of that year.
      (1)   Resident dog owners who move into an unincorporated area of the
county must obtain an appropriate annual county individual dog or kennel
license at the time the residence is occupied, and a dog or dogs are kept on
the property and/or premises.
      (2)   A numbered dog tag for an individual licensed dog can be issued
after a license application is approved and the license fee is paid to the
County Assessor or an approved vendor.
   (C)   Use of a license tag for a dog other than the dog described in the
receipt may be deemed a failure to properly license infraction. A violation of
this division (C) is an infraction punishable as provided in Idaho Code § 18-
113A.
   (D)   All dogs over the age of four months must be vaccinated for rabies,
and a copy of the vaccination certificate must be presented with the license
application. A suitable certificate can be a copy of invoice showing the rabies
vaccination from a licensed veterinarian.
   (E)   While all dogs must be appropriately licensed, the annual individual
dog license fee may be exempted and waived for:
      (1)   Dogs that are owned by a government entity for public service in
law enforcement, search and rescue or other qualified purposes;
      (2)   Dogs that qualify as a “service animal” or “service animal in
training” as defined in the Americans With Disabilities Act, being 42 U.S.C. §
12101. A SERVICE ANIMAL is any dog that is individually trained to do work or
perform tasks for the benefit of an individual with a disability, including a
physical, sensory, psychiatric, intellectual or other mental disability. Other
species of animals, whether wild or domestic, trained or untrained, are not
considered SERVICE ANIMALS; and
      (3)   Qualify as an agricultural and/or livestock working dog. Dog owners
who have a current agricultural exemption will also have their working dog
license fees waived. The exemption will be for working dogs, i.e., cattle,
sheep and other livestock only, and not for household pets.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.03 LICENSE FEES.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2802, the Board of County Commissioners
authorizes the County Assessor, government entities or private vendors to
collect the individual dog license fee from resident dog owners. Dog licenses
are valid for the calendar year, from January 1 to December 31 of the given
year. The annual individual dog license fee is as follows:
      (1)   Neutered or spayed animal: $3;
      (2)   Unaltered animal: $5; and
      (3)   Replacement tags: $1.
   (B)   (1)   In the event that a dog is impounded, there will be additional
fees of microchipping, impounding and boarding fees and vaccination fees to
include rabies prior to redemption of the dog by the owner.
      (2)   See §
92.07 for more information.
      (3)   In the event that the dog is current on vaccination, proof of
vaccination will be required prior to redemption of the dog by the owner.
   (C)   A county resident dog owner’s receipt showing a paid license fee for
an individual dog or kennel license and/or dog tag for the current calendar
year is required to demonstrate compliance with this section.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.04 TAGS TO BE PLACED ON COLLAR.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2803, no dog shall be permitted to go at large
within the county without having a collar about its neck with a license tag or
disc attached to the collar bearing the number of the license issued by the
county or some municipality within the county. A violation of this section is
an infraction punishable as provided in Idaho Code § 18-113A.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.05 RECORD OF LICENSE.
   (A)   The County Assessor shall keep a suitable record of the dog licenses
issued under the provisions of this subchapter and shall record all the facts
required to be stated in the license.
   (B)   Duplicate records of dog licenses issued shall be properly maintained
by the Animal Control Director at the Animal Control Shelter.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.06 UNLAWFUL FOR DOGS TO RUN AT LARGE.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2805, the County Sheriff intends to
enforce laws and ordinances in unincorporated areas of the county, or in cities
that contract with the Sheriff for law enforcement services that pertain to
dogs, to include dogs that are not on the owner’s property, are not under
control or at large in the county.
   (B)   All dogs found to be running at large are declared to be a public
nuisance and it will be the duty of all sworn law enforcement and/or authorized
Animal Control deputies to capture and impound the dog(s). All dogs will be
taken to the Idaho Falls Animal Control Shelter unless other arrangements are
needed for the care of the dog. (See § 92.07 for more information.)
   (C)   (1)   To the extent a licensed dog owner’s telephone number and/or
address is available from a current dog license tag, tag application, microchip
or other means, or a deputy has reasonable cause to believe that an unlicensed
dog is kept on a premises that is believed to be at large, a deputy will make a
reasonable attempt to serve a copy of the following or similar notice upon the
dog owner, or property owner, or adult aged occupant.
 
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2805, a County Sheriff’s deputy has reasonable cause
to believe that one or more of your dogs, and/or one or more dogs kept at this
premises, was or were at large in an area of the county. Any future complaint or
observance concerning one or more of the dogs running at large, may result in
citations that include, but may not be limited to, a “dog at large” citation.
 
      (2)   The notice shall contain the following information:
         (a)   Incident number with date and time;
         (b)   Dog description and area that the dog was at large;
         (c)   Dog/premises owner or occupant’s name and address; and
         (d)   Deputy and contact information.
      (3)   Delivery of said notice shall be by personal service; or left at
premises and sent by certified mail; or sent by certified mail.
   (D)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2805, after a complaint has been made, and
a notice has been served, any future complaints that any person by this person
who willfully or negligently permits any dog owned, possessed or harbored by
them to run at large; or willfully or negligently fails, neglects or refuses to
keep such dog securely confined within the limits of their premises when not
under the immediate care and control of a competent person, may be guilty of an
infraction.
   (E)   A violation of this section is an infraction punishable as provided in
Idaho Code § 18-113A.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.07 IMPOUNDMENT OF DOGS.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2803, it is the responsibility of the
dog’s owner, and/or property owner, to ensure that the dog is properly
controlled and wears a collar about its neck with a current and valid dog
license tag attached. It is the duty of all sworn law enforcement and/or
authorized Animal Control deputies to capture and take up and impound said dog.
All dogs will be taken to the Idaho Falls Animal Control Shelter unless other
arrangements are needed for the care of the dog.
   (B)   Terms of the impoundment, redemption, sale or other disposal of such
dogs will be as follows.
      (1)   Unlicensed dogs. All dogs not licensed or otherwise identified
found to be running at large shall be captured, taken up and impounded at the
Idaho Falls Animal Control Shelter (or other location that may be deemed
necessary for the welfare of the dog). The dog will be retained at the Animal
Control Shelter for a minimum of three business days, excluding weekends and
holidays. At any time during said three-day period, the owner or keeper of such
dog may redeem the dog by procuring a license, as in this subchapter provided,
a microchip identification and by paying impound and boarding fees as set forth
by the Animal Control Shelter.
      (2)   Licensed dogs. All licensed or otherwise identified dogs with a tag
or microchip found running at large shall be captured, taken up and impounded
at the Idaho Falls Animal Control Shelter (or other location that may be deemed
necessary for the welfare of the dog). The dog will be retained at the Animal
Control Shelter for a minimum of five days, excluding weekends and holidays;
and after the owner is notified of the impoundment. As soon as reasonably
possible following impoundment of a properly identified dog, the Animal Shelter
Director shall keep a record of the date, time and manner of giving notice,
with respect to each impounded dog. At any time during said five-day period,
the owner or keeper of such dog may redeem the dog by procuring a license as in
this subchapter provided, a microchip identification and by paying impound and
boarding fees as set forth by the Animal Control Shelter. The five-day period
shall commence to run at 12:00 p.m. on the following date such notice is given.
      (3)   Microchipping. No dog shall be released to a person redeeming an
impounded dog from the Idaho Falls Animal Shelter unless the dog is currently
licensed and microchipped and wearing a collar and license tag.
      (4)   Unaltered dogs. An unaltered (not neutered or spayed) that has been
impounded at the Animal Control Shelter on two previous occasions during the
previous one year shall be required to be spayed or neutered prior to the owner
redeeming the animal. The accrued impound and boarding fees shall be applied
towards the cost of spay or neuter of the dog.
         (a)   The Board of County Commissioners may contract or enter into
mutual aid agreements with government or private impound facilities to enforce
laws, ordinances and impoundment of dogs.
         (b)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2804, county residents who redeem or
recover a dog from a government or private impound facility are subject to and
responsible to pay the county and/or the impound facility in U.S. currency any
and all license fees, pertinent county fine and all costs associated with an
impounded dogs release.
      (5)   Recovery. A dog owner bears sole responsibility to identify and
recover an impounded dog. The Sheriff or his or her authorized representative
will make reasonable effort to notify the owner of an impounded dog when an
owner’s identity can be reasonably be determined.
      (6)   Unclaimed dogs at Animal Control Shelter. After the time elapsed as
stated in this subchapter, excluding weekends and holidays, from the time a dog
was taken up and impounded, if the owner has not claimed the dog by personally
contacting the Animal Control Shelter personnel, the dog will be deemed
abandoned and the owner relinquishes all property rights to the dog.
         (a)   Any dog not redeemed within five days may be adopted or sold for
the best price attainable, excluding weekends and holidays from the time the
dog was taken up and impounded; if the owner, who was not identified; or was
unavailable for contact; or did not respond to any attempted contact; and after
any type of contact was made, failed to recover the dog from the impound.
         (b)   In any case, the dog may be humanely destroyed by euthanasia and
the carcass disposed of in any lawful manner. A dog may be humanely destroyed
of without waiting for five days in order to prevent a dog’s apparent continued
suffering to injury, neglect, abuse or illness.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.08 RABIES VACCINATION REQUIRED.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for the owner of any dog in the county to not
have a current rabies vaccination. Vaccinations must begin when the animal has
reached the age of four months of age. Every owner, upon request of a Deputy or
Animal Control Officer, produce such proof of vaccination.
   (B)   (1)   If a dog has bitten, scratched or otherwise attacked a person,
the owner of such dog or any person having knowledge of such incident shall
immediately notify the County Sheriff’s Office and the Health Department. The
Animal Control Shelter Director shall have authority to require the owner of
any dog which has bitten any person so as to cause an abrasion of the skin, or
showing signs of rabies, to subject such dog to impoundment at the regular
daily impoundment fee, or to a practicing veterinarian for quarantine enclosure
from which the dog cannot escape and is which not accessible to the public and
approved by the Animal Control Deputy/Officer; and shall be kept for a minimum
period of ten days. If the dog shall be determined to be free of rabies, the
dog shall be returned to the owner after applicable impoundment and licensing
fees are paid. If the impoundment fee is not paid, the dog can be subject to
disposal under the conditions of impoundment herein provided.
      (2)   Further, it shall be the duty of all practicing veterinarians to
report to the Health Department all cases of rabies or suspected rabies with
which they come into contact or to which their attention has been directed.
      (3)   No person shall kill or cause to be killed, any rabid dog, any dog
suspected of having been exposed to rabies, or any dog biting a human, except
here in provided, nor remove same from the county without written permission
from the Health Department Officer.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.09 COMMERCIAL AND NONCOMMERCIAL KENNEL LICENSE.
   (A)   Commercial kennel license.
      (1)   State law provides that dog owners who keep their dogs in a kennel,
covering 15 licensed dogs for breeding or commercial purposes shall be entitled
to a kennel license. This subchapter provides the kennel requirements and
restrictions for all kennels housing four or more dogs and their dependent
young.
      (2)   More than one kennel license may be purchased from the County
Assessor’s Office for commercial boarding kennels that house or board more than
15 dogs that are not owned by the kennel or property owner.
      (3)   The county enforcement and zoning ordinances restrict kennel
locations as follows.
         (a)   Kennels are currently allowed in Professional Business (PB)
Zone; Limited Neighborhood Commercial (LNC) Zone; Neighborhood Shopping Center
(SC-1) Zone; Limited Commercial (C-1) Zone; Limited Business (HC-1) Zone;
General Commercial (C-2) Zone; Manufacturing (M-1 and M-1A) Zones; Industrial
and Manufacturing (I and M-1, 2 and 3 Zones); or may be allowed in Agricultural
(A-1) and Grazing (G-1) Zones after a final decision from a use hearing of the
County Planning Board.
         (b)   Residents who choose to apply for a kennel license must attach a
copy of a valid zoning permit to the approved dog kennel license application
before the Assessor’s Office can issue a kennel license.
         (c)   An annual kennel license fee covering up to 15 dogs is $75. The
kennel owner is entitled to individual license tags for up to 15 kenneled dogs.
A kenneled dog must wear a collar and attached license tags whenever the dog is
outside the confines of the kenneled property.
      (4)   Kennel owners who intermittently board more than 15 licensed dogs
that are not owned by the kennel or property owner or property manager must
attach a valid zoning permit to the kennel license application to cover all
boarded dogs.
         (a)   Annual commercial boarding kennel license fee for intermittently
boarding more than 15 licensed dogs that belong to separate owners is $75 for
each separate authorized licensed covering up to 15 boarded dogs.
         (b)   Generally, individual county dog license tags are not issued
with boarding kennel license. Boarded dogs are generally licensed by their
individual owners.
      (5)   For the purpose of this subchapter, animal hospitals or clinics
operated with an accredited veterinary practice, government approved impound
facilities or retail pet stores are not considered dog kennels. The above
mentioned facilities may be governed by separate zoning ordinances.
   (B)   Non-commercial kennel licensing.
      (1)   To promote public health and safety, the maximum number of
individual licensed dogs that can be kept or boarded on the same premises,
property or mobile carrier in the following designated zoned areas shall be:
         (a)   Two licensed dogs and their dependent young in a Residential (R-
1), R-2, R-3 Zones; Residence Park (RP) Zone; Manufactured Home or Travel
Trailer Park (MH) Zone; Recreational- Forestry (RF) Zone; Recreational-
Agricultural (RA-1) Zone;
         (b)   Three dogs and their dependent young in a Residential-
Agricultural (RA-2, 3 and 4) Zones and Agricultural (A-1) and Grazing (G-1);
and
         (c)   A dog kennel that has four or more dogs and their dependent
young, covering up to 15 dogs shall refer to commercial zoning areas.
      (2)   A dog kennel that has four or more dogs and their dependent young,
covering up to 15 dogs, may be allowed in the Agricultural (A-1) Zone or
Grazing (G-1) Zone if the County Planning and Zoning Board approves all aspects
related to the kennel after a use hearing.
      (3)   Applicants for a dog or kennel license may view a current county
zoned map by contacting the County Planning and Zoning Office in order to
determine the number of dogs and/or kennel location that are or is allowed in a
particular area.
      (4)   Residents who possess or possessed a valid county 2017 non-
commercial kennel license may purchase and maintain individual dog licenses for
the 2018 and subsequent annual license years, but only for the natural life of
each dog that resided on the designated kennel premises, whether through
breeding or not, or movement of the dogs to a different premises will require
the dog or premises owner to comply with kennel requirements defined in this or
applicable zoning ordinances.
      (5)   Pursuant to Idaho Code Title 25, Chapter 28, the Board of County
Commissioners authorizes the County Assessor, government entities or private
vendors to collect the annual individual dog license fee from resident dog
owners. Kennel license applications must be submitted to and reviewed by the
County Assessor’s Office. Collected fees will be deposited into the General
Fund except to allow an agency or entity or authorized representative by the
Board of County Commissioners as a vendor for county annual dog licenses, may
keep $1 to help offset administrative costs.
      (6)   Resident dog owners and/or, if applicable, property, premises or
land owners, must pay any and all dog or kennel related fines, infraction
penalties, impound or zoning ordinance related costs and proof of rabies
vaccination before the Assessor or authorized representative can issue an
individual or kennel dog license.
      (7)   License applications will be considered pending until a final
administrative finding or legal disposition is issued concerning an applicant’s
appeal for a license denial or revocation.
         (a)   Zoning appeals must adhere to the county zoning appeal process.
         (b)   License appeals to the Board of County Commissioners must adhere
to the State Administrative Procedure Act, Idaho Code Title 67, Chapter 52.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.10 CRUELTY PROHIBITED.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-3504, the County Sheriff intends to enforce the
laws in unincorporated areas of the county or within cities that contract with
the Sheriff for law enforcement services concerning the cruelty to animals.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.11 AT-RISK AND DANGEROUS DOGS.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2810, the County Sheriff intends to
enforce the laws in unincorporated areas of the county or within cities that
contract with the Sheriff for law enforcement services concerning dogs that
bite or injure a person.
   (B)   The County Sheriff intends to serve and enforce court orders that
relate to law, administrative regulation or ordinance pursuant to Idaho Code §
25-2811.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.12 DOGS KILLING, WORRYING OR WOUNDING LIVESTOCK AND/OR POULTRY.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 25-2806, the County Sheriff intends to conduct a
reasonable investigation concerning any dog that is reported to have killed,
worried or wounded any livestock or poultry.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.13 COUNTY AUTHORITY.
   (A)   Nothing in this subchapter is intended to enlarge or limit the
authority of the Sheriff or Prosecuting Attorney of the county and their
respective deputies from enforcing laws and ordinances anywhere in the county.
   (B)   Nothing in this subchapter is intended to prohibit or limit the
Sheriff or Prosecuting Attorney of the county from enforcing and/or prosecuting
any violation, under the ordinance or Idaho Code section that the Sheriff and/
or Prosecuting Attorney deem appropriate in any situation.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   (1)   Idaho Code violation penalties and civil remedies are found in
individual statutes with regards to misdemeanor and felony crimes that may
include, but are not limited to:
         (a)   Idaho Code § 25-2810: at-risk and dangerous dogs;
         (b)   Idaho Code § 25-3504: committing cruelty to animals; and
         (c)   Idaho Code § 25-2806: liability for livestock and poultry killed
by dogs.
      (2)   For purposes of this chapter, and pursuant to Idaho Code § 18-113A,
the Board of County Commissioners authorizes infraction penalties, based upon
reasonable cause, for violations of the following provisions of the Idaho Code
and/or this chapter from current:
         (a)   State statute;
         (b)   Applicable court discretion;
         (c)   State Supreme Court infraction fee schedule; and/or
         (d)   The Board of County Commissioners infraction fees.
      (3)   Each complaint, ordinance violation and/or occurrence supported by
reasonable cause may be enforced separately with regards to an infraction fine
for:
         (a)   Failure to control dog;
         (b)   Dog or dogs running at large;
         (c)   Nuisance, a dog’s noise, damage, molestation or trespass;
         (d)   Failure to properly license a dog;
         (e)   Failure to properly license a kennel;
         (f)   Exceeding maximum number of dogs in a kennel;
         (g)   Exceed maximum number of dogs in a zoned area; and
         (h)   Failure to obtain a current rabies vaccination.
      (4)   The fine will be for all other infractions pursuant to Idaho Code §
18-113A as set by State Supreme Court infraction fee schedule.
(Ord. 232-17, passed 12-15-2017)
CHAPTER 93: FIRE PREVENTION AND PROTECTION
Section
Fireworks
   
93.01   Definitions
   
93.02   Permits
   
93.03   Procedures
   
93.04   Compliance
 
   
93.99   Penalty
FIREWORKS
§ 93.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   AGRICULTURAL AND WILDLIFE FIREWORKS. Includes any of the following:
      (1)   Fireworks designated or used to prevent damage to crops; and
      (2)   Fireworks designated or used to prevent unwanted occupancy of areas
by animals or birds through the employment of sound, light or both.
   DANGEROUS FIREWORKS. Includes any of the following:
      (1)   Firecrackers, cannon crackers, giant crackers, salutes, silver tube
salutes, cherry bombs, mines, ground bombardment, grass-hoppers and other
explosive articles of similar nature;
      (2)   Blank cartridges;
      (3)   Skyrockets and rockets, including all similar devices employing any
combustible or explosive material and which rise in the air during discharge;
      (4)   Roman candles, including all devices which discharge balls of fire
into the air;
      (5)   Chasers and whistles, including all devices which dart or travel
about the surface of the ground during discharge;
      (6)   Snakes and hats containing bichloride of mercury;
      (7)   Sparklers more than ten inches in length or one-quarter inch in
diameter or mace with other than iron wires;
      (8)   All articles for pyrotechnic display, such as serial shells,
salutes, flash shells, sky battles, parachute shells, mines, Dago bombs and
similar devices;
      (9)   All torpedoes which explode by means of friction or which contain
arsenic, and all other similar fireworks devices, including cracker balls; and
      (10)   Fire balloons of any type which have burning material of any kind
attached thereto.
   FIRE NUISANCE. Anything or any act which increases, or may cause an increase
of, the hazard or menace of fire to a greater degree than customarily
recognized as normal by persons in the public service of preventing,
suppressing or extinguishing fire; or which may obstruct, delay or hinder, or
may become the cause of any obstruction, delay or a hindrance to the prevention
or extinguishment of fire.
   FIREWORKS.
      (1)   Includes blank cartridges, toy pistols, toy cannons, toy canes or
toy guns in which explosives are used, fire balloons (balloons of a type which
have burning material of any kind attached thereto or which requires fire
underneath to propel them), firecrackers, torpedoes, skyrockets, rockets, Roman
candles, fountains, wheels, Dago bombs, sparklers and other fireworks of like
construction and any fireworks containing any combustible or explosive
substances for the purpose of producing a visible or audible effect by
combustion, deflagration, explosion or detonation.
       (2)   Exempted from this definition are all toy pistols, toy cannons,
toy canes and toy guns, and similar devices such as party poppers or party
favors in which paper caps containing not more than 0.25 grain of explosive
compound per cap are used and such caps whether single, roll or tape type.
   PACKAGE. Any case, container or receptacle used for holding fireworks, which
shall be closed or sealed by tape, cordage or by any other secure means.
    PERSON. Includes any individual, firm, partnership, joint adventure,
association, concern, corporation, estate, trust, business trust, receiver,
syndicate or any other group or combination acting as a unit.
   PUBLIC DISPLAY OF FIREWORKS. An entertainment feature where the public is
admitted or permitted to view the display or discharge of dangerous fireworks.
   SAFE AND SANE FIREWORKS. Includes any of the following:
      (1)   Cone fountains with pyrotechnic composition not exceeding 50 grams
each;
      (2)   Cylindrical fountains, whether base, spike or handle, with
pyrotechnic composition not exceeding 75 grams each and inside tube diameter
not exceeding three-quarters inch;
      (3)   Sparklers and dipped sticks not more than ten inches in length or
one-quarter inch in diameter, made on steel or iron wire and Suzuki and Morning
Glories with pyrotechnic composition not exceeding four grams each;
      (4)   Snakes which do not contain bichloride of mercury and pyrotechnic
composition not exceeding two grams each;
      (5)   Wheels with pyrotechnic composition not exceeding 60 grains for
each driver unit or 240 grains for each complete wheel. The inside tube
diameter of driver unit shall not exceed one-half inch; and
      (6)   Whistles, without report and which do not dart or travel about the
ground during discharge, with pyrotechnic composition not exceeding six grams
and containing no picric or gallic acid.
(Ord. 134-83, passed 10-12-1983)
§ 93.02 PERMITS.
   (A)   Dangerous fireworks permit. It shall be unlawful for any person in the
county to import, export, offer for sale, sell, possess, keep or store or
permit the keeping or storing of any dangerous fireworks for any use or
purpose, except that a person holding a dangerous fireworks display permit
issued pursuant to the terms and conditions of this subchapter may use
“dangerous fireworks” for a safely supervised and conducted public display of
fireworks. Said fireworks may be stored for a period not exceeding ten days
immediately preceding the date of said public display, provided the fireworks
are to be used exclusively for public display and are stored in accordance with
Uniform Building Code requirements.
   (B)   Safe and sane fireworks sales permit. No person without having a valid
“safe and sane fireworks sales permit” issued pursuant to terms and conditions
set forth in this subchapter shall import, export, possess for the purpose of
sale, offer for sale or sell “safe and sane fireworks” for any use or purpose.
   (C)   Agricultural and wildlife fireworks. A permit shall not be required
for the sale, use and storage of agricultural and wildlife fireworks, as long
as such use is by persons actively engaged in farming or livestock raising.
Said use and storage shall take place only in areas zoned agricultural or
rural. Persons contemplating the use of agricultural and wildlife fireworks
should notify the appropriate fire district, forest fire prevention district
and/or Sheriff’s Department in advance of such use.
   (D)   Exception. This subchapter does not apply to a state fireworks
manufacturer, importer or wholesaler as to that portion of product inventories
which are designated and intended only for shipment directly out of the state.
(Ord. 134-83, passed 10-12-1983) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.03 PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Dangerous fireworks display permit. Any reputable person over 21 years
of age, in reasonable pursuit or furtherance of any legitimate personal
business or charitable purpose desiring to make a public display of dangerous
fireworks, shall first make written application to the Building Official for a
dangerous fireworks display permit. Each applicant shall pay to the Building
Official a fee of $35 per site at the time he or she files his or her
application. This fee is non-refundable and is to defray costs of
investigations required hereunder.
   (B)   Safe and sane fireworks sales permit. Any reputable person over 21
years of age, in reasonable pursuit or furtherance of any legitimate personal,
business or charitable purpose, desiring to engage in the sale of safe and sane
fireworks within the county, shall first make written application to the
Building Official for a safe and sane fireworks sales permit. Each applicant
shall pay to the Building Official a fee of $35 at the time he or she files his
or her application, which shall not be refunded in any event. The fee is to
defray costs of investigations required hereunder.
   (C)   Form of application. Each applicant for a safe and sane fireworks
sales permit or a dangerous fireworks display permit shall file his or her
application with the Building Official. Each application shall show the
following:
      (1)   Name and address of applicant;
      (2)   Whether or not the applicant is a private, profit-making
corporation, a private non-profit corporation, a sole proprietorship or a
partnership;
      (3)   The names and addresses of the officers, trustees and/or directors,
if any, of the applicant;
      (4)   The location where the applicant requests permission to sell safe
and sane fireworks or display dangerous fireworks together with a legal
description of the property and proof of ownership or right of use;
      (5)   Date of incorporation or date partnership was established or the
applicant’s age, if a sole proprietorship;
      (6)   The location of the applicant’s principal and permanent meeting
place or places or principal place or places of business;
      (7)   The applicant’s state sales tax permit number, except if
application is for dangerous fireworks permit;
      (8)   If the applicant is an entity other than a sole proprietorship, the
name and general description of the business activities of each parent or
subsidiary company, business or entity and a general description of the
ownership organization of each parent or subsidiary, if any;
      (9)   Such other information as the Building Official may require on a
standard form submitted by all applicants and which is reasonably necessary to
protect the public health, safety and morals; and
      (10)   Proof of issuance of a policy of public liability insurance with
coverage of at least $100,000/$1,000,000 and a policy of property damage
insurance with at least $100,000 or a certificate showing said amount of
insurance. Each policy shall contain two riders therein listing and carrying
the county as an additional assured under said policy.
   (D)   Pre-installation investigation.
      (1)   The Building Official, upon receipt of an application for either a
safe and sane fireworks sales permit or a dangerous fireworks display permit,
shall assign an application (permit) number. The Building Official shall then
promptly notify the Fire Chief of the appropriate fire district or the County
Sheriff’s Department if no fire district exists at the location proposed for
sales or display. The Building Official and the Fire Chief or their designated
representatives shall arrange a mutually convenient time to review the
application and to visit the proposed location. If the application is for a
safe and sane fireworks sales permit, the Building Official shall ascertain
that the proposed location is in an area zoned commercial.
      (2)   After meeting with the Fire Chief, the Building Official will
prepare a written report of their findings, together with a recommendation for
approval, approval with conditions or disapproval of the application. Factors
to be considered in the report include compatibility with the Uniform Building
Code and/or Uniform Fire Code, compatibility with the requirements and
standards of this subchapter and the character or repute of the applicant. This
report shall be forwarded to the Board of Commissioners within ten working days
of the receipt of the application. The Board of Commissioners shall then
approve, conditionally approve or deny the application.
   (E)   Installation inspection.
      (1)   Safe and sane fireworks sales permits shall not be valid unless and
until the Fire Chief or his or her designated representative have visited the
site and verified in writing to the Board of Commissioners that the
installation meets the requirements of this subchapter and the permit. This
site visit must take place after the temporary fireworks stand is in place, but
before sales commence.
      (2)   The safe and sane fireworks sales permit number must be posted on
the temporary fireworks stand in a conspicuous place. Letters shall be a
minimum of four inches in height.
    (F)   Term of permit. A safe and sane fireworks sales permit or a dangerous
fireworks display permit issued pursuant to this subchapter shall be valid only
within the calendar year in which issued. A permit shall be valid only for the
specific premises or location designated in the permit. However, subject to
reasonable conditions necessary for protection of public health, safety and
morals, an applicant may be granted permits for more than one premises or
location within the county. No permit shall be transferable or assignable.
   (G)   Time of filing application. Each application for a safe and sane
fireworks sales permit will be filed with the Clerk on or before June 15 of the
calendar year for which the permit is sought. Each application for a dangerous
fireworks display permit will be filed with the Clerk before June 1 of the
calendar year for which the permit is sought.
   (H)   Time of sale. No safe and sane fireworks shall be sold or offered for
sale except from 12:00 p.m. on June 15 to 12:00 a.m. on July 5 of each year. No
temporary fireworks stands will be erected before June 10 of the calendar year.
   (I)   Distribution of fees. Of the $35 collected, $10 shall be considered
the permit fee, as defined by Idaho Code § 39-2604, and shall be credited to
the Clerk and Recorder. The remaining $25 shall be considered a site review and
inspection fee, with $10 credited to the Building Department and $15 credited
to the appropriate Fire District Fund.
(Ord. 134-83, passed 10-12-1983)
§ 93.04 COMPLIANCE.
   It shall be the duty of every person issued a fireworks sales permit to
comply with all the provisions of the State Fireworks Act, being Idaho Code
Title 39, Chapter 26, and this subchapter. Violations of the Act or any of the
provisions of this subchapter by the permittee, or by any of its agents,
employees or officers shall constitute a cause, in and of itself, to deny any
subsequent application for a permit. It shall be unlawful for any person, firm
or corporation to violate any of the provisions of this subchapter.
(Ord. 134-83, passed 10-12-1983) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any violation of §
93.01 through
93.04 shall constitute a misdemeanor.
(Ord. 134-83, passed 10-12-1983)
CHAPTER 94: ALARM SYSTEMS
Section
   
94.01   Title
   
94.02   Purpose and scope
   
94.03   Definitions
   
94.04   Alarm users permits required
   
94.05   Automatic dialing device; certain interconnections prohibited
   
94.06   False alarms and permit revocation
   
94.07   Confidentiality and statistics
 
   
94.99   Penalty
§ 94.01 TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known as “The Burglary and Robbery Alarm Ordinance.”
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)
§ 94.02 PURPOSE AND SCOPE.
   (A)   The purpose of this chapter is to protect the emergency services of
the county from misuse.
   (B)   This chapter governs burglary and robbery alarm systems, requires
permits, establishes fees, provides for revocation of permits, provides for
punishment of violations and establishes a system of administration.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)
§ 94.03 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ALARM BUSINESS. The business by an individual, partnership, corporation or
other entity of selling, leasing, maintaining, servicing, repairing, altering,
replacing, moving or installing any alarm system or causing to be sold, leased,
maintained, serviced, repaired, altered, replaced, moved or installed any alarm
system in or on any building, structure or facility.
   ALARM SYSTEM. Any assembly of equipment, mechanical or electrical, arranged
to signal the occurrence of an illegal entry or other activity requiring urgent
attention and to which police are expected to respond.
   ALARM USER. The person, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company
or organization of any kind in control of any building, structure or facility
wherein an alarm system is maintained.
   AUTOMATIC DIALING DEVICE. A device which is interconnected to a telephone
line and is programmed to select a predetermined telephone number and transmit
by voice message or code signal an emergency message indicating a need for
emergency response.
   BURGLARY ALARM SYSTEM. An alarm system signaling an entry or attempted entry
into the area protected by the system.
   COUNTY CLERK. The individual authorized to issue permits.
   FALSE ALARM. An alarm signal, eliciting a response by an officer when a
situation requiring a response by an officer does not in fact exist, but does
not include an alarm signal caused by violent conditions of nature or other
extraordinary circumstances not reasonably subject to control by the alarm
business operator or alarm user.
   INTERCONNECT. To connect an alarm system, including an automatic dialing
device, to a telephone line, either directly or through a mechanical device
that utilized a telephone, for the purpose of using the telephone line to
transmit a message upon the activation of the alarm system.
   PRIMARY TRUNK LINE. A telephone line serving the public safety
communications that is designated to receive emergency calls.
   PUBLIC SAFETY COMMUNICATIONS. The city/county facility used to receive
emergency and general information from the public to be dispatched to the
respective police departments utilizing communications.
   ROBBERY ALARM SYSTEM. An alarm system signaling a robbery or attempted
robbery.
   SHERIFF. The Sheriff of Bonneville County, Idaho Falls, Idaho, or his or her
designated representative.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)
§ 94.04 ALARM USERS PERMITS REQUIRED.
   (A)   Every alarm user shall obtain an alarm user’s permit for each system
from the County Clerk’s Office upon the effective date of this chapter or prior
to use of an alarm system. Users of systems using both robbery and burglary
alarm capabilities shall obtain permits. Application for a burglary or robbery
alarm user’s permit and an $8 fee shall be filed with the County Clerk’s Office
each year. Each permit shall bear the signature of the Sheriff and be for a
one-year period. The permit shall be physically upon the premises using the
alarm system and shall be available for inspection by the Sheriff.
   (B)   (1)   A revoked user’s permit shall be obtained from the County
Clerk’s Office by filing an application and paying a fee as follows.
 
First revoked user’s permit in the permit year                   $40
Second revoked user’s permit in the permit year                  $100
Third and each additional revoked user’s permit in the permit yea$180
 
      (2)   The payment of any revoked user permit fee shall not extend the
term of the original permit period. Each permit shall bear the signature of the
Sheriff and shall bear the same expiration date as the revoked permit. The
permit shall be physically upon the premises using the alarm system and shall
be available for inspection by the Sheriff.
   (C)   If a residential alarm user is over the age of 65, and is the primary
resident of the residence, and if no business is conducted in the residence, a
user’s permit may be obtained from the County Clerk’s Office according to
division (A) above without the payment of a fee.
   (D)   A $25 charge will be charged in addition to the fee provided in
division (A) above to a user who fails to obtain a permit within 60 days after
the effective date of this chapter, or who is more than 15 days delinquent in
renewing a permit, or any new system operating without first obtaining a permit
after the effective date of this chapter.
   (E)   An alarm user required by federal, state, county or municipal statute,
regulation, rule or ordinance to install, maintain and operate an alarm system
shall be subject to this chapter; provided:
      (1)   A permit shall be designated a special alarm user’s permit;
      (2)   A special alarm user’s permit for a system which has four or more
false alarms in a permit year shall not be subject to revocation under §
94.06, but the holder of the permit shall pay a fee of $100 with the submission
of the report required by §
94.06;
      (3)   For each false alarm over four in a permit year, upon written
demand therefor by the Sheriff, the holder of a special user’s permit shall pay
a fee of $25; and
      (4)   The payment of any fee provided for in divisions (E)(2) and (E)(3)
above shall not be deemed to extend the term of the permit.
   (F)   An alarm user which is a governmental political unit shall be subject
to this chapter; but a permit shall be issued without payment of a fee and
shall not be subject to revocation, payment of additional fees or the
imposition of any penalty provided herein.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)
§ 94.05 AUTOMATIC DIALING DEVICE; CERTAIN INTERCONNECTIONS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person to program an automatic dialing device
to select a primary trunk line; and it is unlawful for an alarm user to fail to
disconnect or reprogram an automatic dialing device which is programmed to
select a primary trunk line within 12 hours of receipt of written notice from
the Sheriff that it is so programmed.
    (B)   Within 60 days after the effective date of this chapter, all existing
automatic dialing devices programmed to select a primary trunk line shall be
reprogrammed or disconnected.
   (C)   It is unlawful for any person to program an automatic dialing device
to select any telephone line assigned to the county other than one so
authorized; and it is unlawful for an alarm user to fail to disconnect or
reprogram such device within 12 hours of receipt of written notice from the
Sheriff that an automatic dialing device is so programmed.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.06 FALSE ALARMS AND PERMIT REVOCATION.
   (A)   Any alarm system which has four or more false alarms within a permit
year shall be subject to permit revocation as provided herein.
   (B)   If the Sheriff records four or more false alarms within a permit year
for any alarm system:
      (1)   The Sheriff shall notify the alarm user by certified mail of such
fact and direct that the user submit a report to the Sheriff within ten days of
receipt of the notice describing actions taken or to be taken to discover and
eliminate the cause of the false alarms;
      (2)   If the alarm user submits a report as directed, the Sheriff shall
determine if the actions taken or to be taken will prevent the occurrence of
false alarms; if he or she determines that the action will prevent the
occurrence of false alarms, he or she shall notify the alarm user, in writing,
that the permit will not be revoked at that time and that if one more false
alarm occurs within the permit year, the permit will be summarily revoked;
      (3)   If no report is submitted, or if the Sheriff determines that the
actions taken or to be taken will not prevent the occurrence of false alarms,
the Sheriff shall give notice by certified mail to the user that the permit
will be revoked without further notice on the tenth day after the date of the
notice if the user does not file within that period a written request for a
hearing;
      (4)   If a hearing is requested, written notice of the time and place of
the hearing shall be served on the user by the Sheriff by certified mail at
least ten days prior to the date set for the hearing, which date shall not be
more than 21 nor less than ten days after the filing of the request for
hearing;
      (5)   The hearing shall be before the Board of County Commissioners and
the Sheriff; the alarm user shall have the right to present written or oral
evidence, subject to the right of cross-examination. If the Board of County
Commissioners determined that four or more false alarms have occurred in a
permit year, and that the user has not taken actions which will prevent the
occurrence of false alarms, the Board of Commissioners shall issue written
findings to that effect and an order revoking the user’s permit;
      (6)   An alarm user shall immediately discontinue use of the alarm system
upon being notified by certified mail of the revocation of a permit pursuant to
division (B)(3) or (B)(5) above;
      (7)   An alarm user whose permit has been revoked may apply for a revoked
user’s permit as provided in §
94.04(B). The Sheriff shall not be required to issue a revoked user’s permit,
unless he or she is satisfied that the user’s system has been properly serviced
and its deficiencies corrected. The Sheriff may impose reasonable restrictions
and conditions upon the user before issuing a revoked user’s permit, which
restrictions and conditions shall be written on the permit and shall provide
for summary revocation on the occurrence of four false alarms in the permit
year; and
      (8)   In situations permitting summary revocation under divisions (B)(2)
and (B)(7) above, revocations shall be effective on the third day following the
mailing by certified mail by the Sheriff of a notice of revocation. There shall
be no appeal of a summary revocation.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)
§ 94.07 CONFIDENTIALITY AND STATISTICS.
   (A)   All information submitted in compliance with this chapter shall be
held in the strictest confidence and shall be deemed a public record exempt
from disclosure pursuant to state statute; and any violation of confidentiality
shall be deemed a violation of this chapter. The Sheriff shall be charged with
the sole responsibility for the maintenance of all records of any kind
whatsoever under this chapter.
   (B)   Subject to the requirements of confidentiality, the Sheriff shall
develop and maintain statistics having the purpose of assisting alarm system
evaluation for use by members of the public.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.99 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any provisions of this chapter shall be guilty of a
misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, may be punished by a fine of not more
than $300 or by imprisonment for not more than six months, or by both such fine
and imprisonment.
(Ord. 117-80, passed 1-2-1980)

CHAPTER 110: SOLICITING AND PEDDLING
Section
   
110.01   Definitions
   
110.02   License required
   
110.03   Application; bond
   
110.04   Licenses
   
110.05   Orders
   
110.06   Fees
 
   
110.99   Penalty
§ 110.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   PEDDLER.
      (1)   One who goes from place to place and/or from house to house, within
the county, carrying for sale and/or exposing to sale goods, wares and/or
merchandise which he or she carries, whether traveling by foot or other method.
PEDDLER shall include persons who sell and/or offer to sell, goods, wares and/
or merchandise on or alongside any public street, highway or alley, if such
person is not on privately owned land with the consent of the owner.
      (2)   Exempt from the above definition of PEDDLERS are persons selling
newspapers or religious tracts and persons representing charitable
organizations.
   SOLICITORS. Any person who goes from house to house, and/or from place to
place within the county, taking orders for and/ or offering to take orders for
goods, wares or merchandise, or any article for future delivery, or for
services to be performed in the future, or for the making, manufacturing or
repairing of any article or thing whatsoever for future delivery.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
§ 110.02 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to act as a peddler or solicitor within
the meaning and application of this chapter without first securing a license
from the County Clerk.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -) Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.03 APPLICATION; BOND.
   (A)   (1)   Any person desiring a license to engage as a peddler or
solicitor within the county shall make application therefor to the Clerk on
forms to be provided, stating the name and address of the applicant, the name
and address of the firm which he or she represents and the kind of goods
offered for sale or the kinds of services to be performed.
      (2)   The application shall contain a statement to the effect that if a
license is granted, it will not be used or represented in any way as an
endorsement by the county or by any department or officer thereof. Such
application shall be accompanied by a bond in the penal sum of $1,000 executed
by a surety company or by two responsible freeholders residing with the county,
or in lieu thereof, a cash bond or equal amount, conditioned upon the making of
final delivery of the goods ordered or services to be performed, in accordance
with the terms of such order, or failing therein, that the advance payment on
such order be refunded.
      (3)   Any person aggrieved by the action of such peddlers or solicitors
shall have a right of action on the bond for the recovery of money or damages
or both; payment from the bond will be made only upon final judgment against
such peddler or solicitor. Such deposit shall be retained by the county for a
period of 90 days after the expiration of the license, unless sooner released
by order of the Commissioners.
   (B)   The bond required under this section may be waived by the County Clerk
upon proof presented by the applicant that the applicant:
      (1)   Owns real property located, or personal property registered, in the
county;
      (2)   Is a resident of the county; and
      (3)   Has his or her principal place of business in the county.
   (C)   Waiver of the bond required by this section does not in and of itself
waive the requirement to pay a license fee.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
§ 110.04 LICENSES.
   (A)   Licenses issued under this chapter shall bear the name and address of
the person by whom the solicitation is to be made, the date issued and the date
the license expires and the number of the license. Each license shall contain a
statement that the county does not endorse the product or service in any way by
issuing the license.
   (B)   Any license under this chapter shall be non-transferable and shall be
returned to the County Clerk within three days after its date of expiration,
together with any copies thereof.
   (C)   If a person falling within the definition of either divisions (A) or
(B) above has persons working under him or her as solicitors or peddlers, that
person must apply for said license and shall provide to the County Clerk a list
of all subordinate persons. Said list shall be the responsibility of the
applicant and, and any additions or deletions to the list must be brought to
the attention of the County Clerk immediately.
   (D)   A copy of the license issued to the applicant must be given to each
subordinate, and it shall be the responsibility of the applicant to collect all
copies upon the completion of a subordinate’s employment.
   (E)   The license required under this chapter shall be carried on the person
of each solicitor or peddler, and, upon request, it must be shown to all
persons solicited and to any police officer. Before any solicitation at a
residence, the solicitor must present the license for inspection by the
occupant and must also clearly identify the purpose of his or her visit.
   (F)   The license fee required under this chapter may be waived by the
County Clerk upon proof presented by the applicant that said applicant has a
state sales tax number.
   (G)   Waiver of the fee requirement by means of division (F) above does not
in and of itself waive the requirement to post a bond.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
§ 110.05 ORDERS.
   All orders taken by licensed solicitors or peddlers shall be in writing, in
duplicate, stating the terms thereof, and the amount paid in advance, and shall
be signed by the peddler or solicitor showing plainly his or her name and
license number, and one copy shall be given to the purchaser at the time of the
taking of the order.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
§ 110.06 FEES.
   Peddlers or solicitors, as defined by this chapter, shall be subject to the
following license fees, payable to the county.
 
Monthly   $5
Quarterly $12.50
 
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
§ 110.99 PENALTY.
   Any person who violates any of the provisions of this chapter shall be
guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment in the county jail not
exceeding six months, or by a fine not exceeding $300, or by both.
(Ord. 110-78, passed - -)
