GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 111.001 LICENSES; DENIAL, SUSPENSION OR REVOCATION.
   (A)   The County Clerk may deny, suspend or revoke any license issued under
this chapter at any time, if the licensee does not comply with the applicable
provisions of this chapter or other provisions of the county ordinances or of
the Idaho Code. A license issued under this chapter may be denied, suspended or
revoked if any criminal history investigation reveals that the applicant or
licensee does not comply with the applicable provisions or intent of this
chapter, or other provisions of the county ordinances, or the Idaho Code.
   (B)   If a criminal history check reveals that there is a pending criminal
investigation or criminal charge that would result in the revocation or denial
of a business license or other alcohol license, or if there is a conviction or
withheld judgment entered for the charge, the Clerk shall deny the application
until such time as the applicant has been cleared of such charges. An applicant
shall then be allowed the opportunity to reapply after being cleared of said
investigation or criminal charge.
   (C)   The Clerk may revoke or suspend any license issued under this chapter
upon giving ten days’ notice to the license holder. Appeals of the Clerk’s
decision must be filed no later than 14 days after the revocation or suspension
and shall be heard by the Board of County Commissioners. Denials of licenses
issued pursuant to this chapter shall be based on noncompliance to the stated
provisions of this chapter, county ordinances or state law. Failure to appeal
shall be deemed as a final decision of the county. The ruling of the County
Commissioners shall be the final decision of the county.
   (D)   The use of any license issued under the provisions of this chapter for
any business or action for which the license was not issued shall be a
violation of this chapter, and said license shall be subject to immediate
suspension and/or revocation.
   (E)   The State Liquor Dispensary shall be exempt from the purchase of a
liquor license.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
LIQUOR BY THE DRINK
§ 111.015 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BARTENDER. Any person, whether agent, servant, employee or person acting in
any other capacity, who pours, mixes or prepares any liquor by the drink, wine
by the drink or beer by the drink upon any premises licensed to sell alcohol by
the drink. A BARTENDER can only operate within the county when employed by a
business that holds a license as required by state law.
   DIRECTOR. The Director of the State Department of Law Enforcement.
   INTERDICTED PERSON. A person to whom the sale of liquor is prohibited under
the laws of the state.
   LICENSE. A license issued by the Clerk authorizing a licensee to sell liquor
by the drink at retail.
   LICENSEE. The person/business to whom a license to sell and dispense liquor
by the drink is issued under the provisions of this subchapter.
   LIQUOR. Any kind of liquor which may be sold by a state liquor store.
   PREMISES. The building, room or place in which the retail sale of wine by a
licensee is authorized under this subchapter.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.016 LICENSE TO SELL OR DISPENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   No person shall sell or dispense liquor/alcohol by the drink at retail
on any premises in the county without first obtaining all licenses as required
by law.
   (B)   The State Liquor Dispensary shall be exempt from the purchase of a
liquor license.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.017 LICENSE FEE AND EXPIRATION.
   (A)   Each licensee shall pay in advance an annual license fee which shall
be determined by a schedule of fees adopted by the County Commissioners by
resolution which may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and
shall expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on
the license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.018 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE.
   (A)   Each applicant for a license to sell liquor by the drink shall file
with the Clerk an application on a form designated by the Clerk, stating the
following:
      (1)   A copy of a current license issued by the State Director of Law
Enforcement pursuant to the provisions of Idaho Code Title 23, Chapter 9, as
amended;
      (2)   The names and addresses of all persons of contact and responsible
for the operation of the business. This list shall include owner of the
business as well as all managers; and
      (3)   Any other information reasonably necessary for the Clerk to
determine the applicant’s qualifications or disqualifications for a license.
   (B)   If, during the term of any license issued under this subchapter, any
change shall take place in any of the information stated in the application,
the licensee shall deliver a verified report of the change to the Clerk no
later than seven working days after the change occurs.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.019 INVESTIGATION.
   (A)   Upon receipt of an application for a license or for a transfer of a
license under this subchapter, accompanied by the necessary documents and fees,
the Clerk shall review all information in the application. If it is determined
that the contents of the application meet the requirements of this subchapter
and that the applicant is qualified to receive a license, a license shall be
issued or transferred. Otherwise, the application shall be denied and the
license or transfer fee refunded.
   (B)   All applications for a license to sell liquor by the drink or any
transfer or renewal of a license to sell liquor by the drink shall be granted
or denied within 60 days from the date the application was delivered to the
Clerk.
   (C)   If the Clerk denies an application for a license to sell liquor by the
drink or any renewal or transfer of a license to sell liquor by the drink, he
or she shall specify in writing:
      (1)   The statutes, ordinances and standards used in evaluating the
application;
      (2)   The reasons for denial; and
      (3)   The actions, if any, the applicant could take to obtain the
license, transfer or renewal.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.020 ISSUANCE OF LICENSE.
   When the applicant for sale of liquor by the drink has produced evidence as
required by §
111.018 and paid the required license fee, the Clerk shall submit the
application to the County Commissioners within 30 days after the application is
filed. Upon approval of the Commissioners, the Clerk shall issue the license to
the applicant.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.021 FORM OF LICENSES; DISPLAY; LICENSE FOR DESIGNATED ADDRESS ONLY.
   (A)   Every license issued under this subchapter shall state the name of the
person or business entity to whom issued and the location by street and number
or other definite designation of the premises. The license shall be signed by
the licensee and shall be posted on the licensed premises in a place
conspicuous to the public. No person except the named licensee shall exercise
any of the privileges granted under the license. Licenses issued under this
subchapter apply only to premises for which they have been issued.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be pursuant to all
requirements and regulations of the Idaho Code Title 23 and related county
ordinances as they may apply.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.022 QUALIFICATIONS.
   An applicant for a license to sell liquor by the drink shall possess all
qualifications necessary to obtain a license from the Director. Licensees shall
maintain qualifications throughout the period for which their license is
issued. Possession of licenses regularly issued by the Director and the county
shall be prima facie evidence of the applicant’s qualifications and shall be a
requirement for the applicant to possess, prior to receiving a license under
this subchapter.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.023 EXPIRATION OF LICENSES.
   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and shall
expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on the
license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.024 AGE RESTRICTIONS.
   Pursuant to the laws of the Idaho Code Title 23, any amendments and
subsequent new regulations related to age shall apply to the regulations of the
county. The following sections of Idaho Code Title 23 are provided for easy
reference:
   (A)   Idaho Code § 23-312 (Persons Under 21 and Intoxicated Persons -
Inhibited Sales);
   (B)   Idaho Code § 23-943 (Persons Under Specified Ages Forbidden to Enter,
Remain In or Loiter at Certain Licensed Places);
   (C)   Idaho Code § 23-944 (Exceptions from Restriction on Entering or
Remaining); and
   (D)   Idaho Code § 23-949 (Persons Not Allowed to Purchase, Possess, Serve,
Dispense or Consume Beer, Wine or Other Alcoholic Liquor).
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.025 TRANSFER OF LICENSES.
   No license may be transferred to another person, unless the transferee first
obtains approval of the county upon application containing substantially the
same information required by §
111.018. If the proposed transferee is qualified for the license, the Clerk
shall approve the transfer and shall reissue the license in the name of the
transferee. The fee for each license transfer shall be determined by a schedule
of fees adopted by the County Commissioners Council by resolution which may be
amended from time to time.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.026 LOCATION RESTRICTIONS.
   No license shall be issued that does not meet the requirements of Idaho Code
§ 23-913.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.027 RIGHT OF ENTRY.
   Any duly authorized police officer shall have the right at any time to enter
and examine the premises of any licensee to ascertain compliance with the laws
of the state and the county. It shall be unlawful to refuse any police or
designated enforcement officer admittance to the premises for such purpose.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.028 CONDUCT OF BUSINESS.
   Every person licensed under this subchapter to sell liquor by the drink
shall at all times conduct a quiet and well-lighted, orderly place of business.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.029 HOURS OF SALE.
   (A)   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-927, no liquor shall be sold, offered for
sale or given away upon any licensed premises during the following hours:
      (1)   Between 1:00 a.m. on Christmas and 10:00 a.m. of the following day;
and
      (2)   Between 1:00 a.m. and 10:00 a.m. of any day.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to dispense liquor for consumption
on the premises in any place not licensed under the ordinances of the county.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.030 CONSUMPTION ON PREMISES.
   Retailers who do not possess a valid license for the retail sale of liquor
by the drink or wine by the drink shall not permit consumption of wine on the
licensed premises.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.031 LIQUOR CATERING PERMIT.
   (A)   An alcohol beverage catering permit is a permit issued pursuant to
Idaho Code § 23-934A which authorizes the permittee to serve and sell liquor by
the drink, beer and wine, or beer, or wine, at a party or convention. An
alcohol beverage catering permit shall be limited to authorization to sell
liquor or beer or wine, or any combination thereof, based upon the type of
license which the applicant possesses. An applicant is not required to hold a
liquor, beer or wine permit within the county to apply for a catering permit,
but must hold a valid permit within the state. Applications for such permit
shall be submitted to the county who shall verify the type of license held by
the applicant.
   (B)   After determination of qualification of the applicant, the Clerk shall
issue a catering permit following the requirements of any applications required
by the county which shall contain at a minimum, but not limited to, the
following information:
      (1)   The name and address of the applicant and the number of his or her
state liquor license;
      (2)   The dates and hours during which the permit is to be effective, not
to exceed five consecutive days;
      (3)   The names and addresses of the organizations, groups or persons
sponsoring the event; and
      (4)   The address at which the liquor is to be served, and if a public
building, the rooms in which the liquor is to be served.
   (B)   The application shall be verified by the applicant and filed with the
Clerk. A filing fee which shall be determined by a schedule of fees adopted by
the County Commissioners by resolution which may be amended from time to time
shall be paid at the time the application is approved. The fee shall be for
each day the permit is to be effective and shall be non-refundable irrespective
of whether the party or convention is held. Catering permits issued under this
section are issued pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-934A.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
BEER
§ 111.045 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BEER. Any beverage obtained by the alcoholic fermentation of an infusion or
decoction of barley, malt and/or other ingredients in drinkable water and which
contains not more than 6% alcohol by weight.
   BEER BY THE DRINK. A license to sell beer by the individual glass or open
bottle at a retail for consumption on the premises.
   DISTRIBUTOR. A person who is employed by or is an agent of a retailer to
sell, serve or dispense beer.
   LICENSE. A license issued by the Clerk authorizing a licensee to sell beer
at retail.
   LICENSEE. A qualified person to whom a license for the retail sale of beer
is issued under the provisions of this subchapter.
   PREMISES. The building, room or place in which the retail sale of beer by a
licensee is authorized under this subchapter.
   RETAILER. A person to whom a beer license has been issued.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.046 LICENSE TO SELL OR DISPENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   (1)   Except as otherwise provided in this subchapter, no person shall
sell or dispense beer at a retail establishment within the county without first
obtaining a license from the county as required by this subchapter.
      (2)   Any person, whether agent, servant, employee or person acting in
any other capacity, who dispenses beer by the drink upon any premises licensed
to sell beer by the drink, in which said establishment also holds a liquor by
the drink permit, pursuant to this subchapter, must be licensed.
      (3)   A copy of all employees licensed as a bartender shall be kept and
maintained in an orderly fashion. All bartender’s licenses, names of persons
acting as bartenders and hours worked shall be available upon request of any
police or enforcement officer representing the county. It shall be the
responsibility of the business to confirm and maintain required documentation
for all persons acting as bartenders within the business. Failure to maintain
such documentation shall constitute a violation of this subchapter by the
business. Further, the county shall have the authority to revoke the business
license and any county license issued authorizing the sale of liquor, beer or
wine by the drink until compliance is established.
      (4)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be pursuant to all
requirements and regulations of the Idaho Code Title 23 and related county
ordinances, as they may apply.
   (B)   The State Liquor Dispensary shall be exempt from the purchase of a
beer license.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.047 LICENSE FEES AND EXPIRATION.
   (A)   License fees for the retail sale of beer in the county shall be
determined by a schedule of fees adopted by the County Commission by resolution
which may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and
shall expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on
the license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.048 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE.
   Each applicant for a license for the retail sale of beer shall file with the
county an application on a form designated by the Clerk, stating the following:
   (A)   The name and residential address of the applicant;
   (B)   The street address of the premises where beer is to be sold;
   (C)   The name of the owner of the premises for which the license is sought;
   (D)   The applicant, if an individual, is at least 21 years old;
   (E)   The applicant has not been convicted of a felony or any crime
involving moral turpitude;
   (F)   The application shall include a copy of a current license issued by
the state allowing the retail sale of beer;
   (G)   The applicant agrees to abide by the terms and conditions of this
subchapter, and any laws, ordinances, rules or regulations subsequently
promulgated by the state or the county regarding the retail sale of beer within
the county; and
   (H)   Any other information the Clerk requires to determine that the
applicant possesses the qualifications and has none of the disqualifications
for a license, as provided in this subchapter and in Idaho Code §§ 23-1010 and
23-1016, as amended.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.049 INVESTIGATION.
   (A)   Upon receipt of an application for a license or for a transfer of a
license under this subchapter, accompanied by the necessary license or transfer
fee, the Clerk shall investigate all information stated in the application. If
it is determined that the contents of the application are true, that the
applicant is qualified to receive a license, that the premises are suitable for
carrying on the intended business and that the requirements of this subchapter
have been met, a license shall be issued or transferred. Otherwise, the
application shall be denied and the license or transfer fee refunded.
   (B)   All applications for a retail beer license or any transfer or renewal
of a retail beer license shall be granted or denied within 60 days from the
date the application was delivered to the Clerk.
   (C)   If the Clerk denies an application for a retail beer license or any
renewal or transfer of a retail beer license, he or she shall specify in
writing:
      (1)   The statutes, ordinances and standards used in evaluating the
application;
      (2)   The reasons for denial; and
      (3)   The actions, if any, the applicant could take to obtain the
license, transfer or renewal.
   (D)   Appeals of any denial of a license, transfer or renewal of a license
shall be pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-1016(4).
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.050 ISSUANCE OF LICENSE.
   When the applicant for a beer license has produced evidence as required by §
111.048 and paid the required license fee, the Clerk shall submit the
application to the County Commissioners within 30 days after the application is
filed. Upon approval of the Commissioners, the Clerk shall issue the license to
the applicant.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.051 FORM OF LICENSES; DISPLAY; LICENSE FOR DESIGNATED ADDRESS ONLY.
   (A)   Every license issued under this subchapter shall state the name of the
person or business entity to whom issued and the location by street and number
or other definite designation of the premises. The license shall be signed by
the licensee and shall be posted on the licensed premises in a place
conspicuous to the public. A license for the retail sale of beer shall be
granted only for the place designated in the application. The place of business
shall not be changed or moved without the consent of the Commissioners.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be pursuant to all
requirements and regulations of the Idaho Code Title 23 and related county
ordinances, as they may apply.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.052 QUALIFICATIONS.
   (A)   An applicant for a beer license shall possess all qualifications
necessary to obtain a license from the Director. Licensees shall maintain
qualifications throughout the period for which their license is issued.
   (B)   Possession of licenses regularly issued by the Director and the county
shall be prima facie evidence of the applicant’s qualifications and shall be a
requirement for the applicant to possess, prior to receiving a license under
this subchapter.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.053 EXPIRATION OF LICENSES.
   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and shall
expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on the
license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.054 RESTRICTIONS CONCERNING AGE.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-1013, any person who is 21 years of age or older
may sell, serve, possess or dispense beer in the course of his or her
employment, otherwise it shall be unlawful for any person to sell, serve or
dispense beer to or by any person under 21 years of age, proof of which, shall
be a validly issued state, district, territorial, possession, provincial,
national or other equivalent government driver’s license, identification card
or military identification card bearing a photograph and date of birth, or a
valid passport.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.055 TRANSFER OF LICENSE; TRANSFER FEE.
   No license may be transferred to another person, unless the transferee first
obtains approval of the county upon application containing substantially the
same information required by §
111.048. If the proposed transferee is qualified for the license, the Clerk
shall approve the transfer and shall reissue the license in the name of the
transferee. The fee for each license transfer shall be determined by a schedule
of fees adopted by the County Commissioners by resolution which may be amended
from time to time.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.056 LOCATION RESTRICTIONS.
   No license shall be issued that does not meet the requirements of Idaho Code
§ 23-1011B.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.057 RIGHT OF ENTRY.
   Any police officer shall have the right at any time to enter and examine the
premises of any licensee or of any place where beer is sold at retail to
ascertain the alcoholic content of any beer kept for sale on the premises or to
ascertain compliance with the laws of the state and the county. It shall be
unlawful to refuse any police officer admittance to the premises for such
purposes.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.058 CONDUCT OF BUSINESS.
   Every person licensed under this subchapter to sell beer at retail shall at
all times conduct a quiet and well-lighted, orderly place of business.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.059 HOURS OF SALE.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-1012, it shall be unlawful for any person in any
place licensed to sell beer by the drink or beer for consumption on the
premises, whether for pleasure or profit, to sell or dispense beer for
consumption on the premises or to permit the consumption of beer on the
premises between the following hours:
   (A)   1:00 a.m. and 6:00 a.m. of any day; and
   (B)   1:00 a.m. on Christmas and 6:00 a.m. of the day following such day.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
WINE
§ 111.070 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BARTENDER. Any person, whether agent, servant, employee or person acting in
any other capacity, who pours, mixes or prepares any liquor by the drink, wine
by the drink or beer by the drink upon any premises licensed to sell alcohol by
the drink. A BARTENDER can only operate within the county when employed by a
business that holds a license as required in this subchapter or other county
ordinances.
   DIRECTOR. The Director of the State Department of Law Enforcement.
   DISTRIBUTOR. A person who is employed by or is an agent of a retailer to
sell, serve or dispense.
   LICENSE. A license issued by the Clerk authorizing a licensee to sell wine
at retail.
   LICENSEE. A qualified person to whom a license for the retail sale of wine
is issued under the provisions of this subchapter.
   PREMISES. The building, room or place in which the retail sale of wine by a
licensee is authorized under this subchapter.
   RETAILER. A person to whom a retail wine license or wine by the drink
license has been issued.
   RETAIL WINE LICENSE. A license issued by the Director authorizing a person
to sell wine at retail for consumption off the licensed premises. The term also
means a license issued by the county authorizing a person to sell wine at
retail for consumption off the licensed premised only.
   WINE. Any alcoholic beverage containing not more than 14% alcohol by volume
obtained by the fermentation of the natural sugar content of fruits or other
agricultural products containing sugar, whether or not other ingredients are
added.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.071 LICENSE TO SELL OR DISPENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   (1)   Except as otherwise provided by this subchapter, no person shall
sell wine at retail for consumption off the premises or by the individual glass
or open bottle for consumption on the premises within the county, without first
obtaining a license under this subchapter. A person who holds a valid current
wine-by the-drink license issued by the county may sell on the licensed
premises wine at retail for consumption off the premises without obtaining a
retail wine license from the county.
      (2)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be pursuant to all
requirements and regulations of the Idaho Code Title 23 and related ordinances
of the county.
   (B)   The State Liquor Dispensary shall be exempt from the purchase a wine
license.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.072 LICENSE FEES AND EXPIRATION.
   (A)   License fees for the retail sale of wine in the county shall be paid
in advance as determined by a schedule of fees adopted by the County
Commissioners by resolution which may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and
shall expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on
the license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.073 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE.
   Each applicant for a license for the retail sale of wine shall file with the
Clerk an application on a form designated by the Clerk, stating the following:
   (A)   The name and residential address of the applicant;
   (B)   The street address of the premises where wine is to be sold;
   (C)   The name of the owner of the premises for which the license is sought;
   (D)   The applicant, if an individual, is at least 21 years old;
   (E)   The applicant has not been convicted of a felony or any crime
involving moral turpitude;
   (F)   The application shall include a copy of a current license issued by
the state allowing the retail sale of wine;
   (G)   The applicant agrees to abide by the terms and conditions of this
subchapter, and any laws, ordinances, rules or regulations subsequently
promulgated by the state or the county; and
   (H)   Any other information the Clerk requires to determine that the
applicant possesses the qualifications and has none of the disqualifications
for a license, as provided in this subchapter and in Idaho Code Title 23, as
amended.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.074 INVESTIGATION.
   (A)   Upon receipt of an application for a license or for a transfer of a
license under this subchapter, accompanied by the necessary license or transfer
fee, the Clerk shall investigate all information stated in the application and,
if it is determined that the contents of the application are true, that the
applicant is qualified to receive a license, that the premises are suitable for
carrying on the intended business and that the requirements of this subchapter
have been met, a license shall be issued or transferred. Otherwise, the
application shall be denied and the license or transfer fee refunded.
   (B)   All applications for a retail wine license or any transfer or renewal
of a retail wine license shall be granted or denied within 60 days from the
date the application was delivered to the Clerk.
   (C)   If the Clerk denies an application for a retail wine license, wine by
the drink or any renewal or transfer of a retail wine license, the Clerk shall
specify in writing:
      (1)   The statutes, ordinances and standards used in evaluating the
application;
      (2)   The reasons for denial; and
      (3)   The actions, if any, the applicant could take to obtain the
license, transfer or renewal.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.075 ISSUANCE OF LICENSE.
   When the applicant for a retail wine license or wine by the drink license
has produced evidence as required by §
111.073 and paid the required license fee, the Clerk shall submit the
application to the County Commissioners within 30 days after the application is
filed. Upon approval of the Commissioners, the Clerk shall issue the license to
the applicant.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.076 FORM OF LICENSES; DISPLAY; LICENSE FOR DESIGNATED ADDRESS ONLY.
   (A)   (1)   Every license issued under this subchapter shall state the name
of the person or business entity to whom issued and the location by street and
number or other definite designation of the premises.
      (2)   The license shall be signed by the licensee and shall be posted on
the licensed premises in a place conspicuous to the public.
      (3)   A license for the retail sale of beer shall be granted only for the
place designated in the application.
      (4)   The place of business shall not be changed or moved without the
consent the Commission.
   (B)   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be pursuant to all
requirements and regulations of the Idaho Code Title 23 and related county
ordinances as they may apply.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.077 QUALIFICATIONS.
   An applicant for a retail wine license or wine by the drink license shall
possess all qualifications necessary to obtain a license from the Director.
Licensees shall maintain qualifications throughout the period for which their
license is issued. Possession of licenses regularly issued by the Director and
the county shall be prima facie evidence of the applicant’s qualifications and
shall be a requirement for the applicant to possess, prior to receiving a
license under this subchapter.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.078 EXPIRATION OF LICENSES.
   All licenses issued under this subchapter shall be paid in advance and shall
expire one year from the date of issue unless another date is endorsed on the
license by the Clerk.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.079 RESTRICTIONS CONCERNING AGE.
   (A)   Pursuant to the laws of Idaho Code Title 23, any amendments and
subsequent new regulations related to age shall apply to the regulations of the
county.
   (B)   The following Idaho Code Title 23, chapters and sections are provided
for easy reference:
      (1)   Idaho Code § 23-312 (Persons Under 21 and Intoxicated Persons -
Inhibited Sales);
      (2)   Idaho Code § 23-943 (Persons Under Specified Ages Forbidden to
Enter, Remain In or Loiter at Certain Licensed Places);
      (3)   Idaho Code § 23-944 (Exceptions from Restriction on Entering or
Remaining); and
      (4)   Idaho Code § 23-949 (Persons Not Allowed to Purchase, Possess,
Serve, Dispense or Consume Beer, Wine or Other Alcoholic Liquor).
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.080 TRANSFER OF LICENSE; TRANSFER FEE.
   (A)   The procedure for the transfer of a retail wine license or a wine by
the drink license shall be the same as provided in Idaho Code § 23-1317, as
amended, upon application to the Clerk.
   (B)   The fee for transfer of a retail wine license or wine by the drink
license shall be determined by a schedule of fees adopted by the County
Commissioners by resolution which may be amended from time to time.
   (C)   The license of the transferring licensee shall be surrendered to the
Clerk before such transfer may be made.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.081 LOCATION RESTRICTIONS.
   Any license issued must meet the zoning requirements pursuant to Idaho Code
§ 23-1307A.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.082 RIGHT OF ENTRY.
   (A)   Any police officer shall have the right at any time to enter and
examine the premises of any licensee or of any place where wine is sold at
retail to ascertain the alcoholic content of any wine kept for sale on the
premises or to ascertain compliance with the laws of the state and the county.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful to refuse any police officer admittance to the
premises for such purposes.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.083 CONDUCT OF BUSINESS.
   Every person licensed under this subchapter to sell wine shall at all times
conduct a quiet and well-lighted, orderly place of business.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)
§ 111.084 HOURS OF SALE.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person in any place licensed to sell wine
by the drink or wine for consumption on the premises, whether for pleasure or
for profit, to sell or dispense wine for consumption on the premises or to
permit the consumption of wine on the premises during the following hours:
      (1)   1:00 a.m. and 6:00 a.m. of any day; and
      (2)   1:00 a.m. on Christmas and 6:00 a.m. of the day following such day.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to dispense wine for consumption
on the premises, in any place not licensed as an eating place under the zoning
requirements of the county.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.085 CONSUMPTION ON PREMISES.
   Retailers who do not possess a valid license for the retail sale of liquor
by the drink or wine by the drink shall not permit consumption of wine on the
licensed premises.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any violation of the provisions of §
111.001 shall be deemed subject to infraction or misdemeanor as specifically
provided for in this chapter and shall be subject to penalties prescribed for
such violations of state law. In addition to the foregoing penalties, the
county may seek an order of injunction from the county court prohibiting the
business of engaging in business within the county. Said business shall be
liable for any court costs and all attorney costs incurred by the county in
relation thereto.
   (B)   Any violation of the provisions of §§
111.015 through
111.031 shall be deemed a misdemeanor pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-947 and shall
be subject to penalties prescribed for such violations under Idaho Code § 18-
113.
   (C)   Any violation of the provisions of §§
111.045 through
111.059 shall be deemed a misdemeanor pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-1020 and
shall be subject to penalties prescribed for such violations under Idaho Code §
18-113 and any ordinances of the county.
   (D)   Any violation of the provisions of §§
111.070 through
111.085 shall be deemed a misdemeanor pursuant to Idaho Code § 23-947 and shall
be subject to penalties prescribed for such violations under Idaho Code § 18-
113 and as allowed by said Idaho Code or any ordinance of the county.
(Ord. 236-19, passed 2-12-2019)

AMBULANCE SERVICE PROVIDERS
§ 112.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   AMBULANCE. Any privately or publicly owned motor vehicle that is
specifically designed or constructed, and equipped, and is intended to be used
for, and is maintained or operated, for the transportation of wounded, injured
or sick patients, which otherwise comply with the provisions of this
subchapter.
   AMBULANCE OPERATOR or AMBULANCE BUSINESS. Anyone engaging in the business of
transporting ill, sick or injured persons, for hire, or who, by advertising,
representation or otherwise, holds himself or herself out as engaging in such
business.
   APPOINTED COMMITTEE. A committee appointed annually by the Board of County
Commissioners and consisting of at least the following members:
      (1)   A licensed physician who is a resident of the county;
      (2)   A representative of an agency performing emergency services. This
representative shall be: The Chief of the Fire or Police Department in the
largest city in the county, or the Chief of the largest rural district Fire
Department in the county, or the County Sheriff; and
      (3)   A citizen who is a resident of the county. This citizen shall
possess current certification in first aid, at least through the advanced level
of the American Red Cross or the equivalent.
   ATTENDANT. A trained and/or qualified individual responsible for the
operation of an ambulance and the care of the patients, whether or not the
attendant also serves as a driver.
   ATTENDANT-DRIVER. An individual who is qualified as an attendant and a
driver.
   DRIVER. An individual who drives an ambulance.
   PATIENT. An individual who is sick, injured or wounded or otherwise
incapacitated or helpless.
   PERSON. An individual, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company
or group of individuals acting together for a common purpose or organization of
any kind, including any governmental agency other than the United States.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.02 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person, either as owner, agent or
otherwise, to furnish, operate, conduct, maintain, advertise or otherwise be
engaged in the business or service of the transportation of patients within the
county, unless he or she holds a current license for an ambulance issued
pursuant to this subchapter.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for an ambulance to be operated, and no person
shall drive, attend or permit it to be operated, within the county, unless the
ambulance shall be under the immediate supervision and direction of a person
who is holding a current, valid license as a driver, attendant and/or
attendant- driver.
   (C)   Provided, however, that no license shall be required for an ambulance,
or the driver, attendant or attendant-driver of an ambulance which:
      (1)   Is operated by the state, the county or other government agency;
      (2)   Is operated from a location or headquarters outside of, and
transports patients within who are picked up beyond the limits of the county;
      (3)   Is operated from a location or headquarters outside the limits of
the county and either:
         (a)   Picks up patients beyond the limits of the county to be
transported to locations within the county and picks up patients within the
county to be transported to locations beyond the limits of the county; or
         (b)   In the case of an emergency where no county based ambulance is
available, picks up patients within the county to be transported to locations
within the county.
    (D)   Provided, however, that no such outside ambulance shall be used to
pick up patients within the county for transportation to locations within the
county, unless the driver, attendant and attendant-driver and the ambulance
operator hold current, valid licenses issued pursuant to this subchapter,
except as provided in division (C)(3) above.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.03 APPLICATION FOR AMBULANCE LICENSE.
   Application for ambulance licenses shall be made upon forms prepared or
prescribed by the Board of Commissioners and shall contain:
   (A)   The name and address of the applicant and the owner of the ambulance;
   (B)   The trade or other fictitious name, if any, under which the applicant
does business and proposes to do business;
   (C)   The training and experience of the applicant in the transportation and
care of patients;
   (D)   A description of each ambulance, including the make, model, year of
manufacture, motor and chassis numbers, current state license number, the
length of time the ambulance has been in use; patient compartment dimensions of
the ambulance, cot and stretcher capacity, list of equipment carried in the
ambulance and design and color scheme of each ambulance;
   (E)   The location and description of the place or places from which it is
intended to operate; and
   (F)   Such other information as the Board of Commissioners shall deem
reasonably necessary.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.04 AMBULANCE LICENSE LIABILITY INSURANCE.
   (A)   An ambulance operator shall at all times indemnify and save harmless
the county from and for any and all losses, claims, actions or judgments for
damages or injury to persons or property and losses and expenses caused or
incurred by the ambulance operator and its drivers, attendants and attendant-
drivers. No ambulance license shall be issued under this subchapter, nor shall
the license be valid after issuance, nor shall any ambulance be operated within
the county unless there is at all times in force and effect insurance coverage
issued by an insurance company licensed to do business in the state for each
and every ambulance owned and/or operated by or for the applicant or licensee,
providing the following minimum limits of liability insurance, in which the
county shall be named as a named insured:
      (1)   For property damage to any one person: $100,000;
      (2)   For property damage in any one occurrence: $200,000;
      (3)   For personal injury to any one person: $250,000; and
      (4)   For personal injuries in any one occurrence: $500,000.
   (B)   The limits of insurance provided above shall not be deemed the
limitation of the applicant’s or licensee’s covenant to indemnify and save and
hold harmless the county, and if the county becomes liable for an amount in
excess of the insurance limits herein provided, the applicant or licensee
covenants and/or any of its agents upon whom duties may be imposed by this
subchapter, agrees to indemnify and save and hold harmless the county from any
and all such losses, claims actions, judgments or expenses for damages or
injury to persons or property.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.05 AMBULANCE EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   The required first aid-patient handling equipment regularly carried in
each ambulance shall be:
      (1)   Elder valve type resuscitator (portable);
      (2)   One bag-type resuscitator;
      (3)   One manual operated aspiration device (portable, minimum of ten-
inch Hg vacuum);
      (4)   One oxygen tank (size D, minimum) with regulator, liter gauge and
mask;
      (5)   One oxygen tank (size D, minimum) spare;
      (6)   One orthopedic stretcher (Robinson, Greene, Ferno-Washington or
equivalent);
      (7)   One extrication back splint or half-backboard;
      (8)   One hare traction splint;
      (9)   Two adult-size arm splints (one of inflatable type);
      (10)   Two adult-size leg splints (one of inflatable type);
      (11)   Two child-size leg splints;
      (12)   Four sandbags (five pounds each);
      (13)   Two emesis basins;
      (14)   One urinal;
      (15)   One bed pan;
      (16)   One “obstetric” kit;
      (17)   Two plastic or rubber cot or stretcher covers;
      (18)   Two autoclaved and wrapped sheets;
      (19)   One blanket for each cot or stretcher;
      (20)   Four changes of sheets, pillow cases and towels;
      (21)   Three pry bars;
      (22)   One hacksaw;
      (23)   One bolt cutter;
      (24)   One metal cutting rescue saw, recommended (K-12 type);
      (25)   One portable hydraulic kit, recommended (Portapower type);
      (26)   An attendant’s bag(s) which shall contain the following:
         (a)   One bandage shears;
         (b)   Two flashlights (one regular and one pencil type);
         (c)   Two adult-size airways;
         (d)   One child-size airway;
         (e)   One infant-size airway;
         (f)   Ten rolls of four-inch autoclaved dressings (Kirlex or
equivalent);
         (g)   Two rolls of one-inch adhesive tape;
         (h)   Two rolls of two-inch adhesive tape;
         (i)   Six tongue depressors (including two padded types);
         (j)   One oxygen face cone, infant-size;
         (k)   Six elastic bandages in assorted widths (Ace type);
         (l)   Three 18-inch pieces of surgical rubber tubing;
         (m)   Ten four-inch by four-inch pressure dressings;
         (n)   One nose, ear and throat syringe;
         (o)   One infant-size bulb syringe;
         (p)   One container (unbreakable), minimum of quart size, of
physiological saline (for irrigation of wounds, eye wash and poison dilution
purposes);
         (q)   Four packages of universal antidote;
         (r)   One curved hemostat;
         (t)   One curved forceps;
         (u)   Two straight hemostats; and
         (v)   Two straight forceps.
   (B)   Each ambulance shall be mobile radio equipped with the radio(s)
operating on a frequency or frequencies authorized by the Federal
Communications Commission. Direct mobile radio communication shall be possible
between each ambulance and:
         (1)   The ambulance dispatcher;
         (2)   The State Police;
         (3)   The County Sheriff’s Office; and
         (4)   The Idaho Falls Police.
   (C)   The appointed committee, prior to the issuance of an ambulance
license, or the renewal thereof, shall inspect each ambulance to determine the
adequacy of equipment carried in each ambulance along with equipment required
by the State Motor Vehicle Code (siren, red lights and the like) and shall
determine that each ambulance is in safe and sound mechanical and operating
condition, including compliance with state vehicle inspection laws. In
determining the adequacy of first aid patient handling equipment, the appointed
committee shall take into consideration, in addition to the list of equipment
specified by this subchapter, the list of minimal equipment currently published
by the American College of Surgeons or its authorized Committee on Trauma. In
addition to the inspections required prior to issuance of an ambulance license,
or the renewal thereof, the appointed committee is authorized to conduct
inspections at other reasonable times.
   (D)   A report of all inspections by the appointed committee must be
promptly submitted to the Board of County Commissioners following each
inspection.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.06 DRIVER’S, ATTENDANT’S AND ATTENDANT-DRIVER’S LICENSE.
   (A)   Applications.
      (1)   Application for driver’s, attendant’s and attendant-driver’s
licenses hereunder shall be made upon forms prepared or prescribed by the Board
of Commissioners. No person shall drive or serve as a driver, attendant or
attendant-driver with an ambulance licensed hereunder without first procuring
the applicable license from the county.
      (2)   Each license application shall contain at least the following:
         (a)   The applicant’s full name, current residence and length of time
he or she has resided in the county;
         (b)   The applicant’s age, marital status and other physical
attributes;
         (c)   Whether he or she has ever been convicted of a felony or a
misdemeanor and, if so, when and for what cause;
         (d)   The applicant’s training and experience in the transportation
and care of patients, whether he or she has previously been licensed as a
driver, chauffeur, attendant or attendant-driver and, if so, when, where and
whether his or her license has ever been revoked or suspended in any
jurisdiction and for what cause;
         (e)   Affidavits of good character from two reputable citizens;
         (f)   Fingerprints and photograph to be taken by the County Sheriff’s
Department; and
         (g)   Such other information as the Board of Commissioners shall deem
reasonably necessary to make a determination of compliance with this
subchapter.
   (B)   Standards.
      (1)   The Board of Commissioners shall, within a reasonable time after
receiving an application as provided for herein, cause such investigation as he
or she deems necessary to be made of the applicant for a driver’s, attendant’s
or attendant-driver’s license.
      (2)   The Board of Commissioners shall issue a license to a driver,
attendant or atten4ant-driver hereunder, valid for a period of one year, unless
earlier suspended, revoked or terminated, when it finds that:
         (a)   The applicant is not addicted to the use-of intoxicating liquors
or narcotics;
         (b)   The applicant is able to speak, read and write the English
language;
         (c)   The applicant has been found by a duly licensed physician, upon
examination attested to on a form provided by the Board of Commissioners, to be
of sound physique, possessing good eyesight (at least correctable to 20-30
vision in the worst eye and also having good peripheral vision, depth
perception and acceptable dark adaptation recovery for night driving) and free
of physical defects or disease which might impair the ability to drive or
attend an ambulance; and
         (d)   The applicant for a driver, attendant or attendant-driver
license has:
 
            1.   A currently valid certificate evidencing successful completion
of American Red Cross first aid training at least through the advanced level;
            2.   Certification of successful completion of a course on rescue-
extrication taught by the Idaho Falls Fire Department; and
            3.   Certification of successful completion of a course on
cardiopulmonary resuscitation taught by an instructor specified by the
appointed committee.
         (e)   The applicant for a driver or attendant-driver license has, in
addition to the above requirements, successfully completed courses on defensive
driving and emergency vehicle driving taught by instructors specified by the
appointed committee, and also holds a currently valid driver’s license from the
state.
      (3)   A license of a driver, attendant or attendant-driver issued
hereunder shall not be assignable or transferable.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.07 OPERATIONAL STANDARDS.
   No ambulance license shall be issued, renewed or continued in force unless
the ambulance operator shall:
   (A)   Have a minimum of three acceptable ambulance units based in the
county. One ambulance unit must be no more than five years old, another unit no
more than ten years old and the third unit no more than 15 years old;
   (B)   Equip each ambulance to conform with the minimal standards,
requirements and regulations provided for herein;
   (C)   Maintain each ambulance in mechanically safe, sound and sanitary
condition at all times;
    (D)   Have a minimum of five licensed drivers, and five licensed
attendants, or ten licensed attendant-drivers or an acceptable combination
thereof, on the ambulance service staff at all times;
   (E)   Have at least two ambulance crews available for prompt response at all
times. Each such crew shall consist of two appropriately licensed personnel;
and
   (F)   Maintain records, on forms specified by the Board of Commissioners, as
follows:
      (1)   Monthly call report;
      (2)   Detailed individual call report;
      (3)   Annual ambulance service report;
      (4)   Daily equipment checkoff list;
      (5)   Vehicle maintenance log;
      (6)   Patient collection/non-collection report; and
      (7)   Other records and reports as may be required.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.08 DUTIES OF THE BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners shall, within a reasonable time after
receiving an application for an ambulance license as provided for herein, cause
such investigation as it deems necessary to be made of the applicant and his or
her proposed operation.
   (B)   The Board of Commissioners shall issue a license hereunder for a
specified ambulance to be valid for a period of one year, unless earlier
suspended, revoked or terminated, when it found that:
      (1)   The appointed committee has caused the ambulance licensed hereunder
to be inspected and found such ambulance vehicle so licensed to be in proper
condition as prescribed by the applicable provisions of this subchapter;
      (2)   The appointed committee has certified that the ambulance equipment
meets the minimal requirements prescribed by this subchapter and by which to
comply with all applicable laws, ordinances and regulations relating to health,
sanitation and safety;
      (3)   The applicant is a reasonable and proper person to conduct and
operate an ambulance business;
      (4)   A sufficient number of duly licensed drivers, attendants and/or
attendant-drivers are employed in such capacities by the ambulance operator;
and
      (5)   All the requirements of this subchapter and all other applicable
laws and ordinances have been met.
   (C)   Prior to the issuance of any ambulance license hereunder, the Board of
Commissioners shall cause to be inspected the vehicles, equipment and premises
designated in each application hereunder to determine compliance with the
standards prescribed by this subchapter, and no license shall be issued by the
County Clerk unless the appointed committee of the county has certified
compliance with the requirements and standards of this subchapter.
   (D)   Subsequent to the issuance of any ambulance license hereunder, the
Board of Commissioners shall cause to be inspected each licensed ambulance or
vehicle and its equipment and premises whenever they deem such inspection to be
necessary. The inspection authorized by this division (D) shall be in addition
to any other safety or motor vehicle inspection required to be made for
ambulance or other motor vehicles under the law or ordinance of the state or
the county.
   (E)   The Board of Commissioners shall determine the need for additional
ambulance operators and shall issue additional licenses only if the existing
ambulance operators fail to meet the needs and requirements of the county.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.09 LICENSE, FEES AND DURATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   All licenses authorized to be issued hereunder shall bear the date of
issue and shall expire on the anniversary date next following the date of
issue, unless sooner suspended, terminated or revoked. The following fees shall
be charged by the County Clerk, which fees shall accompany the appropriate
application:
      (1)   Ambulance license, for each vehicle: $10; and
      (2)   Driver’s, attendant’s and attendant-driver’s licenses: $1.
   (B)   Any license issued pursuant to the provisions of this subchapter may
be renewed at the expiration thereof by filing a proper application, paying the
fee required for such license and provided that the applicant is then qualified
to receive a license, each applicant being required to conform with all the
requirements as upon original licensing.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
§ 112.10 SUSPENSION AND REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   (1)   Any licensee who shall: neglect or refuse to comply with the
provisions of this subchapter, or with the conditions under which any license
shall be issued; or who shall violate any of the provisions of any ordinance of
this county; or who shall violate any provisions of the laws of the state,
shall be subject to have his or her license suspended or revoked.
      (2)   The County Clerk, upon order by the Board of Commissioners, shall
give notice of the suspension or revocation to the holder of any license issued
hereunder, which notice shall set forth specifically the facts supporting the
charges or grounds for which the license was suspended or revoked, and such
notice shall be served on the holder of such license.
      (3)   The notice of suspension or revocation shall designate the time and
place that a hearing will be held before the Board of Commissioners.
      (4)   If the Commissioners find that the basis of either the suspension
or revocation is substantiated, the license shall be suspended or revoked.
      (5)   If such license is suspended, the license and all evidence thereof
shall be surrendered by the holder of such license and shall be held by the
County Clerk until the end of the period of suspension; and in the event the
license is revoked, the holder shall be ineligible to obtain another license
until granted by the Board of Commissioners.
   (B)   Upon notice of suspension or revocation of an ambulance license
hereunder, operations shall cease with such ambulance; upon notice of
suspension or revocation of a driver’s, attendant’s or attendant-driver’s
license, such driver, attendant or attendant-driver shall cease to drive or
attend an ambulance, and no person shall employ or permit such individual to
drive or attend an ambulance.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.11 COMPLIANCE.
   In addition to the provisions of this subchapter, all licenses hereunder
shall comply with all applicable laws, ordinances and regulations now in force
or hereafter enacted governing the operation of motor vehicles upon highways,
streets, alleys and other public ways.
(Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE SERVICE PROVIDERS
§ 112.25 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   APPOINTED COMMITTEE. A committee appointed annually by the Board of County
Commissioners and consisting of at least the following members:
       (1)   A licensed physician who is a resident of the county;
      (2)   A representative of an agency performing emergency services. This
representative shall be the Chief of the Fire or Police Department in the
largest city in the county, or the Chief of the largest rural district Fire
Department in the county or the County Sheriff; and
      (3)   A citizen who is a resident of the county. This citizen shall
possess current certification in first aid, at least through the advanced level
of the American Red Cross or the equivalent.
   ATTENDANT. A trained and/or qualified individual responsible for the
operation of a non-emergency ambulance and the cure of the patients, whether or
not the attendant also serves as a driver.
   ATTENDANT-DRIVER. An individual who is qualified as an attendant and a
driver.
   DRIVER. An individual who drives a non-emergency ambulance.
   NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE. Any privately or publicly owned motor vehicle that
is designed or constructed, and equipped, and is intended to be used for and is
maintained or operated, for the transportation of non-emergency injured or sick
patients, operating within the boundaries of basic life support unit not
normally involved in first run transportation in an emergency situation, which
otherwise comply with the provisions of this subchapter.
   NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE OPERATOR OR BUSINESS. Anyone engaging in the
business of transporting ill, sick or injured persons, for hire, or who, by
advertising, representation or otherwise, holds himself or herself out as
engaging in such business.
   PATIENT. An individual who is sick, injured or wounded or otherwise
incapacitated or helpless.
   PERSON. An individual, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company
or group of individuals acting together for a common purpose or organization of
any kind, including any governmental agency other than the United States.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.26 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person, either as owner, agent or
otherwise, to furnish, operate, conduct, maintain, advertise or otherwise be
engaged in the business or service of the transportation of patients within the
county, unless he or she holds a current license for a non-emergency ambulance
issued pursuant to this subchapter.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for a non-emergency ambulance to be operated,
within the county, unless the non-emergency ambulance shall be under the
immediate supervision and direction of a person who is holding a current, valid
license as a driver, attendant or attendant-driver.
   (C)   (1)   Provided, however, that no license shall be required for a non-
emergency ambulance, or the driver, attendant or attendant-driver of a non-
emergency ambulance which:
         (a)   Is operated by the state, the county or governmental agency;
         (b)   Is operated from a location or headquarters outside of, and
transports patients within who are picked up beyond the limits of the county;
         (c)   Is operated from a location or headquarters outside the limits
of the county and either:
            1.   Picks up patients beyond the limits of the county, to be
transported to locations within the county; or
            2.   Picks up patients within the county to be transported to
locations beyond the limits of the county.
      (2)   However, no such outside ambulance shall be used to pick up
patients within the county for transportation to locations within the county,
unless the driver, attendant and attendant-driver and the ambulance operator
hold current, valid licenses issued pursuant to this subchapter.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.27 APPLICATION FOR NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE LICENSE.
   Application for non-emergency ambulance licenses shall be made upon forms
prepared or prescribed by the Board of Commissioners and shall contain:
   (A)   The name and address of the applicant and the owner of the non-
emergency ambulance;
   (B)   The trade or other fictitious name, if any, under which the applicant
does business and proposes to do business;
   (C)   The training and experience of the applicant in the transportation and
care of patients;
   (D)   A description of each non-emergency ambulance, including the make,
model, year of manufacture, motor and chassis number, current state license
number, the length of time the non-emergency ambulance has been in use, patient
compartment dimensions of the non-emergency ambulance, cot and stretcher
capacity, list of equipment carried in the non-emergency ambulance, design and
color scheme of each non-emergency ambulance;
   (E)   The location and description of the place or places from which it is
intended to operate; and
   (F)   Such other information as the Board of Commissioners shall deem
necessary.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.28 NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE LICENSE LIABILITY INSURANCE.
   (A)   A non-emergency ambulance operator shall at all times indemnify and
save harmless the county from and for any and all losses, claims, actions or
judgments for damages or injury to persons or property and losses and expenses
caused or incurred by the non-emergency ambulance operator and its drivers,
attendants and attendant-drivers. No non-emergency ambulance license shall be
issued under this subchapter, nor shall license be valid after license after
insurance, nor shall any non-emergency ambulance be operated within the county
unless there is at all times in force and effect insurance coverage issued by
an insurance company licensed to do business in the state for each and every
ambulance owned and/or operated by or for the applicant of licensee, providing
the following minimum limits of liability insurance, in which the county shall
be named as a named insured:
      (1)   For property damage to any one person: $100,000;
      (2)   For property damage in any one occurrence: $200,000;
      (3)   For personal injury to any one person: $250,000; and
      (4)   For personal injuries in any one occurrence: $500,000.
   (B)   The limits of insurance provided above shall not be deemed the
limitation of the applicant’s or licensee’s covenant to indemnify and save and
hold harmless the county, and if the county becomes liable for an amount in
excess of the insurance limits herein provided, the applicant or licensee
covenants and or any of. its agents upon whom duties may be imposed by this
subchapter, agrees to indemnify and save and hold harmless the county from any
and all such losses, claims actions, judgments or expenses for damages or
injury to person or property.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.29 NON-EMERGENCY AMBULANCE EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   The required equipment regularly carried in each non-emergency
ambulance shall be those required under Idaho Code § 56-1016, and EMS 2-3300
ambulance standards as promulgated by the State Board of Health and Welfare.
   (B)   The appointed committee, prior to the issuance of a non-emergency
ambulance license, or the renewal thereof, shall inspect each ambulance to
determine the adequacy of equipment carried in each non-emergency ambulance
along with equipment required by the State Motor Vehicle Code (siren, red
lights and the like) and that each non-emergency ambulance is in safe and sound
mechanical and operating condition, including compliance with State Vehicle
Inspection Laws. In determining the adequacy of first aid-patient handling
equipment, the appointed committee shall take into consideration, in addition
to the list of equipment specified by this subchapter, the list of minimal
equipment currently published by the American College of Surgeons or its
authorized Committee on Trauma. In addition to the inspections required prior
to insurance of a non-emergency ambulance license, or the renewal thereof, the
appointed committee is authorized to conduct inspections at other reasonable
times. A report of all inspections by the appointed committee must be property
submitted to the Board of County Commissioners following each inspection.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.30 DRIVER’S, ATTENDANT’S AND ATTENDANT-DRIVER’S LICENSE.
   (A)   Application. Application for driver’s, attendant’s and attendant-
driver’s licenses hereunder shall be made upon forms prepared or prescribed by
the Board of Commissioners. No person shall drive or serve as a driver,
attendant or attendant-driver with a non-emergency ambulance licensed hereunder
without first procuring the applicable license from the county. Each license
application shall contain at least the following:
      (1)   The applicant’s full name, current residence and length of time he
or she has resided in the county;
      (2)   The applicant’s age, marital statutes and other physical
attributes;
      (3)   Whether he or she has ever been convicted of a felony or a
misdemeanor and, if so, when and for what cause;
      (4)   The applicant’s training and experience in the transportation and
care of patients, whether he or she has previously been licensed as a driver,
chauffeur, attendant or attendant-driver and, if so, when, where and whether
his or her license has ever been revoked or suspended in any jurisdiction and
for what cause;
      (5)   Affidavits of good character from two reputable citizens:
      (6)   Fingerprints and photograph to be taken by the County Sheriff’s
Department; and
      (7)   Such other information as the Board of Commissioners shall deem
necessary to make a determination of compliance with this subchapter.
   (B)   Standards.
      (1)   The Board of Commissioners shall, within a reasonable time after
receiving an application as provided for herein, cause such investigation as he
or she deems necessary to be made of the applicant for a driver’s attendant’s
or attendant-driver’s license.
      (2)   The Board of Commissioners shall issue a license to a driver,
attendant or attendant-driver hereunder, valid for a period of one year, unless
earlier suspended, revoked or terminated, when he or she finds that:
         (a)   The applicant is not addicted to the use of intoxicating liquors
or narcotics;
         (b)   The applicant is able to speak, read and write the English
language;
 
         (c)   The applicant has been found by a duly licensed physician, upon
examination attested to on a form provided by the Board of Commissioners, to be
of sound physique, possessing good eyesight (at least correctable to 20-30
vision in the worst eye and also having good peripheral vision, depth
perception and acceptable dark adaptation recovery for night driving) and free
of physical defects or disease which might impair the ability to drive or
attend an ambulance; and
         (d)   The applicant for a driver, attendant or attendant-driver
license has:
            1.   A currently valid certificate evidencing successful completion
of American Red Cross first aid training at least through the advanced level;
            2.   Certification of successful completion of a course on rescue-
extrication taught by the Idaho Falls Fire Department; and
            3.   Certification of successful completion of a course on
cardiopulmonary resuscitation taught by an instructor specified by the
appointed committee.
         (e)   The applicant for a driver, or attendant-driver license has, in
addition to the above requirements, successfully completed courses on defensive
driving and emergency vehicle driving taught by instructors specified by the
appointed committee and also holds a currently valid driver’s license from the
state.
         (f)   A license of a driver, attendant or attendant-driver issued
hereunder shall not be assignable or transferable.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.31 OPERATIONAL STANDARDS.
   No non-emergency ambulance license shall be issued, renewed or continued in
force unless the ambulance operator shall:
   (A)   Equip each ambulance to conform with the minimal standards,
requirements and regulations provided for herein;
   (B)   Maintain each ambulance in mechanically safe, sound condition and
sanitary condition at all times;
   (C)   Have a minimum of one licensed driver, and one licensed attendant, or
two licensed attendant-drivers or an acceptable combination thereof, on the
non-emergency ambulance service staff at all times;
   (D)   Have at least one ambulance crew available for prompt response at all
times. Each such crew shall consist of two appropriately licensed personnel;
and
 
   (E)   Maintain records, on forms specified by the Board of Commissioners, as
follows:
      (1)   Monthly call report;
      (2)   Detailed individual call report;
      (3)   Annual non-emergency ambulance service report;
      (4)   Daily equipment checkoff list;
      (5)   Vehicle maintenance log;
      (6)   Patient collection/non-collection report; and
      (7)   Other records and reports as may be required.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.32 DUTIES OF THE BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners shall, within a reasonable time after
receiving an application for non-emergency ambulance licenses provided for
herein, cause such investigation as he or she deems necessary to be made of the
applicant and his or her proposed operation.
   (B)   The Board of Commissioners shall issue a license hereunder for a
specified non-emergency ambulance to be valid for a period of one year, unless
earlier suspended, revoked or terminated, when they found that:
      (1)   The appointed committee has caused the non-emergency ambulance
licensed hereunder to be inspected and found such non-emergency ambulance
vehicle so licensed to be in proper condition as prescribed by the applicable
provisions of this subchapter;
      (2)   The appointed committee has certified that the non-emergency
ambulance equipment meets the minimal requirements prescribed by this
subchapter and by which to comply with all applicable laws, ordinances and
regulation relating to health, sanitation and safety;
      (3)   The applicant is a responsible and proper person to conduct and
operate a non-emergency ambulance business;
      (4)   A sufficient number of duly licensed drivers, attendants and/or
attendant-drivers are employed in such capacities by the non-emergency
ambulance operator; and
      (5)   All the requirements of this subchapter and all other applicable
laws and ordinances have been met.
    (C)   Prior to the issuance of any non-emergency ambulance license
hereunder, the Board of Commissioners shall cause to be inspected the vehicles,
equipment and premises designated in each application hereunder to determine
compliance with the standards by this subchapter, and no license shall be
issued by the County Clerk unless the appointed committee of the county has
certified compliance with the requirements and standards of this subchapter.
   (D)   Subsequent to the issuance of any non-emergency ambulance license
hereunder, the Board of Commissioners shall cause to be inspected each licensed
non-emergency ambulance or vehicle and its equipment and premises whenever they
deem such inspection to be necessary. The inspection authorized by this
division (D) shall be in addition to any other safety or motor vehicles under
the law or ordinance of the state or the county.
   (E)   The Board of Commissioners shall determine the need for additional
non-emergency ambulance operators and shall issue additional licenses only if
the existing non-emergency ambulance operators fail to meet the needs and
requirements of the county.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.33 LICENSE, FEES AND DURATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   All licenses authorized to be issued hereunder shall bear the date of
issue and shall expire on the anniversary date next following the date of
issue, unless sooner suspended, terminated or revoked.
   (B)   The following fees shall be charged by the County Clerk, which fees
shall accompany the appropriate application:
      (1)   Non-emergency ambulance license for each vehicle: $25. Said license
shall be prominently displayed in the vehicle; and
      (2)   Driver’s, attendant’s and attendant-driver’s licenses: $5. Said
license shall be on the person.
   (C)   Any license issued pursuant to the provisions of this subchapter may
be renewed at the expiration thereof by filing a property application, paying
the fee required for such license and provided that the applicant is the
qualified to receive a license, each applicant being required to conform with
all the requirements as upon original licensing.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.34 SUSPENSION AND REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   Any licensees who shall neglect or refuse to comply with the
provisions of this subchapter, with the conditions under which any license
shall be issued; who shall violate any of the provisions of any ordinance of
this county; or who shall violate any provisions of the laws of the state shall
be subject to have his or her license suspended or revoked.
   (B)   The County Clerk, upon order by the Board of Commissioners, shall give
notice of the suspension or revocation to the holder of any license issued
hereunder, which notice shall set forth specifically the facts supporting the
charges or grounds for which the license was suspended or revoked, and such
notice shall be served on the holder of such license. The notice of suspension
or revocation shall designate the time and place that a hearing will be held
before the Board of Commissioners. If the Commissioners find that the basis of
either the suspension or revocation is substantiated, the license shall be
suspended or revoked. If such license is suspended, the license and all
evidence thereof shall be surrendered by the holder of such license and shall
be held by the County Clerk until the end of the period of suspension; and in
the event the license is revoked, the holder shall be ineligible to obtain
another license until granted by the Board of Commissioners.
   (C)   Upon suspension or revocation of a non-emergency ambulance license
hereunder, operations shall cease with such non-emergency ambulance; upon
suspension or revocation of a driver’s, attendant’s or attendant-driver’s
license, such driver, attendant or attendant-driver shall cease to drive or
attend a non-emergency ambulance, and no person shall employ or permit such
individual to drive or attend a non-911 emergency ambulance.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.35 COMPLIANCE.
   In addition to the provisions of this subchapter, all licensees hereunder
shall comply with all applicable laws, ordinances and regulations now in force
or hereafter enacted governing the operation of motor vehicles upon highways,
streets, alleys and other public ways.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -)
§ 112.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any person violating the provisions of §§
112.01 through
112.11 shall, upon conviction thereof, be punished by a fine not exceeding $300
or imprisonment in the county jail not exceeding 30 days, or both such fine and
imprisonment.
   (C)   Any person violating the provisions of §§
112.25 through
112.35 shall, upon conviction thereof, be punished by a fine not exceeding $300
or imprisonment in the county jail not exceeding 30 days, or both such fine and
imprisonment.
(Ord. 130-83, passed - -; Ord. passed 7-9-1970)
