CHAPTER 130: GENERAL OFFENSES
Section
   
130.01   Communication with inmates
   
130.02   Willful damage or injury of antenna system property prohibited
   
130.03   Firearms and dangerous weapons
 
   
130.99   Penalty
§ 130.01 COMMUNICATION WITH INMATES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to have any communication, whether
verbal, written or otherwise with any inmate of the county jail, not serving in
the capacity of a trustee, except at regularly scheduled jail visiting periods
or at other times with the express prior permission of the County Sheriff or a
Deputy Sheriff.
(Ord. passed 4-13-1971) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 WILLFUL DAMAGE OR INJURY OF ANTENNA SYSTEM PROPERTY PROHIBITED.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to:
      (1)   Without the consent of the owner thereof, willfully destroy, damage
or in any way injure any television community antenna system, also known as a
CATV system, and as cable television system, or its poles, cables, wires,
fixtures, antennas, amplifiers or other apparatus, equipment or appliances;
      (2)   Willfully obstruct, impede or impair the service of any such
system, or any of its lines, or the transmission of television signals
thereover;
      (3)   Without such consent, attach any device or equipment to such
system, or any line, antenna, pole, cable, wire, fixture, amplifier or other
apparatus, instrument, equipment or appliance thereof;
      (4)   Tap or connect, directly or indirectly, by wire or any other means
whatsoever, to or with any cable television system line so as to hear, or see,
or be in a position to hear or see, for any use or purpose whatsoever, any
signal going over said cable television system line, or to make said tapping or
connection for the purpose of receiving or enabling any other person to
receive, any service over said line or lines;
      (5)   Use or attempt to use, in any manner, or for any purpose, any
information so obtained, or communicate the same in any way or for any purpose,
or attempt to so communicate the same; and
      (6)   Aid, agree with, employ or conspire with any person to do or cause
to be done any of the acts hereinbefore set forth.
   (B)   All prosecutions of this section, proof that any of the acts herein
forbidden were done on or about the premises owned or occupied by the defendant
charged with the commission of such offense, or that the defendant unlawfully
received the benefit of the transmission of television signals from any
television community antenna system or cable television system on account of
the commission of such acts, shall be prima facie evidence of the guilt of the
defendant.
(Ord. passed 9-1-1972) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.03 FIREARMS AND DANGEROUS WEAPONS.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this section is to prohibit the possession of
a firearm, other dangerous weapon, explosive or incendiary device in any public
building, owned or operated by the county wherein any elected official, court
of the state or any judicial officer is housed or has, offices, and making such
possession a misdemeanor violation, except in those circumstances where the
individual carrying or possessing the firearm, other dangerous weapon,
explosive or incendiary device is a law enforcement official of the state or a
political subdivision thereof, a district court marshal, or an individual
possessing a valid permit to carry a concealed weapon.
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, all words and phrases
used shall be given their ordinary, commonly understood and accepted meaning.
   (C)   Exceptions. This section does not apply to law enforcement officials
of the state, or a political subdivision thereof, to marshals of the Seventh
Judicial District, or to an individual who possesses a valid permit to carry a
concealed weapon, issued by the County Sheriff.
   (D)   Weapon possession. It shall be unlawful for any person to have in his
or her possession a firearm, other dangerous weapon, explosive or incendiary
device, whether loaded or unloaded, while said person is within any public
building, owned and operated by the county wherein any elected official, court
of the state or any judicial officer is housed or has offices.
(Ord. 150-89, passed 8-31-1989) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Violations of §
130.01 shall be a misdemeanor punishable by a maximum fine and/or sentence of
$300 and six months in the county jail.
   (C)   Any person violating the provisions of §
130.02 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction, shall be fined in
a sum not exceeding $200, and each day that such violation exists shall
constitute a separate offense.
   (D)   Any person convicted of a violation of §
130.03 shall be punished by a fine of not more than $300 or by imprisonment in
the county jail for not more than six months or by both such fine and
imprisonment.
(Ord. passed 4-13-1971; Ord. passed 9-1-1972; Ord. 150-89, passed 8-31-1989)
CHAPTER 131: MINORS
Section
   
131.01   Purpose
   
131.02   Definitions
   
131.03   Minor curfew
   
131.04   Runaways
   
131.05   Parental violation of curfew hours
   
131.06   Violation of curfew hours by business establishments
   
131.07   Jurisdiction
   
131.08   Authority to stop and detain
   
131.09   Encouraging delinquency
   
131.10   Defenses
 
   
131.99   Penalty
§ 131.01 PURPOSE.
   The purposes of this chapter are: to regulate and prohibit runaways; to
regulate and prohibit minors from remaining in public places during curfew
hours; to protect minors from each other and other adult perpetrators of
crimes; to reduce nocturnal juvenile crime and juvenile delinquency; to promote
family responsibility and parental control over minors; and to protect and
promote the public health, peace, safety, welfare and tranquility of the
inhabitants of the county. Each violation of this chapter shall constitute a
separate offense.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997)
§ 131.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CURFEW HOURS. The hours between 1:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m., every day of the
week. Emergency and unforeseen combination of circumstances or the resulting
state that calls for immediate action to prevent, control or minimize serious
bodily injury, death or significant loss of property.
   EMANCIPATED. Any person either 18 years or age or older or, if under the age
of 18, any person who is married or has been married; or any person who is in
active military service; or any person who is both self-supporting and neither
resides with, nor is subject to, parental control.
   EMPLOYMENT ACTIVITY. The performance of any responsibilities of duties
expressly or impliedly required as a condition of employment of a minor.
   ESTABLISHMENT. Any privately owned place of business operated for a profit
to which the public is invited, including, but not limited to, any place of
amusement or entertainment.
   GUARDIAN.
      (1)   A person who, under court order, is the guardian of the person of a
minor;
      (2)   A public or private agency with whom a minor has been placed by
order of a court; or
      (3)   A person to whom a parent or guardian has delegated any of his or
her powers with respect to a minor pursuant to Idaho Code § 15-5-104.
   LEGAL CUSTODIAN. Any person, other than a parent or guardian, having the
legal care, custody or control of a minor.
   MINOR. Any unemancipated person under the age of 18 years.
   OPERATOR. Any individual, firm, association, partnership or corporation
operating, managing or conducting any establishment. The term includes members
or partners of an association or partnership and the officers of a corporation
which owns or operates an establishment.
   PARENT. Any person who is a natural parent, adopted parent or step-parent,
whether the mother, father, step-mother, step-father or both, being referred to
in the singular as PARENT.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any place to which the general public has access, including,
but not limited to, streets, highways, alleys, sidewalks and the common areas
of schools, hospitals, apartment houses, office buildings, transportation
facilities, shopping malls, public parking lots and commercial businesses.
   REMAIN. To linger or stay or to fail to leave premises when requested to do
so by a police officer or the owner, operator or other person in control of an
establishment.
   RUNAWAY. Any minor who is absent from his or her residence without consent
of his or her custodial parent, guardian or legal custodian and has been absent
for such a period of time as to create a well-founded belief on the part of the
custodial parent, guardian or legal custodian that said minor’s health, safety
or well-being is at risk or may reasonably be at risk.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997)
§ 131.03 MINOR CURFEW.
   It shall be unlawful for any minor to be, or remain, in or upon any street,
alley, public ground, public place or any place open to the public, place of
amusement or entertainment, vacant lot or other unsupervised place,
playgrounds, parks, highways, road, public streets or exposed to public use,
during curfew hour (between the hours of 1:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m. every day of
the week). It shall be unlawful for any parent, guardian or legal custodian to
allow a minor to be, or remain in or upon any street, alley, public ground,
public place or any place open to the public, place of amusement or
entertainment, vacant lot or other unsupervised place, playgrounds, parks,
highways, road, public streets or exposed to public use within the county
during the curfew hours (between the hours of 1:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m. each day
of the week.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.04 RUNAWAYS.
   (A)   Generally. It shall be unlawful for any minor residing or located
within the county to be or to remain a runaway.
   (B)   Assist and encourage runaways. It shall be unlawful for any person to
assist, aid, abet or encourage any minor to runaway. A violation of this
section shall be a misdemeanor punishable as provided in §
131.99.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.05 PARENTAL VIOLATION OF CURFEW HOURS.
   Any parent, guardian or legal custodian of a minor who knowingly permits, or
by culpable indifference allows any minor to loiter, idle, wander, stroll or
play in or upon any street, alley, public ground, public place or any place
open to the public, place of amusement or entertainment, vacant lot or other
unsupervised place, playgrounds, parks, highways, road, public streets or
exposed to public use, during curfew hours (between the hours of 1:00 a.m. and
5:00 a.m. every day of the week) shall be guilty of a misdemeanor provided,
however, that the provisions of this chapter do not apply when a minor is
accompanied by his or her parent, guardian or legal custodian. Each violation
of the provisions of this section shall constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.06 VIOLATION OF CURFEW HOURS BY BUSINESS ESTABLISHMENTS.
   Any owner or operator of or any employee or other person in control of an
establishment is guilty of a misdemeanor if he or she knowingly or willingly
allows a minor to be upon the premises of the establishment during the curfew
hours.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.07 JURISDICTION.
   (A)   The violation of this chapter by a minor shall bring such minor within
the purview of the State Juvenile Correction Act.
   (B)   The violation of this chapter by a person not a minor shall subject
such person to the jurisdiction of the district courts of the state.
   (C)   Any peace officer shall have authority to make a juvenile referral for
any violation of this chapter by a minor and to issue a uniform summons and
citation to any other person who commits a violation of this chapter.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.08 AUTHORITY TO STOP AND DETAIN.
   (A)   (1)   Any law enforcement officer shall have the authority to stop and
temporarily detain a minor, or one who appears to be a minor, as herein
defined, during the hours of curfew to obtain the minor’s name, address, age
and parent’s, guardian’s or legal custodian’s name, whenever said officer has
reasonable suspicion that the minor and/or his or her parent, guardian or legal
custodian are in violation of the curfew provisions of this chapter.
      (2)   Upon determining that the minor and/or his or her parent, guardian
or legal custodian are in violation of the curfew provisions herein, the
officer shall direct or deliver such minor to the residence of his or her
parents, guardian or legal custodian; in addition, the officer may issue
warnings, juvenile referrals and citations as provided under this chapter.
Warnings issued shall clearly state the possible penalties which may be imposed
for subsequent violations.
   (B)   Any peace officer shall have authority as provided in Idaho Code § 20-
516 to take into custody any runaway or other status offender.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.09 ENCOURAGING DELINQUENCY.
   Any person who by any act or neglect encourages, aids or causes a minor to
come within the purview of the State Juvenile Correction Act shall be guilty of
a misdemeanor, punishable as provided in §
131.99.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.10 DEFENSES.
   (A)   It is a defense to prosecution under the curfew provisions of this
chapter if the offending minor does prove and establish any of the following,
the burden of proof for establishing the defense being upon the minor:
      (1)   Accompanied by the minor’s parent, guardian or legal custodian;
      (2)   On an errand at the express direction of the minor’s parent,
guardian or legal custodian without any detour or stop;
      (3)   In a motor vehicle involved in interstate travel;
      (4)   Engaged in employment activity or going to an employment activity
or returning to the minor’s place of residence, without any detour or stop;
      (5)   Involved in an emergency;
      (6)   Within the yard or upon the sidewalk abutting the minor’s residence
or abutting the residence of another adult with whom the parent, guardian or
legal custodian of such minor has expressly placed temporary custody of the
minor;
      (7)   Attending an officially-sponsored school, religious or other
recreational, cultural or educational activity supervised by adults and
sponsored by a public entity or a religious, charitable, civil or other similar
organization organized by adults, or going to or returning home from such
activities, without any detour or stop;
       (8)   Exercising First Amendment rights protected by the United States
Constitution or rights protected by Article I, § 9 of the Constitution of the
state; or
      (9)   Is married or has had the disabilities of minority removed in the
manner provided by law.
   (B)   It is a defense to the prosecution of this chapter that the owner,
operator or employee of an establishment asks the minor to leave the premises
and promptly notifies the law enforcement officials that a minor is present on
the premises of the establishment during curfew hours and refuses to leave.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997)
§ 131.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any minor who violates any provision of this chapter shall be
punishable by means provided for under the State Juvenile Correction Act.
   (B)   Any person who shall violate the provisions of this chapter is guilty
of a misdemeanor and is punishable, upon conviction, by a fine not to exceed
$300 and/or six months in the county jail.
(Ord. 174-95, passed 10-31-1995; Ord. 189-97, passed 12-16-1997)

§ 150.01 CODES ADOPTED.
   (A)   The approved editions of the following nationally recognized codes, as
adopted by the state or the State Building Code Board, are adopted as the
Official Building Codes of the county:
      (1)   2018 Edition International Building Code, including all rules
promulgated by the Board to provide equivalency with the provisions of the
American with Disabilities Act accessibility guidelines and the Federal Fair
Housing Act accessibility guidelines;
      (2)   2018 Edition International Residential Code, parts I-VI and IX;
      (3)   2018 Edition International Energy Conservation Code;
      (4)   2018 Edition International Fuel Gas Code;
      (5)   2018 Edition International Mechanical Code;
      (6)   2018 Edition International Fire Code including amendments by State
Fire Marshal and including Appendices B, C, D, E and F;
      (7)   2018 Edition International Existing Building Code; and
      (8)   2018 Edition International Swimming Pool and Spa Code.
   (B)   All amendments made by the state or the State Building Code Board and
the State Fire Marshal’s office are included.
   (C)   Agricultural buildings are exempt from the Building Codes adopted
herein, but shall remain subject to placement requirements established by
zoning regulations. Qualification as an agricultural building will be
determined upon adherence to the definition of agricultural building in § 202
of the IBC and the property on which the building is sited has been determined
by the County Assessor to be land actively devoted to agriculture, per Idaho
Code § 63-604.
(Ord. 239-21, passed 6-23-2021)
§ 150.02 SAFETY ASSESSMENTS OF BUILDINGS AND STRUCTURES.
   (A)   Purpose. This section authorizes posting of standard placards to show
the condition of a structure for continued occupancy after any natural or
human-made disaster. This section further authorizes the County Department of
Planning and Zoning and designated representatives to post the appropriate
placard at each entry point to a building or structure upon completion of a
safety assessment. Once posted, these placards establish authorized occupancy
of buildings and structures until the posting agency authorizes removal of
placards.
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      PLACARDS. The official posting instrument to be used to designate the
condition for continued occupancy. The following are texts of the official
PLACARDS to be used to designated the condition for continued occupancy of
buildings or structures.
         (a)   Inspected: lawful occupancy permitted. This placard will be
posted on any building or structure in which no apparent structural hazard has
been found. This placard is not intended to mean that there is no damage to the
building or structure, only that none is apparent.
         (b)   Restricted use. This placard will be posted on each building or
structure that has been damaged wherein the damage has resulted in some form of
restriction to continued occupancy. The individual who posts this placard will
note in general terms the type of damage encountered and will clearly and
concisely note the restrictions on continued occupancy.
         (c)   Unsafe: do not enter or occupy. This placard will be posted on
each building or structure damaged such that continued occupancy poses a threat
to life safety. Buildings or structures posted with this placard shall not be
entered under any circumstance except as authorized in writing by the posting
authority. Safety assessment teams shall be authorized to enter these buildings
at any time. This placard is not to be used or considered as a demolition
order. The individual who posts this placard will note in general terms the
type of damage encountered.
      SAFETY ASSESSMENT. A visual, nondestructive examination of a building or
structure for the purpose of determining the condition for continued occupancy
following a natural or human-made disaster.
   (C)   Applicability.
      (1)   The provisions of this section are applicable, following each
natural or human-made disaster, to all buildings and structures of all
occupancies within the county, and within cities in the county who have signed
letters of promulgation signifying their acceptance and participation in the
county’s emergency management operations plan.
      (2)   The number of the ordinance codified in this section, the name of
the department conducting the inspection, its address and phone number and the
inspector’s identification number shall be permanently affixed to each placard.
      (3)   Once a placard has been attached to a building or structure, it is
not to be removed, altered or covered until done so by an authorized
representative of the posting authority or upon written notification from the
posting authority.
(Ord. 172-95, passed 8-21-1995)
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   A violation of any provisions of §
150.02 shall be a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine not to exceed $300, or
imprisonment for a period not to exceed six months in the county jail, or both
for each offense. Each day that a violation occurs or continues shall
constitute a separate criminal offense.
(Ord. 172-95, passed 8-21-1995)
CHAPTER 151: MOBILE HOMES
Section
   
151.01   Definition
   
151.02   Ownership and location
   
151.03   Dealer responsibility
   
151.04   Building permit
   
151.05   Assessment sticker
   
151.06   Moving of mobile home
   
151.07   Lien
 
   
151.99   Penalty
§ 151.01 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definition shall apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   MOBILE HOME. A trailer that:
      (1)   Is designed to be transported upon the highways on its own wheels
or other trailers or detachable wheels;
      (2)   Is capable of being used for human habitation, business, commercial
or office purposes; and
      (3)   Is over 40 feet in length, eight feet or more in width and five
feet five inches or more in height from floor to ceiling.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.02 OWNERSHIP AND LOCATION.
   The owner or contract buyer of any mobile home to be located in the county
for a continuous period of 30 days or more in any calendar year shall declare
ownership and location of such mobile home to the County Assessor at any time
during the calendar year in which said mobile is delivered to a location in the
county.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.03 DEALER RESPONSIBILITY.
   Any dealer, upon the sale of a mobile home and delivery of said mobile home
to a location in the county shall declare the same to the County Assessor upon
delivery of said mobile home to a location in the county and said dealer shall
also state the owner, address, length, width, make and year.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.04 BUILDING PERMIT.
   The owner or contract buyer of a mobile home which becomes located in the
county after the enactment of this chapter, for a continuous period of 30 days
or more in any calendar year, shall request the County Planning and Zoning to
issue said mobile home a building permit, of which one copy of that form will
be given to the County Assessor for placement on the tax rolls of the county.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.05 ASSESSMENT STICKER.
   The County Assessor, after having assessed a mobile home located in the
county, shall affix an emblem or sticker, which emblem or sticker shall be
printed and furnished at county cost, on said mobile home, and said emblem or
sticker shall be affixed so that it shall be visible from the public roadway,
or as specified by the owner, and said emblem or sticker shall designate said
mobile home as having been assessed for that year.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.06 MOVING OF MOBILE HOME.
   (A)   Any owner or occupant of a mobile home, or any person or entity
desiring to move said mobile home from a location in the county shall license
the same with the County Assessor, or purchase a trip permit from the port of
entry, Department of Transportation, and pay all current years property taxes
levied against said mobile home before moving the same from the county.
   (B)   Any dealer, person or other entity moving a mobile home from the
county shall determine that the current year taxes have been paid, proof being
stamped receipt from the County Treasurer’s office showing such taxes paid in
full, before moving said mobile home from the county.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.07 LIEN.
   If the owner of any mobile home or any other person required by this chapter
shall fail to make the proper requests to the county as required by this
chapter, the County Assessor shall list and assess such mobile home according
to his or her best judgment and information, and such assessment shall
automatically become a lien upon such mobile home and remain such lien until
the taxes thereon are paid.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
§ 151.99 PENALTY.
   Any owner, contract buyer, dealer or other person or entity who fails to
comply as required by this chapter, or otherwise violates any term of this
chapter, shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be subject to
punishment by a fine of not more than $300, or by imprisonment not to exceed
six months, or by both such fine and imprisonment. In addition, the county may
enforce the provisions of this chapter by any appropriate civil action,
including, but not limited to, injunction, in which event the county shall be
entitled to all costs and expenses, including attorneys’ fees in the
prosecution of such action.
(Ord. 138-85, passed 9-30-1985)
CHAPTER 152: SIGN CODE

152.01   Title

152.13   Blanketing
   
152.14   Signs on public property
   
152.15   Maintenance of signs
   
152.16   Removal of signs
   
152.17   Signs to comply with zoning regulations
   
152.18   Removal of nonconforming signs
   
152.19   Compliance with safety regulations
   
152.20   Erection of signs over public streets or sidewalks
   
152.21   Construction project or land development sign
   
152.22   Sign regulations in zoning districts
   
152.23   Sports field fencing
   
152.24   Electric signs
   
152.25   Height of signs
   
152.26   Electronic message center signs and changeable copy panels
   
152.27   Design
   
152.28   Construction
   
152.29   Projection and clearance
   
152.30   Billboard signs
   
152.31   Signs, zones permitted and controls
   
152.32   Exemptions
 
   
152.99   Penalty
§ 152.01 TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known as the “Sign Code for Bonneville County, Idaho.”
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.02 PURPOSE.
   The County Commissioners hereby find and declare it is necessary to provide
sign regulations in furtherance of regulating the construction, erection,
maintenance, electrification, illumination, type, size, number and location of
signs for the purpose of protecting the health, safety, property and welfare of
the public; improving the neat, clean, orderly and attractive appearance of the
county; providing for the safe erection and maintenance of signs; eliminating
signs that demand, rather than invite, public attention; and providing for sign
needs of special areas, facilities, centers and districts.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.03 ENFORCEMENT AND ADMINISTRATION.
   The Planning and Zoning Department shall be responsible for the enforcement
and administration of this chapter. The Planning and Zoning Department shall
inspect each sign which requires a permit under this chapter and shall require
the proper maintenance of said signs which are subject to the provisions of
this chapter. Upon reasonable notice to a property owner and upon presentation
of proper credentials, an agent of the Planning and Zoning Department may enter
at reasonable times those premises, buildings and structures reasonably
necessary to perform those duties of this chapter.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.04 SIGN PERMITS.
   (A)   Sign permit required. Subject to the provisions of this chapter:
      (1)   No sign shall be erected, re-erected, constructed, altered or
maintained, except as provided by this chapter and a permit for the same has
been issued by the Planning and Zoning Department;
      (2)   A separate permit shall be required for each person or entity
erecting a sign or signs;
      (3)   A separate permit shall be required for each separate sign on a
single supporting structure; and
      (4)   All sign permit requests shall be applied for through the county
online portal and include a complete set of drawings and specifications.
   (B)   Sign permit fees. Before any sign permit will be issued, the permit
and inspection fees shall be paid in full based on bid price and the normal
building permit fee schedule.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.05 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BLANKETING. The partial or complete shutting off of the face of one sign by
another sign.
   BUILDING MURAL. An exterior sign attached to and covering all, or
substantially all, of the vertical wall surface of a commercial or industrial
building or other structure displaying photographic or graphic images, with or
without text, relating to the building or structure, or a permitted use or
occupant of the property on which the sign is located.
   HEIGHT OF SIGN. The distance from the base of the sign at grade to the
highest attached component of the sign.
   MAINTAIN. To service, repair, alter, remodel, re-letter, redecorate or
repaint a sign. It does not include the removal of signs by a licensed wrecking
contractor, structural alterations, cosmetic or style changes or enlargements
of face changes.
   PERSON. A person, association, partnership or corporation, trust or any
other entity recognized at law.
   SIGN. Unless otherwise further specifically defined or qualified herein, a
SIGN shall be a presentation or representation or words, letters, figures,
designs, pictures or colors, publicly displayed so as to give notice relative
to a person, a business, an article of merchandise, a service, an assemblage, a
solicitation or a request for aid. A SIGN shall further include the structure
or framework or any natural object on which any sign is erected or is intended
to be erected or exhibited or which is being used or is intended to be used for
sign purposes.
   SIGN, ANIMATED. A sign depicting action, motion, light or color changes
through electrical or mechanical means. Includes the use of sequential
switching on and off of graphically arranged fluorescent lamps, cathode tubes,
LEDs or incandescent bulbs so as to provide the illusion of movement.
   SIGN, BILLBOARD. An off-premises sign supported by one or more upright
columns or braces in or upon the ground having either a solid background used
for pasting of a sign, a display device capable of presenting three separate
images sequentially by rotating triangular devices, or a programmable display
that has the capability, through light emitting diodes (LED) technology, to
present text or symbolic imagery.
   SIGN, FLASHING. A sign which uses strobe lights, rotary beacons, zip lights,
flashing lights or signs illuminated in such a way they appear to flash.
   SIGN, FREESTANDING. A sign, which is supported by one or more columns,
uprights or braces in or upon the ground.
   SIGN, NON-COMBUSTIBLE. A sign, which is constructed entirely of non-
combustible material.
   SIGN, OFF-PREMISES. Any sign which directs attention to a use, products,
commodity or service not related to the premises on which it is located.
   SIGN, ON-PREMISES. A sign promoting any service, product or business
located, sold or available on the premises upon which the sign is located.
   SIGN, PROJECTING. Any sign attached to a building or structure and extending
perpendicularly from said building or structure.
   SIGN, REVOLVING. A sign which may be interior illuminated, which has
vertical or horizontal rotation of any of its parts by mechanical means which
does not exceed ten rpm.
   SIGN, STRUCTURE. Any structure, which supports any sign as defined in this
chapter.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.06 PASTING AND TACKING PROHIBITED.
   (A)   Unless advertising products sold on premises, no sign shall be pasted
or glued directly on any exterior wall or roof or affixed directly on any
exterior wall or roof by means of any similar adhesive substance.
   (B)   No paper or cloth sign shall be tacked directly on any exterior wall
or roof.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.07 NOISE SIGN PROHIBITED.
   Unless otherwise permitted or provided by other prevailing governing
authority, it shall be unlawful to use in connection with any sign or to use
for advertising purposes any radio, phonograph, whistle, bell or any other
sound or noise making or transmitting device or instrument for the purpose of
commercial advertising.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.08 ANIMATED AND FLASHING SIGNS PROHIBITED.
   Unless otherwise permitted or provided by other prevailing governing
authority, it shall be unlawful to construct or use an animated or flashing
sign within the county.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.09 NONCONFORMING SIGNS.
   Signs in existence on the date of enactment of this chapter which do not
conform to the provisions of this chapter, but which were constructed, erected,
affixed or maintained in compliance with all previous regulations, shall be
regarded as nonconforming signs which may be continued from the effective date
of this chapter, but shall not be structurally altered, relocated or replaced
without immediately being brought into compliance with all the provisions of
this chapter.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.10 VARIANCES.
   (A)   Any person may seek a variance to provisions of this chapter by
following the procedures outlined in
Chapter 156.
   (B)   The Planning and Zoning Commission may grant a variance for signs
regulated by this chapter if it finds that the proposed sign does not pose a
risk to those purposes set forth in §
152.02; or is historically significant to the community.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.11 SIGNS OVER STREETS PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful to erect or maintain any sign over any street or alley,
except as herein expressly provided.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.12 SIGNS NOT TO COVER WINDOWS, DOORS OR SIMILAR OPENINGS.
   No sign shall cover a window, doorway or other opening providing light or
ventilation or exit facilities which are required by the county-adopted edition
of the International Building Code which are deemed to be necessary to give
access to the building, or to afford fire protection in the event of a fire;
provided, however, that flat signs, wall signs, cloth signs and projecting
signs may cover transoms.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.13 BLANKETING.
   To prevent blanketing, no wall sign shall be erected to project over public
property. Projection shall be measured from the facing of the structure to
which the sign is attached and shall be parallel thereto. No outdoor
advertising sign shall be placed in such a manner such as to obscure an
existing sign.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.14 SIGNS ON PUBLIC PROPERTY.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to fasten or attach, paint or place, any
sign, handbill, poster, advertisement or notice of any kind or sort, whether
political or otherwise, or to cause the same to be done in or upon the
curbstone, lamp post, telephone pole, electric light or power pole, hydrant,
bridge or tree, or in or upon any portion of any sidewalk or street. Unless
otherwise permitted, it shall be unlawful to paste, place, paint or attach any
sign defined in this chapter on any building, street or property of the county.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.15 MAINTENANCE OF SIGNS.
   (A)   Every sign shall be maintained in complete operating condition and in
good safety and repair. No broken, bent, cracked, decayed, dented, leaning,
splintered, ripped, torn, twisted or signs in danger of falling shall be
allowed. The landscaped area in which any sign is placed shall be kept free
from weeds, garbage and debris.
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, MAINTENANCE includes the repair of
facades where signs have been removed, the painting, cleaning and repairing of
the sign. MAINTENANCE does not include structural alterations, cosmetic or
style changes or enlargements of face changes. The Planning and Zoning
Department and its agents shall not be liable for the negligence or failure of
the owner, or the person responsible for maintaining any sign, to keep such
sign in good condition.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.16 REMOVAL OF SIGNS.
   (A)   Any person occupying a building or portion of a building, who owns or
maintains a sign in connection therewith, shall, upon vacating the premises, or
discontinuing the business advertised, cause the sign to be removed with the
exception of the structure, framework or natural object unless the same is
deemed to be unsafe.
   (B)   Any person who owns and maintains a sign which is maintained for the
benefit of another person who occupies a building or part of a building whereon
the sign is located shall cause the sign to be removed if the person for whom
the sign is maintained vacates the premises with the exception of the
structure, framework or natural object unless the same is deemed to be unsafe.
Failure of the owner of the sign or of the person responsible for maintaining
same to remove the sign within 30 days after written notice from the Planning
and Zoning Department shall be considered as a violation of this chapter and
shall subject the owner of the sign and the owner of the property to the
penalties herein.
   (C)   If a sign is advantageous to pending sale or lease of premises,
written permission may be granted, to post notice of the same with a blank
background to lease said sign on premises for a period not to exceed 90 days.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.17 SIGNS TO COMPLY WITH ZONING REGULATIONS.
   All signs shall be erected and maintained in full compliance with this
chapter and
Chapter 156.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.18 REMOVAL OF NONCONFORMING SIGNS.
   (A)   Any sign posted, erected or constructed after the enactment of this
chapter that is not in conformance with the provision of this chapter and/or
which was erected or installed without a permit, shall be removed within 30
days upon the sending of written notification by the Planning and Zoning
Department.
   (B)   The provisions of §
152.09 shall apply to such nonconforming signs.
   (C)   Any existing sign posted, erected or constructed after the enactment
of this chapter that is conforming to the provisions of this chapter relative
to size and location, but nonconforming to structural requirements shall be
removed or replaced within one year upon written notice from the Planning and
Zoning Department. However, if they are deemed to be a hazard or more than 50%
damaged, they shall be removed or repaired in accordance with the structural
requirements of this chapter within 30 days after receiving notice from the
Planning and Zoning Department.
   (D)   Any sign determined to be abandoned shall be removed within 30 days of
notification by the Planning and Zoning Department, the property owner and/or
owner of the business advertisement and or owner of the sign.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.19 COMPLIANCE WITH SAFETY REGULATIONS.
   It is unlawful for any person erecting any sign or other advertising
structure to fail to comply strictly with the requirements of this chapter and
any and all regulations pertaining to safety.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.20 ERECTION OF SIGNS OVER PUBLIC STREETS OR SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   Whenever any sign or other advertising structure shall be erected in
whole or in part on or over any public street or sidewalk, the person or
persons performing the work shall, before proceeding to erect the same, take
all precautions necessary to ensure the safety of persons and property on such
street or sidewalk.
   (B)   Before hoisting any material above the surface of any street or
sidewalk or placing any material upon the traveled portion of any street or
sidewalk, the persons or person performing such work shall exclude the
traveling public from the portion of such street or sidewalk in which such work
is to be done by means of suitable barriers, protected walkways and/or other
adequate warning devices.
   (C)   The Planning and Zoning Department may require the owner or sign
contractor to provide a guard or guards to exclude all persons not concerned in
the performance of the work from the portion of the street or sidewalk in which
the work is to proceed.
   (D)   When hoisting any sign or advertising structure above the surface of
any street or sidewalk, the same shall be accomplished by hoisting devices with
proper design and adequate capacity to accomplish the work in accordance with
established engineering practices, all of which hoisting equipment shall be
kept and maintained in good and workable condition.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.21 CONSTRUCTION PROJECT OR LAND DEVELOPMENT SIGN.
   One sign identifying the contractor and purpose of development of no greater
than 100 square feet and 15 feet in height shall be permitted in commercial
zones, and no greater than 32 square feet and 15 feet in height in residential
zones, may be erected on sites which a construction project is under
development. Such sign may be erected no earlier than 60 days prior to the
beginning of the construction project and removed within 30 days after such
constructions is completed.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.22 SIGN REGULATIONS IN ZONING DISTRICTS.
   (A)   Signs in Agriculture (A-1) Zones. Advertising signs for the sale of
goods or services produced on the premises shall be limited to two signs of not
more than 32 square feet per sign.
   (B)   Signs in Residential Agricultural Zones (RA-1,2,3,4). Signs allowed to
be placed in Residential Agricultural Zones are as follows:
      (1)   Identification signs for the identification of buildings not to
exceed 12 square feet when attached to the building. Any lighting of said signs
shall be diffused or screened;
      (2)   Group daycare signs as permitted in compliance with the provisions
of a home occupation; and
      (3)   Signs for offices for professional persons and home occupations,
provided signs are limited to one non-flashing sign not larger in area than one
and one-half square feet; if lighted, it shall be indirect.
   (C)   Signs in Residence Zones (R-P, R-1, R-1.5, R-2, R-3). Signs allowed to
be placed in residence zones are as follows:
      (1)   Signs not exceeding eight square feet to inform the public as to
the sale of property and name plates attached to dwellings not exceeding one
and one-half square feet which are designed and maintained to harmonize with
the residential character of the zone;
      (2)   Group daycare signs as permitted in compliance with the provisions
of a home occupation; and
      (3)   Signs for offices for professional persons and home occupations,
provided signs are limited to one non-flashing sign not larger in area that one
and one-half square feet; if lighted, it shall be indirect lighting.
   (D)   Signs in Manufactured Home (MH) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed in
Manufactured Home Zones are as follows:
      (1)   Signs not exceeding eight square feet to inform the public as to
the sale of property and name plates attached to dwellings not exceeding one
and one-half square feet which are designed and maintained to harmonize with
the residential character of the zone;
      (2)   Group daycare signs as permitted in compliance with the provisions
of a home occupation; and
      (3)   Signs for offices for professional persons and home occupations,
provided signs are limited to one non-flashing sign not larger in area that one
and one-half square feet; if lighted, it shall be indirect lighting.
   (E)   Signs in Limited Neighborhood Commercial (LNC) Zones. Signs allowed to
be placed in Limited Neighborhood Commercial Zones are as follows: two wall
mounted signs not exceeding 32 square feet each.
   (F)   Signs in Neighborhood Shopping Center (SC-1) Zones. Signs allowed to
be placed in Neighborhood Shopping Center Zones are as follows:
      (1)   Non-flashing signs advertising services, merchandise or products
offered for sale in the building on which the sign is located, provided that
such signs are attached to and do not protrude more than four feet beyond the
wall of the building to which the sign is attached; and
      (2)   Signs identifying the buildings and signs advertising products sold
on the premises.
   (G)   Signs in Limited Commercial (C-1) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed in
Limited Commercial Zones are as follows: signs identifying the buildings; and
signs advertising products sold on the premises, including billboard signs.
   (H)   Signs in Limited Business (HC-1) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed in
Limited Commercial Zones are as follows: any use permitted in the SC-1 Zone
(division (F) above); and any use permitted in the C-1 Zone (division (G)
above).
   (I)   Signs in General Commercial (C-2) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed in
General Commercial Zones are as follows: any use permitted in the C-1 Zone
(division (G) above).
   (J)   Signs in Professional Business Office (P-B) Zones. Signs allowed to be
placed in Professional Business Office Zones are as follows: non-flashing pole
signs identifying the business not to exceed 100 feet in size or exceed 15 feet
in height. Wall signs are limited to 10% of the area of the buildings front
surface area.
   (K)   Signs in Manufacturing (M-1, M-1A) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed
in Manufacturing Zones are as follows: identifying signs attached to buildings.
   (L)   Signs in Industrial and Manufacturing (I&M-1, I&M-2, I&M-3) Zones.
Signs allowed to be placed in Industrial and Manufacturing Zones are as
follows: all signs, given they meet the provisions of this chapter.
   (M)   Signs in Recreational Forestry (R-F) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed
in Recreational Forestry Zones are as follows: signs advertising services,
articles or products which are offered for sale on the premises on which the
sign is located.
   (N)   Signs in Grazing (G-1) Zones. Signs allowed to be placed in Grazing
Zones are as follows: any sign permitted in an A-1 Zone (division (A) above)
and outdoor advertising signs.
   (O)   Signs in Planned Unit Developments (PUD). Signs allowed to be placed
in PUDs as follows: all signs which are allowed in the zone which the PUD
overlays.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.23 SPORTS FIELD FENCING.
   Signs placed on sports field fencing may be allowed, provided the sign does
not exceed the height of the fence and shall be permissible only on the field
side of the fence facing the playing field.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.24 ELECTRIC SIGNS.
   All signs which utilize or are illuminated by electricity shall comply with
the most current state- adopted edition of the National Electrical Code and
county-adopted edition of the International Fire Code. An electric sign must
have the installer’s name, address, date of installation and the listing label
affixed to the sign in such a manner that it can be easily read from the ground
using only the naked eye.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.25 HEIGHT OF SIGNS.
   No sign shall exceed the height limitations established for each zone as set
forth in the table found in §
152.31.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.26 ELECTRONIC MESSAGE CENTER SIGNS AND CHANGEABLE COPY PANELS.
   Electronic message center signs and changeable copy signs shall be permitted
within an on-premises freestanding pole or wall sign in commercial and
industrial zones in which they are allowed. In such cases, the following
provisions apply.
   (A)   The sign copy area of an electronic message center sign and changeable
copy sign shall be counted in the permissible wall or freestanding pole sign
copy area. The face area of such sign shall not exceed 75% of the applicable
total allowable sign or 250 square feet, whichever is less, except that signs
which are part of a master planned development sign and within 660 feet of I-15
or U.S. Hwy 20 right-of-way shall not exceed 75% of the total allowable sign
area.
   (B)   The design and construction of electronic message centers shall be
integrated into the architecture of the on-site buildings using similar
construction materials and colors as used in the building(s).
   (C)   The displays on copy areas of electronic message center signs shall be
limited to static displays or messages that appear or disappear from the
display through dissolve, fade or instantaneous modes but which may otherwise
not have movement, appearance or optical illusion of movement.
   (D)   Each message on the sign must be displayed for a minimum of six
seconds.
   (E)   Each message transition must be accomplished within 0.5 seconds.
   (F)   (1)   Each electronic message center shall be equipped with a
photocell dimmer to automatically dim with changes in ambient light.
      (2)   When requesting a permit for an electronic message center, the
applicant shall submit documentation certifying the sign is equipped with a
photocell dimmer which shall be used at all times the sign is in operation.
   (G)    The night-time illumination of electronic message centers shall
conform to the following criteria. The difference between off and solid-message
measurements of an electronic message center shall not exceed 0.3 foot-candles
at night, applicable one-half hour after sunset. The illuminance of an
electronic message center shall be measured with an illuminance meter set to
measure foot-candles accurate to at least two decimals. Illuminance shall be
measured with the sign turned off, and again with the sign displaying a white
image, for a full color-capable sign or a solid message for a single-color
sign. All measurements shall be taken perpendicular to the face of the
electronic message center at the distance determined by the total square
footage of the sign as set forth in the following table.
Area of Sign in Sq. Measurement     Area of Sign in Sq. Measurement Distance
Ft.                 Distance in Ft. Ft.                 in Ft.

For signs with an area in sq. ft. other than those specifically listed in
the table the measurement distance may be calculated with the following
formula: measurement distance = area of sign sq. ft. x 100
 
   (H)   Prior to the issuance of a permit, the applicant shall provide written
certification from the sign manufacturer that the night-time illuminance has
been factory pre-set not to exceed the allowed foot-candles and is protected
from end-user manipulation by password-protected software or other method as
deemed appropriate by the Planning and Zoning Director. The applicant shall
submit a written statement as part of their application to the Planning and
Zoning Department stating he, she or it has read and agrees to the standards
set forth in this section.
   (I)   One electronic message center sign or changeable copy sign shall be
allowed per sign structure.
   (J)   Electronic message signs shall be permitted as school signs when
approved by the Planning and Zoning Commission as a conditional use permit and
according to the following standards.
      (1)   The hours of operation shall be limited to the hours of 7:00 a.m.
to 10:00 p.m. local time.
      (2)   The sign shall be placed on the wall of the school unless the sign
is to be located adjacent to an arterial street.
      (3)   The sign complies with the standards in §
152.26.
      (4)   The size of the sign copy area shall not exceed 24 square feet and
shall count towards the total permissible sign copy area.
      (5)   The sign shall be designed to have a maximum viewing range of 300
feet.
      (6)   The Planning and Zoning Commission may modify the above standards
where necessary to minimize adverse effects on adjoining residential uses or
where necessary to assure visibility to students and parents on and immediately
adjacent to the school entrance or parking areas.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.27 DESIGN.
   (A)   Signs and sign structures shall be designed and constructed to resist
wind and seismic forces as specified in this section. All bracing systems shall
be designed and constructed to transfer lateral forces to the foundations. For
signs on buildings, the dead and lateral loads shall be transmitted through the
structural frame of the building to the ground in such manner as not to
overstress any of the elements thereof. The overturning moment produced from
lateral forces shall in no case exceed two-thirds of the dead-load resisting
moment. Uplift due to overturning shall be adequately resisted by proper
anchorage to the ground or to the structural frame of the building. The weight
of earth superimposed over footings may be used in determining the dead-load
resisting moment. Such earth shall be carefully placed and thoroughly
compacted.
   (B)   Signs and sign structures shall be designed and constructed to resist
wind forces as specified in the county-adopted edition of the International
Building Code.
   (C)   Design and construction with plastic material shall be in accordance
with the county-adopted edition of the International Building Code.
   (D)   The display panel face of light-transmitting signs shall be made of
approved plastics as specified in the International Building Code.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.28 CONSTRUCTION.
   The supports for all sign structures shall be placed in or upon private
property and shall be built, constructed and erected in conformance with the
most current county-adopted edition of the International Building Code. An
inspection of forms and footings shall be called for before any concrete is
poured whenever this manner of construction is applicable. The sign contractor
must call for an inspection when a sign has been installed.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.29 PROJECTION AND CLEARANCE.
   All signs and sign structures covered by this chapter:
   (A)   Shall conform to the clearance and projection requirements of this
chapter;
   (B)   Shall not be located not less than six feet horizontally or 12 feet
vertically from overhead electrical conductors which are energized in excess of
750 volts. For the purpose of this section, the term OVERHEAD CONDUCTORS means
any electrical conductor, either bare or insulated, installed above the ground
except such conductors, which are enclosed in rigid conduit or other material
covering of equal strength;
   (C)   Shall not be erected in such a manner that any portion of its surface
or supports will interfere in any way with the free use of any fire escape,
exit or standpipe;
   (D)   In all commercial zones, wall mounted signs within five feet of an
opening in an exterior wall shall be constructed of non-combustible material or
must current county-adopted edition of the International Building Code approved
plastics, when mounted against a non-combustible wall; and
   (E)   Shall not project into any public alley. Projection into a private
alley or drive shall not be allowed below a height of 14 feet above grade.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.30 BILLBOARD SIGNS.
   (A)   No billboard sign may be installed within the following locations:
      (1)   Within a 750-foot radius from any other billboard sign;
      (2)   Within 750 feet of the nearest boundary of the Snake River or the
Riverwalk;
      (3)   Within 750 feet from properties or districts listed on the National
Register of Historic Places; or
      (4)   Within 500 feet from any residential zone.
   (B)   Stacking or side-by-side billboards are not allowed.
   (C)   Billboards shall only be used for off-premises signs.
   (D)   LED and Tri-vision billboards must adhere to the following
regulations.
      (1)   Billboards shall not include any illumination or image which moves
continuously, appears to be in motion or has any moving or animated parts, or
video displays, or broadcasts. No such sign shall include any illumination
which is flashing or moving.
      (2)   The message or image must remain static for a minimum of eight
seconds.
      (3)   An automated change of message or image must be accomplished within
two seconds or less and contain a default design that will freeze the sign face
in one position should a malfunction occur.
      (4)   Each electronic message center shall be equipped with a photocell
dimmer to automatically dim with changes in ambient light. When requesting a
permit for an electronic message center, the applicant shall submit
documentation certifying the sign is equipped with a photocell dimmer, and the
dimmer shall be used at all times the sign is in operation.
      (5)   The night-time illumination of LED billboards shall conform to the
criteria in this section. The difference between off and solid-message
measurements of an LED billboard shall not exceed 0.3 foot-candles at night,
applicable one-half hour after sunset. The illuminance of an LED billboard
shall be measures with an illuminance meter set to measure foot-candles
accurate to at least two decimals. Illuminance shall be measured with the sign
off, and again with the sign displaying a white image for a full color-capable
sign or a solid message for a single-color sign. All measurements shall be
taken perpendicular to the face of the LED billboard at the distance determined
by the total square footage of the sign as set forth in the following  table.
Area of Sign in Sq. Ft. Measurement Distance in Ft.

For signs with an area in sq. ft. other than those specifically listed in the
table the measurement distance may be calculated with the following formula:
measurement distance= area of sign sq. ft. x 100
 
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)

violation of the controls specified in this table.

§ 152.32 EXEMPTIONS.
   Signs exempt from county permit requirements are:
   (A)   Directional or information signs that are no larger than three square
feet per face, which help direct on-premises land uses or activities, and do
not display an advertising message (for example: public convenience signs
identifying restrooms, public telephones, walkways and similar features or
facilities, parking directions and the like);
   (B)   Memorial signs or tablets displaying names of buildings and dates of
erection when cut into the surface or facade of the building;
   (C)   Signs required by law or that serve the public interest, such as
traffic signs, railroad crossing signs, legal notices, warning of temporary
emergencies, construction zones, presence of underground cable, utility signs
and similar signs installed on public property and are owned and maintained by
county, state or federal agencies or a public utility company;
   (D)   Temporary signs or banners that bear no product advertising and
announce community or civic events. Such signs or banners shall be removed
within seven calendar days following the event;
   (E)   Signs that identify the architects, engineers, contractors, lenders,
government agencies or programs (i.e., FHA, HUD, FMHA, low cost housing, Senior
Citizen Center and the like) and other individuals or firms involved with the
construction. This shall not include any advertisement of any product, or signs
announcing the character of the building enterprise or the purpose for which
the building is intended, or use of the project. The signs shall be confined to
the site of the construction and shall be removed within 14 calendar day after
the beginning of the intended use of the project;
   (F)   Homeowner locator signs are allowed if each sign is no more than one
square foot per face and the mounting device is located out of public road
right-of-way. Such signs shall be kept in good repair;
   (G)   Signs advertising the sale, rental or lease of real property shall be
confined to the property to which they apply. Such signs may be up to a total
area of 32 square feet per face in size. The number of signs shall be limited
to one sign per parcel, up to and including 40 acres, or not more than one sign
per frontage. Directional signs may be placed at intersections to direct
interested parties to real estate that is for sale, for rent or lease. Only one
directional sign per agency will be allowed. The directional sign shall not be
more than 16 square feet. Such sign may indicate the name of the real estate
agency or their logo. Signs must be kept in good repair. All such signs shall
be removed within 14 calendar days after the sale, rental or lease. All signs
are to be placed on private property. Said signs are allowed only when a change
in direction is required to lead customers to the property. Subdivision
directional signs may only be erected after county approval of the final plat
for the subdivision and must be removed after the last lot within the approved
final plat is sold by the developer;
   (H)   Signs announcing candidates seeking public office and other data
pertinent thereto, up to an area of 32 square feet for each premises. Such
signs shall be confined to private property and removed within 14 calendar days
after the election for which they were intended;
   (I)   Window signs in a window display of merchandise when incorporated in
that display;
   (J)   Holiday signs, religious signs, no soliciting signs, welcome home
signs and no trespassing signs; and
   (K)   Temporary signs which are intended to be festive, holiday or invite
gathering are permitted provided such are not located within the road right-of-
way and comply in all other respects for permitted signs.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
§ 152.99 PENALTY.
   Any person who is found guilty of violation of this chapter is guilty of an
infraction. A violation of this chapter shall constitute civil violation,
punishable in the amount of $100. A second violation of this chapter shall
constitute a civil violation, punishable in the amount of $200. A third
violation of this chapter shall constitute a civil violation, in the amount of
$300. A fourth violation of this chapter shall constitute a civil violation,
punishable in the amount of $400. A fifth violation of this chapter and each
subsequent violation shall constitute a civil violation, punishable in the
amount of $500. Each calendar day in which the property shall be in violation
of this chapter and shall constitute a separate and distinct offense. In the
event said fees are unpaid, a lien may be recorded against the subject
property. In addition to all other remedies, the county shall further have a
right to injunctive relief.
(Ord. 254-23, passed 6-21-2023)
